So it landed as ONE new `PassiveEffects` field `damageAttacker?: {amount, requiresTool?}` — **no new op, TriggerTiming, action, or phase.** continuous.ts sums it seat-free, evaluating Custom Trap's `requiresTool` against the holder's board (so the read site stays a plain number); attack.ts folds it right after `DAMAGE_DEALT`, placing flat counters (outside the §8.5 W/R pipeline — the confusion-self-hit / `damageSelf` model, `COUNTERS_PLACED source:"ability"`) on the attacker's Active when `dealt > 0`. **"Even if this Pokémon is Knocked Out" is FREE:** the placement precedes finishAttack's both-board §8.1 sweep (present in BOTH the program branch's `attackEpilogue` and the direct call), which then KOs a lethally-retaliated attacker and prizes it to the defender — the confusion-self-KO path proves the sweep already handles a mid-turn attacker KO. **The "in the Active Spot" clause needs no `activeOnly` flag** — it is enforced by the READ SITE: only the main-hit Active defender retaliates, so a benched holder hit by spread naturally does not (which is exactly what the clause says). Cacnea/Cacturne share one const (byte-identical). **Deliberate deviation, with a trail:** the general `onDamagedByAttack` trigger framework (a new TriggerTiming + scan point + a reactive program that PARKS an opponent choice) is genuinely larger and is still needed by the OTHER two cards on that row — **Armarouge** sv03-044 (Burn the attacker, an `applyStatus`) and **Klawf ex** sv03-120 (discard an Energy from the attacker, an opponent-side parking discard) — so it stays the documented follow-up, now the whole of the (annotated) `onDamagedByAttack` row. This is "the smallest coherent unit that leaves the repo better and green": the `damageAttacker` backlog row CLOSED 3/3 with a passive-field slice smaller than Defiance/Choice Belt (no `BoardCondition`, no seat plumbing, one read site). **Snipe/spread hits on the Active do not retaliate yet** — the recoil folds only at the §8.5 main hit; a documented follow-up (the interpreter's snipeActive/spreadDamage/damageChosen sites would each need the same fold, and each knows the attacker as `ctx.seat`'s Active). **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/damageAttacker.test.ts` (8) — the registry-authored amounts + Tool gate; Cacnea's 30 recoil with the exact event order (`DAMAGE_DEALT` → `COUNTERS_PLACED` → turn end); Cacturne's shared 30; "even if KO'd" (recoil lands as the attack KOs the defender, `COUNTERS_PLACED` before `KNOCKED_OUT`); a lethal recoil KOing the ATTACKER, prized to P2 (the read-before-sweep proof); the `dealt > 0` gate (Yawn, a 0-damage status attack → no recoil); and Custom Trap's Tool gate BOTH ways (bare Stunfisk no recoil / Vitality Band → 50). New fixtures: Cacnea/Cacturne/Stunfisk battlers + `DAMAGE_ATTACKER_DECK`. **Every test passed after one deck rebalance** (2→4 Cacturne copies — a 60-card deck can prize/draw out a 2-of before surgery fields it). No web/API/DB/schema change (the counters surface via the existing `COUNTERS_PLACED` event). engineVersion 0.48.0 → **0.49.0**; 140 → **141** files, 1985 → **1993** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — the two research sub-agents scoped it, and the passive rides the just-touched continuous/attack pipeline. `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + D97 + this slice all await it). *Why: the backlog's op-family LABEL named a trigger, but the code-map showed the counter-placing cards are a passive the engine already has the shape for — choosing the smaller idiomatic model (and leaving the genuinely-trigger cards to a follow-up) is the D94/D95 lesson one level up: the catalog + the code, not the backlog label, decide the real shape of the work.* (2026-07-27) |
| D99 | `onDamagedByAttack` — the general reactive TRIGGER D98 deliberately deferred, landing Armarouge "Scorching Armor" (Burn the attacker) (P3-M5, engine 0.50.0) | The natural continuation of D98, and this time the effect genuinely IS a trigger (not a passive): **Armarouge sv03-044 "Scorching Armor"** — VERBATIM re-confirmed against the live D1 catalog before authoring (the D95/D96/D97/D98 lesson — the query also enumerated the WHOLE `onDamagedByAttack` family to fix the slice boundary: Cacnea/Cacturne/Stunfisk already covered by D98's passive, Klawf ex deferred, and Fezandipiti sv06.5-073 is out of the sv01–sv03 backlog scope AND a damage-PREVENTION read, not a post-damage reaction): "If this Pokémon is in the Active Spot and is damaged by an attack from your opponent's Pokémon (even if this Pokémon is Knocked Out), the Attacking Pokémon is now Burned." **Modelled as a `TriggeredAbility` on a new `TriggerTiming` `onDamagedByAttack` — NOT a D98-style passive — because the effect runs an EffectOp program (`applyStatus`) reactively, which is exactly what `TriggeredAbility.program` is for; a passive carries no program.** The scan point is the SAME attack.ts spot D98's recoil uses: right after `DAMAGE_DEALT`, gated on `dealt > 0`, main-hit Active only (so the "in the Active Spot" clause is enforced by the read site, exactly as D98 — a benched holder hit by spread does not react), BEFORE finishAttack's §8.1 sweep (so "even if Knocked Out" is FREE — a lethally-damaged Armarouge still Burns the attacker; the KO case's apply PAUSES at `ko:takePrizes` before the Checkup, so the attacker's `burned` flag is observable pre-cure). The trigger program runs under the DAMAGED Pokémon's seat, so `applyStatus target:"defender"` lands on the ATTACKER's Active (`otherSeat`) — mirrors Trevenant/Mawile (an Ability that runs on its holder and targets the opponent). `activeOnly: true` carries the clause redundantly with the read site (belt-and-braces). **Framework, not just a card:** a new `runDamagedByAttackTriggers` in triggers.ts (mirrors `runCheckupTriggers` — NON-PARKING by construction, so it threads state straight through via `.state`, no settleProgram); `triggersOf` stayed module-private (the new helper is the only caller from attack.ts, keeping the scan machinery in triggers.ts and attack.ts a one-line call). **Klawf ex sv03-120** ("discard an Energy from the Attacking Pokémon" — an opponent-side PARKING choice, the §9.4 shape) is the one remaining `onDamagedByAttack` card, still deferred: it needs the parking-through-settleProgram path a Burn does not. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/scorchingArmor.test.ts` (4) — the registry-authored trigger (id → the exact `TriggeredAbility`); Bite deals 30 → the attacker is Burned with the exact order `DAMAGE_DEALT` → `ABILITY_TRIGGERED`(Scorching Armor, seat p2) → `STATUS_APPLIED`(burned, seat p1); "even if KO'd" (lethal Bite → Burn lands BEFORE `KNOCKED_OUT`, `burned` flag set pre-Checkup, Armarouge prized to P1); the `dealt > 0` gate (Yawn, a 0-damage status → no trigger, attacker un-Burned). Burn's final-state flag is Checkup-coin-dependent in the non-KO case, so those assert via EVENTS (`STATUS_APPLIED`/`ABILITY_TRIGGERED`); the KO case (paused pre-Checkup) asserts the stable flag. New fixtures: an Armarouge sv03-044 battler + a DEDICATED `SCORCHING_ARMOR_DECK` (the DAMAGE_ATTACKER_DECK is seed-pinned — the PSY_PURGE/HAIL_BLADE "dedicated deck" convention). **Every test passed on first run — no behavioural fix.** No web/API/DB/schema change (the Burn surfaces via the existing `STATUS_APPLIED` event; the attacker takes its Burn at the very next Checkup, which the attack cascade runs since attacking ends the turn — correct TCG behaviour). engineVersion 0.49.0 → **0.50.0**; 141 → **142** files, 1993 → **1997** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer** — one Explore sub-agent mapped the trigger/attack.ts seams and confirmed the template, and the new trigger rides the just-touched attack.ts spot. `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + D97 + D98 + this slice all await it). *Why: D98 split the `onDamagedByAttack` label into "passive-shaped" (counters) and "genuinely trigger-shaped" (status/parking-discard); this closes the FIRST trigger-shaped card by building exactly the framework D98 named as the follow-up — reusing D98's scan spot means the Burn variant is a small honest addition (a timing, a helper, a card, a fixture), and the parking Klawf ex stays deferred as the one card that needs more.* (2026-07-27) |
| D100 | `disableAbilities` — the continuous Ability-lock AURA, the first passive that reaches OTHER Pokémon, gating all four Ability read sites (P3-M5, engine 0.51.0) | The resume point's recommended "widest coverage" row, and the backlog's biggest blast radius. **Verbatim re-confirmed against the live D1 before authoring** (the D95→D99 lesson): one query (`abilities_json LIKE '%have no Abilities%'`) returned EXACTLY the six ids the registry now carries and nothing else — **Klefki sv01-096 "Mischievous Lock"** ("As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot, Basic Pokémon in play (both yours and your opponent's) have no Abilities, except for Mischievous Lock."), **Spiritomb sv02-089 "Fettered in Misfortune"** ("Basic Pokémon V in play (both yours and your opponent's) have no Abilities." — no Active-Spot clause, so it locks from the BENCH), **Ting-Lu ex sv02-127/-243/-263/-275 "Cursed Land"** ("As long as this Pokémon is in the Active Spot, your opponent's Pokémon in play that have any damage counters on them have no Abilities, except for Pokémon ex."). A second query confirmed no other phrasing of the lock exists in the pool, and that sv01-129 — the OTHER Spiritomb — has no Ability at all (a pinned negative test). **Modelled as ONE new `PassiveEffects` field `disableAbilities?: {stage?, suffix?, side, requiresDamage?, requiresActive?, exemptSuffix?, exemptAbilityNamed?}`** — seven independent narrowing predicates that between them express all three printed sentences, no new op / timing / action / phase. **But it is the FIRST passive field that does not modify its HOLDER** — it reaches other Pokémon — so continuous.ts reads it through a dedicated both-boards pass, `disabledAbilityUids(state)`, returning the set of top uids whose Abilities are off; only a TOP CARD grants an aura (it is a printed Pokémon Ability, never a Tool). **Four read sites consult that Set by uid:** activated (`cardplay.ts useAbility` → the new `ABILITY_DISABLED` error, checked BEFORE the activeOnly/oncePerTurn/cost gates so a locked Pokémon reports the LOCK rather than an incidental refusal), passive (`continuous.ts passivesOf` — drops the top card's printed passive only; the holder's attached **Tools keep working**, they are §7.4 card effects and not Abilities), triggered (`triggers.ts triggersOf` — the single choke covering onPlayToBench / onEvolve / betweenTurns / onDamagedByAttack; **onKnockOut is deliberately NOT gated**, it fires as the Pokémon leaves play) and the HUD (`redact.ts redactedAbilitiesOf` — the row greys with `reason: null`, self-evident like the Active-only greying, so the client never offers a button the engine will refuse). **Auras are collected UNGATED — a lock never turns itself, or another lock, off.** Self-exemption is what the printed text asks for (Klefki's `exemptAbilityNamed`, matched on the TARGET card's printed Ability name, so a mirror match leaves both locks on); lock-vs-lock is the **KNOWN LIMITATION**: an Active Ting-Lu ex opposite a damaged Klefki should by the rules switch Mischievous Lock off, and does not — that needs a fixpoint pass rather than one read, and is a documented follow-up **pinned by a tripwire test** that asserts the current behaviour and says to flip when the fixpoint lands. The three cards in scope never mute a NON-lock ability of another lock's source, so the limitation only bites lock-vs-lock. **Tests:** `packages/engine/src/abilityLock.test.ts` (19) — the registry row (all six ids + the sv01-129 negative); the aura scan as a set-membership matrix (empty with no lock in play — the no-op guarantee; Klefki locking every Basic on BOTH boards while skipping a Stage 1 and itself; Klefki dark from the Bench; the mirror match; Spiritomb locking Basic V on both boards FROM THE BENCH and MISSING a VMAX — a suffix match, not a prefix one; Ting-Lu reaching only the opponent's damaged non-ex, with its own damaged bench, an undamaged opponent and a damaged opposing **ex** all clear; Ting-Lu dark from the Bench; the lock-vs-lock tripwire); then a **lock-ON / lock-OFF pair per read site** — `ABILITY_DISABLED` vs the `ABILITY_ACTIVE_ONLY` the same call falls back to (the check-ORDER proof), Cursed Land flipping a Stage 2's activated Ability the instant it takes one counter (Tinkaton is out of both Basic-only locks' reach, so that pair is Cursed Land's alone), Bouffalant's Bouffer suppressed (the same printed 30 lands as 30 vs 10), a locked attacker's Vitality Band still adding its 10 (the Tool exemption), Armarouge's `onDamagedByAttack` Burn suppressed vs firing, and the HUD row greyed vs enabled. **The implementation needed NO behavioural fix** — 18 of 19 passed on the first run and the 19th was a seed whose deck had drawn its Klefki. Fixture work this session: HP/retreat/stage corrected against the live prints (Spiritomb 60, Ting-Lu ex 240/4), and `ABILITY_LOCK_DECK` rebuilt to carry one target per read site AND per predicate field — its `board()` helper **asserts both seats open on the neutral fix-bigbody**, because this deck holds three aura sources and two Pokémon V and any other opening Active would smuggle a live aura onto the board and silently poison every set assertion. Klefki's fixture carries fix-attacker's plain Bite ({C}, 30) instead of its printed "Joust" ({C}, 10 + an unsimulated Tool discard) — a DELIBERATE, commented deviation: Klefki must be the Active to emit its aura, so it must be the attacker, and the lock-ON/lock-OFF arms have to compare the same printed 30. No web/API/DB/schema change. engineVersion 0.50.0 → **0.51.0**; 142 → **143** files, 1997 → **2016** tests. Full `bun run check` GREEN (tsc + lint + vitest). **Self-reviewed, no independent reviewer**; `/code-review` still NOT run (user-triggered; D96 + D97 + D98 + D99 + this slice all await it). *Why: the widest row was worth taking before the narrow leftovers because the cost is in the READ SITES, not the cards — once every ability read consults one Set, later locks are registry rows. Landing it as a predicate on the existing passive field (rather than new machinery) kept the diff to one scan function plus four one-line gates, and the honest cost is named rather than hidden: the aura-vs-aura fixpoint is deferred WITH a failing-when-fixed tripwire instead of being quietly wrong.* (2026-07-28) |

D309–D368 live are in `archive/decisions-numbered.md`, verbatim. Look any number up in
`decision-index.md`. Rows about the React web app (`apps/web`, `src/features`, `/play`,

## Locked assumptions (override explicitly if they change)
- Bun-workspace monorepo; API + Lobby DO on Cloudflare **Workers** via `wrangler`.
- **Hono** + **Zod**; **Drizzle** over D1. Ingest validates every payload with
  **our own Zod schemas** and defaults to **plain REST fetch** — the
  2026-07-09 finding that the official `@tcgdex/sdk` is *unnecessary for
  validation* still stands, and is why its types are never the boundary.
  **Amended 2026-08-04 (D188):** the SDK is now an installed, opt-in
  **transport** (`--transport=sdk`), pinned `2.9.0` in `apps/api` only; the
  fetch+Zod posture is unchanged and remains the default.
- Server **sessions** (http-only cookie), not stateless JWT.
- Ingest filters to SV-era but writes the full-history schema.
  **Amended 2026-11-11 (DOC-P0):** the catalog spans the `sv` and `me` series plus the
  `swsh10.5` reprint set (the Mega serie since D581); the full-catalog ingest is in
  `workstreams/backend-data.md` §3.4.1.
- attacks/weaknesses/resistances stored as JSON columns for now (promote to
  child tables only if we need to query inside them).
- English (`en`) first; schema leaves room for i18n later.
- The **match engine (P3)** is the actual gameplay; approximate at first,
  improved iteratively.

## These are defaults, not law
Every decision and assumption here is a **default with a rationale**, chosen with
the context available at the time — revisit any of them when the code or new
context reveals a better path. The point of writing them down is to preserve the
*why*, so a later session can judge whether it still holds.

## Adding / changing a decision
Add a dated entry at the end, `### YYYY-MM-DD — <title>`, with the choice **and
the why**. The numbered table is closed; don't add rows. When an entry changes an
earlier record, say so in the new entry and add one line under the old one:
`*Superseded in part by "<title>" (<date>): <what changed>.*`, or an
`Amended <date> (<id>): …` bullet when only a detail moves. Then run
`bun run docs:index`. The one hard rule: **don't reverse a decision silently** —
leave a trail so the reasoning is never lost.

## Dated entries

### 2026-10-09 — Requested rotated-card support

The user explicitly requested Arven and Miraidon ex. Support their existing-deck printings even though they rotated out of Standard, refining D187’s authoring scope for these cards. Arven uses `searchDeck.also` for separate Item/Tool caps and reveal; Tandem Unit uses the existing Basic Lightning filter, Bench destination, and per-body once-per-turn allowance. Five Arven ids and six Tandem Unit ids share one program per text; other Miraidon ex variants keep their own effects. Photon Blaster already derives its damage and next-turn lock. No new ops, state fields, or match-record version are needed.

### 2026-10-22 — Online play in the Lua frontend

Online moves into the Lua frontend without a server or wire change. The lobby socket logic becomes a framework-free `LobbyClient` shared by React `useLobby` and the Lua adapters, so both clients keep one behaviour. The lobby is a Lua page drawn as a modal (`/lua/?page=online`, short invite link `/lua/?lobby=CODE`), and the live match is page `match`, which reuses the Sandbox `LuaSimulator` through a `TableSource` seam (`LocalTableSource` / `LuaOnlineMatch`). Page hand-offs replace history, so browser Back goes Home and leaves the lobby as React does. User decisions: Copy link uses the Lua URL; the deck picker lists playable decks with a client-only regulation filter (All, Standard legal, Not Standard legal, Expanded legal, Not Expanded legal); spectating is cut from the Lua client (a full lobby shows "Lobby is full"); the in-match Leave needs a second press, while Back and tab close keep React's forfeit behaviour. Coin flips, Energy/Retreat allowances and effect titles stay local-only until `RedactedGame` carries them.

*Superseded in part by "Engine↔client contract: clients read what the engine says" and "Frontend structure: named wire, a typed page shell, one simulator flow" (both 2026-10-26): coin flips, effect titles and allowances are on the wire.*

### 2026-10-22 — The Lua frontend replaces React

The Lua (love.js) app becomes the only web frontend and moves from `/lua/` to `/`; pages stay query-string selected (`/?page=…`, `/?lobby=CODE`, `/?account`, and the new `/?settings`). The root `index.html` is the host page, `bun run build:lua` writes the runtime and game package to `public/love/` (served at `/love/`), and the account bridge is the page's one Vite-bundled module (`src/lua/bridge.ts`). The React UI, its routes and tests, Tailwind, PixiJS, GSAP and the React/testing-library dependencies are deleted. The TypeScript the Lua adapters still use is flattened out of `src/features/` into `src/{lua,game,decks,online,lib}`; mutation rows whose only killer was a deleted DOM test are dropped. Production is a static-assets-only Worker, `colorless` (`https://colorless.jofrescari.workers.dev`, root `wrangler.jsonc`; it supersedes the old React build Worker), with the API renamed to `colorless-api` and its D1 database, KV cache and R2 mirror to `colorless`, `colorless-cache` and `colorless-assets` (the D1 data was copied from the pre-rename database; the caches start empty), built against the deployed API through `.env.production`; the API's `APP_ORIGIN` var is that origin and its `dev` script passes the localhost one. The build-time CSP is kept, now without inline styles. Old React paths (`/decks`, `/lobby/CODE`, …) fall back to the host page (SPA not-found handling), which opens home. Known limit: `workers.dev` is a public suffix, so the frontend and API Workers are different sites and the `SameSite=None` session cookie is third-party; sign-in fails where third-party cookies are blocked until both move to a shared custom domain (or the frontend Worker proxies the API).

**Why** (added 2026-11-11, DOC-P5). In the user's words: "because it's easier to replicate Balatro's visual and feel". The costs accepted with it, as later entries record them:

- The Lua build and its tests need Balatro's source tree, which is not in this repository and cannot be; CI skips those tests ("One gate for CI and `check`", 2026-10-24).
- The page needs WebGL and WebAssembly, and says so when either is missing (`frontend/lua/web/host.js`).
- A screen reader reaches the canvas only through the host page's links, dialogs, text proxies and live region (`src/host/`; "Frontend structure", 2026-10-26, and the Frontend & UI audit entries from 2026-10-29).
- The build ships 37 files from Balatro (shaders, textures, the font, sound effects and the menu music), credited to their owners and outside this repository's licence ("Dependency audit Phase 4", 2026-11-09).

*Superseded in part by "Path routes replace query-string pages" (2026-10-23): pages moved to the path. The known limit is corrected by "A same-site Lax session cookie and a cross-site guard" (2026-10-27): the two Workers are the same site, and the cookie was never third-party.*

### 2026-10-23 — Path routes replace query-string pages

Pages move from the query string to the path: `/decks`, `/decks/<id>` (builder), `/sandbox`, `/sandbox/play` (simulator), `/online`, `/online/<code>` (also the invite link) and `/online/<code>/play` (match). `?account` and `?settings` stay query flags, since they open a popup over whichever page is showing. `frontend/lua/web/host.js` owns the mapping (`routeFromLocation` / `routePath`); old `/?page=…`, `/?lobby=CODE` and React's `/lobby/<code>` still resolve (as do `/simulator` and `/match/<code>`) and are rewritten with `replaceState` on boot. The SPA not-found handling already serves the host page for every path, and Google sign-in returns to the current path with `?account`.

*Superseded in part by "Frontend structure: named wire, a typed page shell, one simulator flow" (2026-10-26): the router moved from host.js to `src/host/router.ts`.*

### 2026-10-23 — Code-quality pass: minimal comments, no source-text tests

A repo-wide audit found that about 80% of the engine source was decision-log comments (D-ids, emoji markers, census counts, struck-through history), and that tests and the mutant corpus asserted on that text, so no comment could be removed or file reformatted without breaking hundreds of checks. Changed: the 26k-line `engineVersion` changelog in `packages/engine/src/index.ts` is gone, along with every test that read raw source or docs (the D506 changelog guard, the D318 progress-log guard, the D504 summary-quote guard and source-counting tests) and the 220 per-file `engineVersion` pins. Source comments were rewritten to a minimal style (conventions.md "Code & quality"); comments in tests were deleted when they carried decision narration. The repo is biome-formatted, and `check`/CI now run the format check, the headless Lua tests and the mutant precheck. `scripts/mutation/reanchor.ts` re-points mutant rows after refactors (ignoring whitespace, comments and file moves) and retires rows; rows whose only killer was a removed source-text test were retired. Version history now lives in git and this file only.

Behaviour-preserving refactors followed in the same pass, with no changes to persisted or wire shapes. In the engine, shared helpers replace hand-written copies (see conventions.md "Code & quality"). `interpreter.ts` (13.9k lines) was split into the op modules under `ops/` and four prompt/target modules, the op contract moved to `effectTypes.ts`, and the program types and repeated registry shapes moved to `programTypes.ts` and `registryBuilders.ts`. `attack()`, `knockOut`, `advance`, `validateChoice`, `passivesOf` and `formatEvent` were split into named parts, and the test fixtures were split into `fixtures/` behind the `testFixtures.ts` barrel. In the API: one request-validation helper, global `notFound`/`onError` handlers, a table-driven match-record migration and per-frame LobbyDO handlers. In the Lua frontend: a shared wire codec, mailbox and focus helpers, and handler-map command dispatch. In scripts: shared `scripts/lib`, and `tsconfig.base.json`. The residue-census and opaque-anatomy instruments were deleted after measuring zero residue. Mutant rows were re-anchored to the moved code, and every hand-re-pointed row was re-run (667 killed, 11 declared survivors); 23 rows were retired, either because their target was deleted or because the defect could no longer be injected on its own.

Follow-ups to the same pass, also behaviour-preserving. Engine functions take their `events: GameEvent[]` out-parameter last. The `fix-*` test programs moved out of the production registry to `fixtures/registry.ts` and are installed only by the vitest setup file through `registerTestPrograms`. Tests read prompts through the kind-narrowing `effectPromptOf`. The gate ops' (`coinFlipGate`, `conditionGate`, `recordGate`, `optional`) `then` branch is now `ops`, so biome's `noThenProperty` override is gone; this changed the stored game state, so `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` is 50, and the v49 step renames the key in place on stored records. The lobby Durable Object parses its match record and smaller storage keys with zod envelope schemas (the embedded `GameState` is checked only structurally), and an unparseable value reads as stale or missing. The Lua frontend and `host.js` take their deck-size and name-length limits from files generated by `scripts/lua-constants.ts`, which `check` and CI verify with `--check`. `LuaSimulator.emit` takes its one-shot events once and builds rows with the pure builders in `src/lua/simulatorRows.ts`.

After these follow-ups, every mutant row whose patch changed beyond whitespace during the pass (2,194 rows) was run: all are killed or declared survivors. The run found that `reanchor.ts` had attached five rows to an identical twin of their code (another card's program, or a sibling builder with the same comment) after the original was rewritten through a shared helper. Those rows now point at their own code. D241-bench-clamp-dropped and D567-clamp-reads-own-bench are declared equivalent: card revision B01 caps the offer at bench space, and both rows already survived before the pass.

### 2026-10-24 — One gate for CI and `check`; symbol-guarded re-anchoring

An architecture audit found CI red on every push: the Lua tests open Balatro's source (`../balatro-source`), which is not in this repository and cannot be, so 15 of 36 Lua test files failed on the runner and later steps never ran. Those tests now go through `frontend/lua/tests/support/balatro_source.lua`, which skips a file (exit 77) when the source is missing; `bun run check` passes `--require-source`, so locally a missing source is still a failure. CI and `check` run the same named scripts (`check:static`, `check:lua`, `check:test`) instead of two hand-kept lists, and CI bundles with `LOVE_VERSIONS_OPTIONAL=1` because the Lua package cannot be built there. `tsc -b` is incremental (a no-op re-run went from about 50 s to 0.1 s). Vitest moved to `vitest.config.ts` with two projects; the engine project runs without per-file isolation, which took the engine suites from about 130 s to about 25 s, so engine tests must not use mocks or leak module state. `bun run release` orders check, remote migrations, the API deploy and the web deploy.

Mutant rows record the `symbol` their `find` sits in. `reanchor.ts` only moves a row to a match inside the same symbol, which closes the hazard of a row re-attaching to an identical twin after its original was rewritten (five rows in the code-quality pass); byte-exact `find` matching at run time is unchanged.

### 2026-10-25 — Safety nets before the architecture refactors

Phase 1 of the architecture audit turns prose contracts into failing tests; behaviour changes are limited to the two gate fixes below.

- **Sync trigger programs.** Triggers on sync-routed timings (`SYNC_TRIGGER_TIMINGS` and opponent-watched rows, `runsSynchronously` in `triggers.ts`) run through `runSyncProgram`, which throws in every build if the program parks. The throw lands inside the action, before the lobby DO saves anything, which is safer than a silently dropped prompt. applyChoice's never-parks list is the exported `UNANSWERED_OPS` table, which also records whether an op can still lead to a park (`coinFlipGate` through Backtrack Badge, and ops that schedule parking ops), and `syncTriggerPark.test.ts` audits every sync-routed registry program against it.
- **Engine-written fields.** `ENGINE_WRITTEN_FIELDS` and `ENGINE_ONLY_OPS` in `effectTypes.ts` list the op fields and pseudo-ops only the engine may write; `engineWrittenFields.test.ts` walks every authored program and every census-derived program and fails if one appears.
- **Persisted shape.** `apps/api/src/lobby/recordShape.test.ts` pins a key and type skeleton of the stored game state to `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` (`recordShape.snap`), so a shape change without a version bump fails. The three registry-length pins became per-suite id assertions.
- **Move legality.** A seeded random-walk fixture (`fixtures/walk.ts`) plays fixture decks forward. `redactFlags.test.ts` requires every redacted affordance flag to agree with `applyAction` in both directions; it found no drift across all 242 fixture decks, so splitting the reducers' check functions stays deferred. `src/lua/onlineGate.test.ts` requires the online client gate never to refuse an accepted action and annotates the known false positives. It found that benching and attaching were offered during the attack-again window, and that `reorderBench` was refused during setup; both are fixed. The window travels as an optional `attackAgain: true` on the redacted `turn:action` phase, which older clients ignore, so either Worker can deploy first.
- **API.** `beginMatch` and `matchFrameFor` in `lobby/match.ts` own match start and per-viewer frames, and a Biome `noRestrictedImports` override keeps every other lobby module off `@colorless/engine`. `LobbyDO` takes optional `LobbyDeps` (clock, seed, session, display name, deck and pool reads), defaulting to `lobbyDepsFromEnv`; `lobby/lobbyHarness.ts` runs it on Map storage, one alarm and fake hibernation sockets, and `lobbyDO.test.ts` covers the lobby lifecycle from create to the empty-lobby wipe. Route tests for auth and decks run on a real D1 through Miniflare (`apps/api/src/testing/d1.ts`, migrations applied in order).
- **Open security finding.** An unverified email never links to an existing account, but when a verified Google sign-in links to an unverified password account with the same address, that account keeps its password and sessions, so whoever registered the address first keeps access. It is an expected-fail test in `auth/routes.d1.test.ts` until the fix is chosen: clear the password and sessions, or refuse the link.
- **Lua wire.** `src/lua/wireFixtures.test.ts` builds simulator, prompt, library and builder snapshots through the real TS adapters into `frontend/lua/tests/fixtures/` (regenerate with `bunx vitest run src/lua/wireFixtures.test.ts -u`), and `frontend/lua/tests/wire_fixtures.lua` parses the same files and asserts fields by meaning. The stadium `C` row now sends all 19 fields, and `Simulator:sync` dropped the two fallbacks that tolerated the short row.
- **Mutants in CI.** A `mutants` job runs `scripts/mutation/sample.ts`: rows on the files the push or PR changed (at most 60) plus 30 drawn with the commit sha as seed. Its first run found `D481-survivor-reason-is-miskeyed` matching only its own `find` literal: the survivor reason it targeted had been rewrapped by the formatter, leaving the row's own string as the one unique match. The row now targets a real `reason:` key, and precheck rejects a corpus row whose only match is its own literal.

### 2026-10-25 — A verified provider link revokes an unverified account's credentials

Closes the security finding in the previous entry, with the user's choice of clearing over refusing the link. When a verified provider email links to an existing account that was not yet verified, the link also clears that account's password and deletes its sessions in the same batch, so whoever registered the address first loses access; the owner signs in through the provider from then on. An account whose email was already verified keeps its password. Accounts linked before this change are not revisited, since a squatter's leftover password cannot be told apart from the owner's own.

### 2026-10-25 — Engine untangling: one implementation per rule

Phase 2 of the architecture audit. Behaviour is unchanged except where noted (the 72 reprints and the builder warning); `GAME_STATE_VERSION` stays 50, so the API and web deploys stay independent.

- **The runtime cycle.** Pure helpers that closed the engine's 9-module import cycle moved down: the once-per-turn keys with `abilityUsedThisTurn`/`spendAbilityUse` to `types.ts`; the post-step checks to `stateChecks.ts` behind one `postStepChecks(state, after, events)`, whose `after` argument keeps `advance`'s and `applyAction`'s different check orders; `finishGame` to `outcome.ts`; the trigger lookups to `triggerLookup.ts`; the printed-text vocabulary to `deriver/vocabulary.ts`. What remains is the genuine recursion {flow, interpreter, ops/board, ops/energy, triggers}. `scripts/import-graph.ts --check` (in `check:static`) fails on any other runtime cycle and on relative imports across workspace roots; tests reach other roots through `@colorless/engine/testing` and `@colorless/schema/tcgdex-fixtures/*`.
- **continuous.ts is a read model.** It no longer pushes events or consumes rng: `spendCoinRedo` moved to `ops/damage.ts`, and the KO-survival clamp and damage coins to `damageCalc.ts`.
- **Damage tail.** The steps after Weakness and Resistance (reduction, full prevention, the coin-flip shield, the KO-survival clamp and coin, the DAMAGE_DEALT row, consumed Tools) are written once as `landAttackHit`; `dealAttackDamage`, `spreadDamage`, `placeSnipe` and `snipeActive` keep their own bonus, debuff and W/R steps. The four Berry guards were all "pre-W/R amount ≤ 0". 16.9M recorded `applyAction` calls were identical in state, events and `rngState` before and after; only the position of `vulnerability` among a bench DAMAGE_DEALT row's keys moved.
- **Op traits.** `OP_TRAITS` (`ops/traits.ts`) declares for every op whether it parks (`true`, `false`, or `"indirectly"` when something else answers the park), its record slot, whether it moves counters, and whether `programPlayable` asks its whiff check (`ONLY_WHIFFS` in `cardplay.ts`). `isCounterMoveOp`, `recordSlotOf`, the sync-trigger audit and the answer dispatch derive from it; a new op fails to compile until it declares its traits.
- **One answer dispatch.** `answerOp` replaces `applyChoice` plus the second `continuationOps` lookup that had to agree with it, returning stepOp's `{done, schedule}` shape and exhaustive over the parking ops. The forced endings of `healChosen` with riders and the `moveCounters*` ops go through it too. A 1500-game seeded walk (4.63M results) and the engine suite were byte-identical before and after.
- **Persisted shape.** The engine owns `GAME_STATE_VERSION`, the migration steps, `migrateGameState` and `poolIdsNeededFor` (`persist.ts`); `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION = GAME_STATE_VERSION`. `withDefaultKeys` fills only absent keys from `clearedAttackEffects()` and `clearedSideInstalls()`, so a version that only adds install fields needs no hand-written step. Local simulator saves still discard on any `engineVersion` change; switching them to migrations is the user's open decision.
- **Reprints.** `programFor` reads REGISTRY, then the program of the card's print_key group through `PROGRAM_ALIAS` (`registryAliases.generated.ts`, written by `scripts/registry-aliases.ts` from the local D1; `check:static` compares it when a sqlite is present and skips otherwise). The 628 same-print_key twin rows are gone and every id that had a program resolves to the same object. The 72 Expanded-only twins that D190/D290 left unbuilt now play their group's program, because legality is OR'd across a print_key group. Cross-name text twins keep explicit rows. Because the table comes from local dev state, a release first diffs local and remote `print_key` (conventions.md).
- **Coverage visible.** `simulationGaps(card)` lists unsimulated Trainer, Ability, Special Energy and attack text; the builder's card detail shows it. Ingest's Special Energy rule calls the engine's `isSpecialEnergy`.
- **Mutant corpus.** Rows were re-anchored or re-pointed to the moved code and re-run; 48 rows on the three deleted damage-tail copies and one duplicate reprint row were retired, because the same defect now lives once with a covering row. Rows labelled `E4` belong to this entry. Four D337 rows written for Ethan's Adventure had matched Licking Catch's identical search, and the symbol backfill had recorded that binding; they now target Ethan's Adventure. A whitespace-insensitive sweep found no other misbound row among the 23 with a lookalike elsewhere.

### 2026-10-26 — Engine↔client contract: clients read what the engine says

Phase 3 of the architecture audit, plus the user's three pending calls.

- **Decisions.** Deck legality is enforced online and not in local play (R1). The unused deck folder and reorder routes are retired (A6). Local simulator saves keep being discarded on any `engineVersion` change rather than migrated (P1); `persist.ts` stays the server's migration path only.
- **One view of a game.** The client sees a game only as `RedactedGame` plus the seat log. Prompts describe themselves: `unit:"counters"` on the damage-counter multi-pick, the engine sets `counterTransfer` itself, and the answering seat's effect phase carries `invokedBy` and `abilityName`. Nothing outside the engine reads `pendingOp`. `projectGameState`, `board.ts`, `viewLog.ts` and the dead `me3-111` bench rule are gone: the redacted side carries an optional `benchLimit` (absent at 5), and `moveToAction` routes over the redacted game. `deckCardIdsOf` replaces the client's copy of the uid scheme.
- **Cues.** `logFromEvents` attaches `cuesFromEvents`' `{shuffle, knockOut, coin}` cues to each log row, each carrying only what its row already shows both seats (`cues.test.ts` checks this over seeded walks). Both table sources animate from cues. That fixes the online Knock Out that was inferred from any discard, gives online play its coin flips, and removes the local coin-flip hold. Rows written before the deploy fall back to the shuffle wording only.
- **Deck rules.** `packages/engine/src/deckRules.ts` owns formats, the ACE SPEC/Radiant/Prism Star classes, `isLegalInFormat` and `validateDeck`/`savedDeckReasons`. The builder, the online picker and `beginMatch` all call it. `beginMatch` refuses a deck that is not legal in its own stored format (null plays Standard); both players see the reason and the lobby returns to deck selection. The sandbox plays any deck `createGame` accepts: 60 catalog cards with a Basic Pokémon.
- **Log deltas.** A client that announces `logDeltas` in its hello gets only the new log entries (`logFrom`) after an accepted action or a forfeit. The flag lives on the socket attachment, so it survives hibernation. Handoff, reconnect, a repeated hello and spectating send the whole log, as does any socket that never asked; the client answers a gap with a hello. On seeded games with real decks, the longest match's traffic fell from 16.8 MB to 4.7 MB. Splitting the card pool out of the stored record was measured and declined: the record peaks under 100 KB and the log, not the pool, is what grows.
  - Superseded in part by "Performance: measure first, then the boot download, the catalog cache, the save, the frame and the loop" (2026-10-27): the stored record is split after all, at the user's request.
- **API.** The lobby's seat and phase transitions moved from schema into `apps/api/src/lobby/snapshot.ts`; schema keeps `makePlayer`/`createSnapshot`. The catalog query schemas and the OAuth provider and failure codes live in schema, and the client imports their types. Deck routes accept `folderId` only as `null`; the `folders` table and column stay, so there is no migration.
- **Deploy order.** Every wire addition is an optional field and none is a new enum literal, so the API can deploy before the web client, as `release` does. `GAME_STATE_VERSION` stays 50.
- **Mutant corpus.** Nine rows on the deleted twin projection were retired because their defects no longer exist; two rows were re-pointed to `cues.ts` and `redact.ts`. Rows labelled `P3` and `R1` belong to this entry.

### 2026-10-26 — Frontend structure: named wire, a typed page shell, one simulator flow

Phase 4 of the architecture audit, web client only.

- **Named wire.** Each Lua mailbox row is declared once in `src/lua/wireFields.ts` (`WIRE_ROWS`, `as const`); emitters encode typed records with `wireRow`, so a missing or extra field fails to compile. `scripts/lua-constants.ts` generates `ui/wire_fields.lua`, and every Lua reader indexes by name. The dead library header slots, the always-empty deck `parent`/`cover` columns and the unread builder image flag are gone without a shim, because web deploys are atomic and content-hashed. Simulator snapshots stayed byte-identical.
- **The page shell.** `host.js` (now `// @ts-check` in its own checkJs project) keeps only love.js boot, mailbox writes, the viewport and card art. Routing, the accessible links, dialog semantics, text proxies and `COLORLESS:` print handling moved to TypeScript in `src/host/` (`router.ts`, `shell.ts`, `textProxy.ts`, `mailboxes.ts`), connected through `window.colorlessHost`. This reverses the 2026-10-23 "Path routes" placement of the router in host.js. The boot invariant: Love() starts on window load after the bridge module has connected, otherwise the failure overlay shows. Lua reads mailboxes by name from the generated `ui/host_constants.lua` (which also carries the lobby code alphabet and length), follows `__page.txt` and `__settings.txt` every frame instead of a 250 ms poll, owns the preferences record, and no longer sets the window title. Text inputs share one record; the sign-in form is checked with the schema's request schemas; screen adapters share `epochGuard()` and one registration table in `bridge.ts`.
- **One simulator flow.** `LuaSimulator` handles every answer from either table in `changed()`, through `TableSource.subscribe`; the local table notifies before `send` returns. Optional capabilities (`history`, `restart`, `rematch`, `turnDraws`, a required `leave`) replace the `instanceof` checks, `allowed()` is always a boolean, and the local table owns its automatic steps and its save (a failed save is `lifecycle().warning`). The local Prize picker was removed because both tables take Prizes automatically. The redacted game now carries the turn owner's allowances (optional, absent in setup), so the online HUD shows the Energy allowance and the card menu greys out a second attach; a dragged second attach still reaches the server and shows its reason, as a local drag shows the engine's.
- **Lua screens.** `simulator.lua`'s parse moved to `ui/sim_model.lua` (no LÖVE dependency, tested against the wire fixtures) and the Knock Out presentation to `ui/sim_ko.lua`. Pages no longer require other pages: deck backs come from `ui/card_back.lua`, the deck grid from `ui/deck_grid.lua`, attention text from `ui/fx.lua`, and `Button.modalFrameRaised`/`CardFrame.scan` replace copies; screenshots against the previous build matched to the pixel. Each screen has its own `cancelInteractions()`, and `main.lua` builds every screen's callbacks with `callbacksFor`, so every sign-in is guarded during a wipe.
- **Accessibility.** A deck that can't be played online stays focusable in the online picker, and its reason is read aloud; a repeated live-region message is re-announced.
- **Docs.** `docs/status.md` is the entry point. The 9.8 MB `progress.md` and the simulator workstream's build history moved verbatim to `docs/archive/`, and `docs/decision-index.md`, generated by `bun run docs:index` and gated in `check:static`, says where each decision is recorded.
- **Engine imports.** `interpreter.ts` and `effects.ts` no longer re-export helpers moved out of them; every importer names the defining module (H6).
- **Not done.** H5 (an explicit card-pool cache) was skipped: removing the test-only reset would thread the cache through every screen adapter for no observed defect. F2's art-loader slice was not attempted.

### 2026-10-27 — Performance: measure first, then the boot download, the catalog cache, the save, the frame and the loop

The 2026-10-26 performance audit (local report `tmp/perf/performance-audit.md`) found the runtime CPU healthy (about 46 µs per engine action) and the costs in downloads, growing persisted data, the catalog's KV use, the Lua frame and the dev loop. Nothing measured performance before, so every change below has a committed benchmark or a deterministic budget.

- **Measurement.** `bun run bench` replays seeded real-card games (`scripts/bench/pool.json`) through `applyAction`, both redactions, `logFromEvents` and the legality probes, times a cold engine import, and prints a fingerprint over every state, event, redaction and log row; a pure performance change must keep the fingerprint. `bun run bench:simsave` and `bun run bench:boot` (CDP-throttled cold boot; needs `CHROMIUM`) are opt-in. Timings never gate. What gates is deterministic: the Worker bundle size and zod locales (`scripts/worker-bundle.ts`), the simulator save sizes, the match record bytes per action and frame bytes (`matchBytes.test.ts`), Lua draw-call and canvas counts (`frame_budget.lua`), and the catalog query plans (`queryPlan.d1.test.ts`). CI runs the bench as a non-blocking job.
- **Engine.** `programFor` reads one pre-built Map; `disabledAbilityUids`, `cardOfUid` faces and the owner-bound Energy flag are remembered by object identity (conventions.md: never write into a state the engine has read); the KO sweep skips bodies below the HP floor. Apply went from 46 to 19 µs, two redactions from 139 to 83 µs, with an identical fingerprint. The engine no longer loads zod (`@colorless/schema/constants`), and the Worker drops zod's locales and the test-only schemas (1,131 → 878 KiB).
- **Boot.** game.data no longer carries the menu music or unused branding (5.36 → 1.45 MB). `music1.ogg`, at its original quality, is fetched after the game is ready, so on a cold first visit the music can start a moment late (user's call). The wasm is compiled while it downloads; game.data is not preloaded because the loader keeps it in IndexedDB. The page's eager bundle no longer contains the rules interpreter: screens before a table import `@colorless/engine/rules`, and the table, sandbox setup and card detail load the engine with `import()` (eager chunk 814 → 149 KB). The builder requests WebP scans. `/auth/me` is reused for 60 s. Lua reads a mailbox only when `__mailbox_revisions.txt` says it changed. Cold boot at 20 Mbps went from about 7.5 to 5.3 s and at 5 Mbps from 15.7 to 8.7 s on the loaded test machine.
- **Catalog.** The account is on the Workers Free plan (1,000 KV writes a day), so free-text queries (`name`, `nameExact`, `text`) bypass KV, and KV writes happen after the response and can fail without failing it. Ingest stamps a `catalog:epoch` key that joins the cache key, so an ingest retires cached pages within minutes without a `CACHE_VERSION` bump, and KV entries live a day. Migration 0009 replaces the name and set indexes (a 20-line decklist import reads 358 rows instead of 203,521); 0010 indexes `users.favourite_deck_id`. Later pages of a search pass `total` and skip `count(*)`. Ingest skips unchanged rows, fetches four at a time with a 24 h payload cache, and spawns wrangler once per set. The asset mirror writes R2 after responding and caches 404s for an hour.
- **Decks and pools.** `GET /cards/full?id=…` returns up to 100 full cards; card pools load in one or two requests instead of one per card. Pickers reuse saved decks while their `updated` stamp matches. Autosave writes only changed `deck_cards` rows. Sign-in deletes that account's expired sessions.
- **Local simulator save.** The save is the starting state once plus the list of accepted actions and a board fingerprint, replayed through the engine on load (`SAVE_VERSION` 2; older saves are dropped). Undo and redo by action or by turn now reach back to the start of the game, also after a reload (the user's requirement; the 40-step limit is gone). The history row now also shows on the setup screens, and a hot-seat prompt (a knock-out's promotion, a card's choice) carries Undo and Redo, so no step strands the player without a way back. Persisting a move takes under 1 ms instead of 15–65 ms, and a 95-turn save is about 66K characters instead of 4 M.
- **Online.** A reconnect sends one hello, and the DO answers an unchanged hello without writing or broadcasting. While awake the DO keeps the match record in memory (`MatchStore`). The record is stored split (`layout: 2`): the head, the card tables written once, and the log in 64-entry chunks of which an action rewrites only the last, so a game writes about 1.5 MB instead of 7.7 MB. This reverses the 2026-10-26 note that declined splitting the pool out: the user asked for the full version. Whole records written before the deploy are still read and are split on their next write; rolling the API back retires split matches. Match start loads both decks in parallel with one join each, the socket carries the display name so a hello makes no D1 query (a rename shows on the next connection), and the online table re-renders only when something it shows changes.
- **Lua table.** The pixel icons are baked once, shader uniforms are sent once per card, pile faces are reused when their top card is unchanged, each PNG is decoded once per distinct face, card-text rows are decoded only when they change, and text fitting is cached. Screens match the previous build to the pixel apart from a few antialiased icon-edge pixels in landscape.
- **Dev loop.** `scripts/check.ts` runs the `check:static` steps and `check:lua` concurrently, then vitest. Vitest keeps a module cache, and app suites that use no mocks or globals run without isolation (`app` / `app-isolated`). The engine and schema are composite TypeScript projects, so their sources are type-checked once. The mutant binding gate caches its verdicts (`tmp/binding-gate-cache.json`), and the mutant harness runs one batched baseline while still giving each mutant a fresh vitest process (the user's call). CI runs `static`, `lua`, `test` and `mutants` as parallel jobs, with TypeScript and vitest caches.
- **Not done.** AD6 (keeping the engine out of catalog-only Worker cold starts): with wrangler's single-file bundle a lazy import gave no gain and a larger bundle; doing it properly needs a custom esbuild build with code splitting. Deferring the save write to `pagehide` was not needed once a save took under 1 ms.
- **Deploy.** Apply migrations 0009 and 0010, deploy the API, then the web app (`bun run release` order), then run an ingest or put `catalog:epoch` once so the epoch keying starts. `GAME_STATE_VERSION` and `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` are unchanged.

### 2026-10-27 — Database safety nets before any schema change

The 2026-10-27 database audit (local report `tmp/db/database-audit.md`) found every query under 3 ms and no orphaned or inconsistent rows locally. Its risks were a latent migration hazard that deletes user data, Free-plan quota exhaustion, and missing recovery procedures. This is Phase 0: tooling and docs only, no schema change and no deploy. The user accepted all eleven recommendations in the report's section 6. They shape later phases: KV writes only for `/facets`, `/series`, `/sets`, `/sets/:id` and `/cards` pages 1–3; the login limiter fails open, then moves to D1 with a per-IP key; cards removed upstream are reported only; a deck-edit conflict reloads with a notice; the favourite-deck field is retired; 500 decks per account; IP limits on lobby create and `/ws`; self-service account deletion; no database triggers for value rules (db-check instead); a lobby join answers 503 when D1 fails.

- **Table rebuilds.** On D1, a drizzle-kit table rebuild deletes the rebuilt table's child rows, because `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF` does nothing inside the migration's transaction. `scripts/migration-guard.ts` (check:static) refuses rebuild SQL in unreviewed migrations. `apps/api/src/db/migrations.d1.test.ts` applies each newer migration file by itself on a database with a row in every table and fails if a row or reference is lost. A sample `decks` rebuild turned it red (deck_cards 1 → 0). docs/conventions.md "Database migrations" holds the expand/contract rule and the rebuild recipe.
- **Test harness.** `testing/d1.ts` reads `drizzle/` from disk. Through `import.meta.glob`, vitest's module cache kept the old file list, so tests did not see a newly added migration.
- **Drift.** `scripts/drizzle-drift.ts` (check:static) runs drizzle-kit generate on a scratch copy and fails if `schema.ts` has unrecorded changes.
- **Consistency.** `bun run db:check` (apps/api, `--remote`, `--upstream`) runs the read-only rules in `apps/api/src/db/consistency.ts`: integrity, foreign keys, cross-user ownership, value domains, one card per set number, the reprint post-pass, stored `print_key` against today's `printKey`, and cards tcgdex dropped. `consistency.d1.test.ts` proves each rule finds its violation.
- **Release.** `release` records the remote Time Travel bookmark (`tmp/d1-bookmarks.log`) and fails when the local and remote `print_key` columns differ; the API `deploy` fails while any migration is unapplied remotely. `ingest.ts --remote` records a bookmark before writing. Backup and recovery are written down in backend-data.md §3.7.3.

### 2026-10-27 — Database hot fixes: the shared quota, races and silent failures

Phase 1 of the 2026-10-27 database audit. Code only, no migration.

- **Login on a spent KV quota.** The Free plan's 1,000 KV writes a day are shared by the login limiter and the catalog cache. Before this, a failed limiter get or put made `/auth/login` and `/auth/register` return 500. Now a failed get lets the attempt through, and a failed put keeps the decision the get made, so a full window stays refused. Both are logged. The D1-backed limiter with a per-IP key comes in Phase 3.
- **Catalog cache.** The KV key is built from the validated query: unknown params, defaults and reordered repeats share one entry, and route params are decoded. Only `/facets`, `/series`, `/sets`, `/sets/:id` and `/cards` pages 1–3 (`MAX_CACHED_CARDS_PAGE` in schema) are stored. `/cards/:id`, `/cards/full`, later pages and any `/cards` request with `total` go to D1, with HTTP caching only. A query is validated before KV is touched. Repeatable filters are capped at 30 values each and 60 across all six, so a long list is a 400 rather than D1's 100-parameter 500.
- **Decklist import.** Exact lookups run first, then the name without its set code; the LIKE search runs only when both miss, with `total: 0`. A 20-line PTCG Live list makes 40 indexed lookups instead of reading about 200k rows.
- **Writes that check what they read.** A password login creates its session only while the verified hash is still stored, so it cannot undo an OAuth link that revoked the password. A rehash is scoped the same way. A deck PATCH writes only the fields it names and is scoped to its owner (versioned saves come in Phase 3). After its D1 reads, the lobby countdown hands off to the match only if the lobby is unchanged; otherwise it retries on the current lobby, with the deck reads reused and at most three new reads.
- **Lobby failures.** A D1 error at handoff becomes "Couldn't load the decks. Ready up to try again."; the alarm always re-arms. A D1 error while resolving the `/ws` cookie closes the accepted socket (1011) and answers 503. A deck loads at handoff only while the socket's session is unexpired and not revoked: the session id rides on the socket, and the existing deck query joins `sessions`.
- **This reverses D80's "a null timestamp counts as ALIVE".** Sockets record `connectedAt`, and a socket that never answered a keepalive is swept once it is older than the silence window plus 5 s. Attachments from before the deploy are stamped at their first sweep.
- **Lobby storage.** Wire ids over 64 characters are refused; names (64) and deck names (100) are clipped, including in stored snapshots. `commit()` writes storage before memory, skips unchanged snapshots and repeated alarm deadlines, and an action that changes nothing writes and sends nothing. Seat bindings are pruned to seated ids.
- **Logs.** `apps/api/src/http/log.ts` writes one JSON line per event (`evt` = `auth`, `kv`, `d1`, `lobby`, `after_response`), with no emails, IPs or player-typed text; repeated failures are throttled per isolate. `GET /health/db` counts the applied migrations and answers 503 on a D1 error. The monitoring runbook is in backend-data.md §3.7.4.
- **Sandbox.** `prepareSandbox` reads the picker's saved-deck cache instead of fetching both decks again.
- **Deploy.** Every wire change is optional, and `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` and `GAME_STATE_VERSION` are unchanged, so the API deploys before the web as `release` does. Old catalog KV entries expire within their one-day TTL.

### 2026-10-27 — Database additive changes: D1 sign-in counters, versioned decks, sweeps and limits

Phase 3 of the 2026-10-27 database audit. Migration 0011 (released with Phases 0–2) is additive only: `auth_rate_limits`, `decks.version`, `cards_pokemon_hp_idx`, and `cards_set_local_idx` made UNIQUE. `db-check` found no duplicate set numbers remotely, so the unique index could not fail.

- **Sign-in limiter in D1.** It replaces the KV limiter, so the catalog cache is the only KV writer. Two keys per attempt: (scope, ip, email) allows 10 per 15 minutes; (scope, ip) allows 50 across emails, room for a shared NAT. Each key is stored as a SHA-256 hash; an IPv6 address counts as its /64. The counting upserts run in the same batch as the user lookup. A refused attempt writes no row, so hammering the endpoint cannot spend D1's 100k writes a day. A D1 error fails open and is logged.
- **Daily sweep.** A Cron Trigger (`17 4 * * *`) deletes expired sessions and passed counters in chunks of 500 and logs `evt = "d1", kind = "sweep"`.
- **Lobby limits.** The user chose an IP limit on `POST /lobby` and the `/ws` upgrade only. Rate Limiting bindings allow 10 lobby creates and 60 socket opens a minute per address (a dead link's reconnect backoff tries about 11 times a minute). They count per location and approximately, so they bound floods rather than keep an exact budget. A missing or throwing binding allows the request.
- **Versioned deck saves.** Every deck write bumps `version`. A PATCH that carries the version it was made from writes only while the deck is still the caller's and at that version. The card writes run before the UPDATE that bumps it, so the three statements see the same row and write all or nothing. A stale save gets a 409 with the current deck; the builder reloads it and says "Deck changed elsewhere; reloaded the latest.", and drops saves still queued from the old copy. A PATCH without a version (an older client, or the library's rename) is last-writer-wins but still bumps the version.
- **Deck cap.** At most 500 decks per account (`MAX_DECKS_PER_USER`); a create past it gets a 409. The count rides on the query that already picks the new deck's position. Two creates racing at 499 can both succeed: the cap bounds reads, it is not an exact quota.
  - Superseded in part by "Quota and abuse limits: searches in the browser, rate-limit bindings, bounded lobbies" (2026-10-27): the insert statement enforces the cap, so racing creates cannot pass it.
- **Favourite deck retired.** The user chose to retire the API field rather than bring back a picker. `favouriteDeckId` is gone from the user schema and from PATCH `/auth/me`; the column stays, because dropping it would rebuild `users` (see "Database migrations" in conventions.md).
- **Index plans.** `sort=hp-desc` reads 50 rows a page through `cards_pokemon_hp_idx` instead of scanning and sorting; `queryPlan.d1.test.ts` pins it.
- **Deploy.** Every wire change is optional, so the API deploys before the web client. The cron and the rate-limit bindings take effect with the API deploy; their namespace ids (1001, 1002) must be unique in the account.

### 2026-10-27 — Account deletion, library controls, and fewer catalog and deck reads

Phase 4 of the 2026-10-27 database audit, and the last phase. Two of its items need no work: cards removed upstream stay report-only, and value rules stay in `db-check` with no triggers (both the user's decisions).

- **Account deletion.** The user chose self-service deletion and no purge of unverified accounts. `DELETE /auth/me` deletes the user with one statement, and the cascades remove sessions, identities, folders, decks and deck cards. A password account must give its current password. Attempts are counted under a separate `delete` scope of the sign-in limiter, and the delete matches only while the verified hash is still stored. The cookie is cleared as logout clears it. `User` gains `hasPassword`, so the UI knows whether to ask. Settings → Account → Delete account opens a form with a warning; the button must be pressed twice, like Leave match (the arm ignores a second press within 0.35 s and lapses after 3 s).
- **Library Rename and Delete.** The Manage, Rename and Delete dialogs added in 0ee12e76 never had a control that opened them. Each deck now has a "…" Manage button under its pile, its own focus stop after the deck and announced as "Manage <name>". Delete keeps its confirmation dialog.
- **Notices that fit.** Builder notices must fit one line of the 294 px caption at size 14 (m6x11plus); a wider one wrapped behind the Back bar. Six were reworded, among them "Could not save. Your edits are kept. Retry." and the deck-conflict notice.
- **Facets in one pass.** `/facets` reads the catalog once with `json_group_array(DISTINCT …)` per column (61,632 → 5,084 rows read), sorted in SQLite's byte order. The JSON is byte-identical, so `CACHE_VERSION` is unchanged.
- **Counted free-text pages.** A `name`/`nameExact`/`text` query without `total` takes its count from a window function over the page statement, ordered like the page so that it walks the sort's index. A page past the end falls back to a separate count. The builder fetches free-text results three pages per request, in fixed blocks, and splits them. Rows read per settled keystroke roughly halve (42,497 → 20,452 over 'char', 'chari', 'boss', 'ultra ball'). Searches of one or two very common letters read slightly more ('a': 5,160 → 7,261), accepted. Responses are unchanged, so old clients still work.
- **Cards on deck summaries.** `GET /decks` returns each complete (60-card) deck's `cards` from rows it already reads, and drops its separate count aggregate. The library's legality alert and the sandbox and online pickers judge decks from the list instead of calling `GET /decks/:id` for each; with an older API they still fetch. Opening a picker is one request instead of 1 + one per complete deck. At the 500-deck cap the worst-case list is about 1.1 MB raw and 111 KB gzipped, no more than the per-deck requests it replaces. Incomplete decks (up to 200 entries) carry no cards. The per-session saved-deck cache is gone.

### 2026-10-27 — Security safety nets before the fixes

Phase 0 of the 2026-10-27 security audit (51 findings: 4 high, 8 medium, 32 low, 7 info). The user accepted all 16 of the audit's recommended decisions. The ones that shape later phases: the session cookie moves to `SameSite=Lax` with no `__Host-` rename, since `workers.dev` is a public suffix so the app and the API are the same site; a Google-only account must type its email to delete itself; catalog search gets a per-IP limit and an offset cap, then moves to the client; one cryptographic generator replaces mulberry32 for online and local play; lobby WebSockets keep cookie auth behind an Origin allowlist; the Workers 100k requests/day quota is accepted as a known availability risk.

- **Route access sweep.** `routeAccess.d1.test.ts` classifies every registered route as public, requireUser or owner. A route missing from the table fails the test. Protected routes must answer 401 without a cookie, and owner routes 404 for another user's deck, with no row changed.
- **Cross-site matrix.** `crossSite.d1.test.ts` sends every unsafe route and the lobby upgrade from a foreign Origin with a valid cookie. What already holds is asserted. The known gaps (AU1 socket upgrade, AEX1 logout CSRF, `SameSite=None`, no Origin guard) are `it.fails` cases asserting the fixed behaviour, so the Phase 1-2 fixes must flip them.
- **Lobby abuse baselines.** `lobbyAbuse.test.ts` measures today's costs: one put and one alarm per accepted frame, no frame size or socket cap, an idle pinging socket keeps a lobby alive, and a shadow socket keeps acting after its seat is bound (LBX1). Each assertion names its finding, so the Phase 1-3 limits change them on purpose.
- **Engine fuzz.** `actionFuzz.test.ts` drives about 68k seeded crafted actions (prototype keys, wrong types, out-of-turn and other-seat actions) through the DO's path in about 3 s. The engine must not throw or mutate its input, and every accepted state must redact cleanly and keep each card in one zone.
- **MI3.** A turn-1 `attack` with index `"__proto__"` threw out of the engine. `attackGateOf` now reads only an integer index, and `applyMatchAction` turns any engine throw into the usual refusal plus a throttled `action_threw` log line. Legal play is unchanged (bench fingerprint unchanged).
- **Dev and release hygiene.** The Vite dev server denies `.dev.vars`, `.wrangler/`, `apps/` and `tmp/`. CI has read-only permissions, actions pinned to commit SHAs, and Dependabot. `release:gate` refuses a dirty tree or a HEAD that is not `origin/main`. Both Workers set `preview_urls: false`. `scripts/autoloop` is deleted. §3.7.4 lists the Worker and Durable Object quotas.

### 2026-10-27 — Security hot fixes: lobby origins, the print bridge, seat ownership and the request edge

Phase 1 of the 2026-10-27 security audit. Server-only and web-only fixes, with no migration. The web client and the API change together in one `bun run release`.

- **Lobby socket origins (AU1).** A WebSocket upgrade whose `Origin` is not one of the app's CORS origins gets 403 before the rate limiter and the Durable Object. An upgrade with no `Origin` (not a browser, so no victim cookie) still passes. The cross-site matrix case now passes. A new web origin must be added to `APP_ORIGIN` or online play refuses it.
- **One print bridge (WC1).** Lua's `announce` print put opponent names on the line-based Lua-to-host bridge raw, so a name containing a line feed forged host commands. Every Lua print now goes through `frontend/lua/ui/bridge.lua`, which percent-encodes each field. `shell.ts` rejects any payload outside the encoded alphabet and decodes each channel exactly once. `scripts/lua-bridge-guard.ts` (in `check:static`) refuses any other print in shipped Lua. `transition-art` became `used\t7`.
- **Names carry no control or format characters.** One rule in `packages/schema/src/text.ts` (Unicode Cc and Cf). Register, `PATCH /auth/me` and the lobby `set-name` refuse such names. A lobby hello and a deck name are stripped instead: a hello that failed validation would lock out an account whose stored name predates the rule. The DO also strips names read from D1 and from stored snapshots. Joined emoji sequences (zero-width joiner) are refused in new account names. The pixel font does not draw them anyway.
- **Deleting a Google-only account needs its email.** `DELETE /auth/me` on an account with no password requires `confirmEmail` equal to the account's email (trimmed, case-insensitive), else 403. The Settings form shows an empty email field for such accounts and is never prefilled. This is the user's decision 2 (a).
- **Seats belong to their account's sockets (LBX1).** When an account binds a seat, other sockets on that seat that are not the account's are closed (1008, `seat_evicted`). Every intent, action, bye, rematch and host reject re-checks `ownsSeat`, and so do `viewerOf` and `ownerOf`, so a shadow socket cannot act, read the hand or supply the deck.
- **Shuffled decks at handoff (MIX1).** `handoffToMatch` crypto-shuffles both deck lists before `beginMatch` mints uids, so uid order no longer reveals the list. This is not the RNG change (MI1, Phase 4).
- **Request edge.**
  - Dependencies: hono 4.13, zod 4.6, @hono/zod-validator 0.9. `bun audit --prod` is clean, and the CI static job now gates on it, through bun 1.4.2, since 1.3.13's audit has no `--prod`.
    - Amended 2026-11-09 (dependency audit PRC5, DEP-P0/DEP-P1): the reason given for the prod-only gate ("dev tools are left to Dependabot") does not hold. GitHub's dependency graph does not read bun.lock: it lists the manifest ranges and no transitive package, so Dependabot raised 0 alerts while `bun audit` reported 42. The `--prod` gate stays. The whole tree is reported weekly by `.github/workflows/deps-report.yml` and monthly by `bun run deps:check`. Since DEP-P1 the gate runs on the pinned bun (1.4.2), not through `bunx`.
  - CORS: preflights allow only `Content-Type` and GET, HEAD, POST, PATCH and DELETE.
  - Requests: 64 KB body cap with a JSON 413 (the largest real request is about 8 KB); at most 20 validation issues; `/decks` POST and PATCH check the session before validating.
  - Responses: every one carries `nosniff` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-site`, and framework errors are JSON.
- **Logging and failures.**
  - AE4: unhandled errors log the route, the error names and the SQL text, never the message or bound values.
  - `isUniqueViolation` looks through drizzle's wrapper, so a racing OAuth insert retries instead of returning 500.
  - AU5: the sign-in limiter now fails closed. If its D1 batch fails, register, login and delete answer 503 with `Retry-After: 60`, instead of becoming an unthrottled password oracle.
- **Web headers (AE3).** Every page carries:
  - `frame-ancestors 'none'` as a header (a meta CSP cannot set it);
  - `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`;
  - a deny-all `Permissions-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`.
  
  There is no COEP, because love.js and the cross-origin card art need none.

### 2026-10-27 — A same-site Lax session cookie and a cross-site guard

Phase 2 of the 2026-10-27 security audit (AEX1). It corrects the premise behind the known limit in "The Lua frontend replaces React" (2026-10-22). The public suffix list's entry is the bare `workers.dev`, with no wildcard, so the registrable domain is `jofrescari.workers.dev`. The web app and the API are therefore different origins but the same site, and the session cookie was never third-party. `SameSite=None` gained nothing and let any site's requests carry the cookie (logout CSRF, the AU1 socket hijack, authenticated framing).

- **Lax.** The session cookie is `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax`. Nobody is logged out: the attribute change keeps the name and the token. `GET /auth/me`, the session check every page load makes, re-sends the cookie with its remaining lifetime (on a 304 too), so the old `None` cookies are replaced instead of living out their 30 days. The `__Host-` rename was declined (user decision 1): it would log everyone out once, so it waits for a move that does that anyway.
- **Cross-site guard.** Any POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE whose `Origin` is not an app origin (`null` included), or that sends `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, gets 403 before it runs. A request without `Origin` passes: browsers send `Origin` on every such method, so it is not a page acting with a visitor's cookie. This also closes `POST /lobby` and register to other sites (LB3's hygiene part). JSON content types are not required globally, because logout and `POST /lobby` send no body.
- **Constraint.** The web app and the API must stay on one registrable domain. A move to a custom domain (AE2, decision 6) must put both on it, or sign-in breaks.

### 2026-10-27 — Quota and abuse limits: searches in the browser, rate-limit bindings, bounded lobbies

Phase 3 of the 2026-10-27 security audit. Anyone could spend the account's shared Workers Free quotas (5M D1 rows read, 100k rows written, 1,000 KV writes and 100k Durable Object writes a day), which stops sign-in, decks and the lobby for everyone until 00:00 UTC. This phase bounds each path. The Worker's own 100k requests a day remains, accepted as a known availability risk (user decision 6, §3.7.4).

- **Search in the browser (CA1, decision 3).**
  - `GET /cards/index` serves every card as one compact row (about 280 KB gzipped, one KV entry per epoch). The builder answers name and rules-text searches from it, and decklist import resolves names from it. A free-text search now reads 0 D1 rows (it was about 10k a request).
  - The shared matcher (`searchCardIndex`) is pinned to `GET /cards` by a parity test of 54 queries.
  - Once loaded, the index also serves every page the API would not cache (HP bounds, pages past the third), so the builder asks `/cards` only for KV-cached pages. Browser QA found that brisk paging through server pages ran into the search limit, which is why.
  - Server-side, `/cards` refuses pages past 1,000 cards. `CATALOG_SEARCH_LIMIT` (30 a minute per IP) covers KV-bypassing scans and `/cards/index` misses. Uncached reads are tallied in an `evt=d1 kind=catalog_reads` line.
- **Catalog and assets (CA2-CA4, CAX1).**
  - Empty results and HP bounds no longer enter KV.
  - Asset URLs are pinned to https `assets.tcgdex.net`, and mirrored bodies must be images under 5 MB.
  - Only the files the client uses are mirrored: card `high.webp`, set `logo.webp` and `symbol.webp`.
  - tcgdex ids are validated at ingest, and the ingest SQL file paths cannot leave their directory.
- **Sign-up and deck writes (AUX1, DK1-DK3, decisions 4 and 5).**
  - Register has its own D1 budget of 5 an hour per IP.
  - `AUTH_LIMIT` (10 a minute per scope and IP) refuses floods before any D1 write.
  - `DECK_WRITE_LIMIT` (60 a minute per account) and `DECK_WRITE_IP_LIMIT` (180 per IP) bound deck writes; the saver waits out a 429 and keeps the edits.
  - The 500-deck cap is now enforced in the insert statement, so racing creates can no longer pass it. This supersedes the database audit's accepted race.
  - Card ids on write must be 1-40 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`.
  - Binding errors fail open everywhere, and the D1 sign-in limiter behind them still fails closed.
  - Not done: a deck save still rewrites every card row, so an account at its burst limit can still spend the daily write quota. Denormalizing and diff-saving decks is the later option (decision 4, option 3).
- **Lobbies (LB1-LB3, MI2, MIX2, decision 7).**
  - Frames: every socket has a budget of 40 frames, refilled at 5 a second, checked before parsing; 1008 when it is overrun, and 1009 for a frame over 16K characters. Both closes take the normal seat-gone path.
  - Alarms are no longer re-armed when one is already due earlier, so a commit costs one row instead of two.
  - Sockets: 12 per lobby (2 seats, 8 spectators, 2 for a reload), with an account that holds a seat let in up to 16.
  - Inactivity: an hour with no activity closes the lobby with 4000, and the updated client stops reconnecting on that code.
  - Each distinct spectator or seat frame is built once per broadcast.
  - Creating a lobby costs 5 rows instead of 8.
  - The match log keeps at most 2,000 rows, cut to the newest 1,500 behind a "trimmed" row.
  - Repeatable-ability uses are not capped, since that would be a rules change; the frame budget bounds their rate.
- **Decklist import (WC3).** At most 200 card lines, 120 characters a line and 8 lookups at a time. The quadratic trailing-set regex is replaced, and set codes may start with a digit ("Pikachu 30TH 023" imports).

### 2026-10-27 — A cryptographic match RNG and secret seat credentials

Phase 4 of the 2026-10-27 security audit. The engine and the lobby protocol change together.

- **ChaCha20 for every new game (MI1, user decision 8a).** The match RNG was mulberry32 with a 32-bit seed. A player recovered the seed from their own opening hand in about four minutes, then predicted every shuffle and flip and located the opponent's hidden cards.
  - `rngState` is now either `{alg: "chacha20", key, counter}`, a 256-bit key from `crypto.getRandomValues` drawn through `@noble/ciphers` 2.4.0 (pinned exactly, audited, synchronous), or the old mulberry32 number.
  - The lobby's seed type admits only the ChaCha20 form, and new local games (sandbox start and restart) mint one. A numeric seed still means mulberry32, so tests, fixtures and the bench are unchanged (fingerprint unchanged).
  - The key never leaves the engine state: redaction copies nothing from `rngState`, and a test plays a match with a spectator through a hibernate and checks that no frame or log line carries it. The audit's brute force over all 2^32 seeds now finds no candidate.
  - Old local saves are not invalidated: a save replays its actions with the generator it was made with, so a mulberry32 save keeps replaying identically. This is the migration the user asked for.
  - Live online matches are rekeyed on their next read: a v50 record still on mulberry32 continues under a fresh ChaCha20 key. Cards it already dealt stay where they are until the next shuffle.
  - `GAME_STATE_VERSION` and `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` go to 51, so a rollback retires ChaCha20 matches instead of misreading their state as a fixed seed.
  - The "fresh 32-bit reseed per action" stopgap was rejected: any shuffle seeded with 32 bits whose output a player partly sees leaks its whole order.
- **Deck ids stay in the lobby (LB5).** Snapshots carry `hasDeck` (and the deck name) instead of each seat's saved-deck id. The DO keeps the ids in its stored state and strips them before sending. A test plays a full match, concede and rematch, and checks that no frame to anyone carries a deck id or a seat secret.
- **Secret seat credentials (LB6).** A seat was claimed by its public `playerId`, which every snapshot broadcast.
  - Each tab now mints a 256-bit secret (in sessionStorage per lobby) and sends it only in `hello`. Snapshots, intents, byes, rematches and kicks use the public id, the first 22 base64url characters of SHA-256(secret), which client and DO compute synchronously (`packages/schema/src/lobby/seatId.ts`, pinned to WebCrypto by a test). The DO never stores the secret.
  - A public id sent as a hello is refused, binds nothing and cannot act.
  - The Phase 1 account binding and eviction are unchanged.
