- **Cutover.** A lobby stored before the change cannot be carried over, since its ids were public, so the DO wipes it on the next upgrade (404) or alarm (4000) and logs `lobby_wiped cause=legacy`. A tab still running the old client sends a UUID hello, is answered "full" and holds nothing until it reloads. The client and the API ship in the same `bun run release`.

### 2026-10-27 — Auth hardening, an audit trail, and private default names

Phase 5 of the 2026-10-27 security audit, and the last phase. One additive migration (0012) and two new Worker secrets, which must be set before the release.

- **Audit trail (AU10, LB8).** Security events are logged:
  - auth: register, login success and failure, logout, logout-all, profile change, deletion, OAuth create and link or claim, rate-limit refusals;
  - lobby: seat-claim refusals, abuse and overflow closes, wipes, rematch refusals.
  
  An account is named by a 12-character HMAC tag under the `LOG_TAG_KEY` secret ("untagged" without it), never by email, IP, id, seat secret or deck id. A test holds lobby lines to that.
- **Password pepper (AU9, decision 10).**
  - New hashes are `pbkdf2p$<pepper id>$…`, HMAC-peppered with `PASSWORD_PEPPER`. Old `pbkdf2$` hashes still verify and are re-peppered at the next login, so nobody is logged out. `PASSWORD_PEPPER_PREVIOUS` allows a rotation.
  - Without the secret the Worker keeps making unpeppered hashes and logs an error rather than refusing sign-in: a configuration mistake should not become an outage. A hash naming a pepper the Worker lacks answers 503.
  - A lost pepper locks peppered accounts out of password sign-in, so the value is kept in a password manager.
  - `release:gate` gains a read-only `secrets` step that refuses a release while either secret is missing.
- **Per-email failure cap (AU6, decision 9).** After 50 failed logins for one email in an hour, from any address, further failures get 429 instead of 401. The password is still checked, so the owner signs in past the cap: no lockout and no new oracle. It does not slow guessing spread over many addresses; the per-IP budgets do that.
- **Sign out everywhere (AU7).** `POST /auth/logout-all` ends every session of the account, and Settings → Account has the control. No idle timeout: it would cost a D1 write per active session per day.
- **Google sign-in (AU12, AU4).** The flow uses PKCE (S256); the verifier rides in the state cookie, so sign-ins in flight at deploy fail once. Claiming an unverified account also removes its other Google identities. A Google email Google has not verified creates no account (`email-unverified`).
- **Private default names (AU3, AU8, decision 12).**
  - New accounts without a typed name, and every new Google account, get a generated handle (`Trainer-YWHU`); the Google real name is no longer read.
  - Migration 0012 adds `users.name_prompt`. It flags accounts whose name is their email's local part and every Google-linked account, since nothing records where a linked account's name came from. Names do not change.
  - Flagged accounts see "Choose your public name" once after sign-in (Save, Keep or Later). Saving or keeping clears the flag, which `User.namePrompt` carries.
  - One `displayNameField` rule (normalized, trimmed, 1-64 characters, no control or format characters) serves register, `PATCH /auth/me` and the lobby hello, which still strips rather than refuses. The unused lobby `set-name` message is gone.
- **Rematch re-check (MI4, decision 13).** A rematch reads the two seats' sessions once. A logged-out or deleted account cannot start one, and its socket is closed so it reconnects as a spectator. If the read fails, the rematch proceeds, since match start re-checks each session anyway.
- **Not done.** Email verification and password reset stay unbuilt (decision 11): register remains an enumeration oracle and a squatted address cannot be recovered (backend-data.md §3.5).
- **Deploy order.** Set `PASSWORD_PEPPER` and `LOG_TAG_KEY` (`openssl rand -base64 32`, saved in a password manager), then `bun run release`, which applies 0012 before the API deploy.

### 2026-10-28 — Online lobby reliability: a client-to-DO seam suite and the fixes it found

Phase 1 of the 2026-10-28 testing and reliability audit. The API changes are backward compatible with the current web client, so the API deploys first, then the web client, in one release.

- **Seam suite (LB4, user decision 2).** `src/seam/lobby.test.ts` runs two tabs' real channel, `LobbyClient`, Lua online modal and table against the real `LobbyDO` on fake timers, through `src/seam/lobbyBridge.ts`. Its sockets deliver each way a task later, answer pings as the runtime's auto-response does, and can drop, go half-open, fail to connect, connect slowly or be cut by a redeploy. Each fix below has a case there and a unit case.
  - The suite imports the harness as `@colorless/api/testing/lobbyHarness`, a test-only export on the api package (`apps/api/src/testing/lobbyHarness.ts`), not an `ALLOWED_CROSSINGS` entry, so the cross-root rule is unchanged. The root package depends on `@colorless/api` and `@cloudflare/workers-types` as dev dependencies.
  - `src/seam` has its own project, `tsconfig.seam.json`, with the DOM and the Workers types; `tsconfig.app.json` excludes it.
  - `worker-bundle.ts --check` now fails if the Worker bundle carries a test-only module (anything under an app's `src/testing/` or a `*Harness.ts`).
  - `createLobbyChannel` takes an optional `openSocket` factory, so each tab's sockets upgrade as that tab's account. The browser path is unchanged.
- **Hello first on every open (LBX1).** A frame queued while the link was down was flushed ahead of the client's next hello, onto a socket with no seat, and dropped. The channel no longer queues a hello. It keeps only the newest one, sends it first on every open, then the queued frames.
- **Crossed rematches (LB1, user decision 1: both sides).** When both players pressed Rematch within one round trip, the DO dropped the second frame and both tables waited on "Waiting..." for good.
  - Client, the primary fix: a table whose rematch is pending readies its seat once per match when it sees the lobby `selecting` with that seat not ready. This also recovers a rematch frame that never arrived. Once per match, so a handoff that fails and unreadies both is left to the players. The `onlineTable.test.ts` fixture that expected the wait is corrected.
  - Server, second: a rematch from an unready seat in a `selecting` lobby readies it, as the set-ready intent would, after the same session check a rematch makes, so a signed-out seat is refused the same way. A rematch whose session read the other rematch overtook is taken the same way. An already ready seat's repeat reads nothing.
- **Half-open sockets (LB3, user decision 3: about 10 s).** The channel gives up on an open socket with no frame back for 10 s since its oldest unanswered ping, and on a socket still connecting after 10 s. It detaches the socket and reconnects at once, without waiting for a close a dead path may hold back for a minute. The count starts at the unanswered ping rather than the last frame, so a background tab whose timers fire once a minute does not trip it.
- **Lone surrogates (WC1).** The name rule (`text.ts`) counts an unpaired surrogate (`\p{Cs}`) with the control and format characters: account writes refuse it, and the hello, intent and stored-seat cleaners strip it. Their clips go through one `clipText`, which strips first and never leaves half a pair. `encodeFields` sends a lone surrogate as U+FFFD, so no string can stop a screen's snapshots.
- **Reconnect backoff (LB5).** The backoff starts over only after a socket stayed open 5 s (`STABLE_OPEN_MS`), not on every open, and each delay is spread by ±20%. A tab a full lobby keeps turning away went from 150 sockets a minute to about 12.
- **Late first state (LB6).** A first state or full refusal after the 4 s not-found window starts the client's poll again, unless the server closed the lobby for good.
- **Mutation survivors (LB7).** New lobbyDO tests pin the rematch's re-read after its session check and the forfeit alarm (a late alarm moves the 30 s sweeps off the forfeit deadline, which they used to hit by coincidence). The audit's third case, an action whose migration read a seat change overtakes, is not written: every change that unseats a player in game also drops the match, which the migration's own re-read already sees, so the guard cannot be reached on its own.
- **Seats orphaned by a deploy (LB2).** A deploy drops every socket without a close the DO hears. The alarm pass now gives the reconnect grace to any seat shown connected that no socket holds and no grace covers (logged as `seat_orphaned`). It is a grace rather than a disconnect, because a socket that reconnected but has not said hello yet holds no seat either. The harness gains `redeploy()`.
- **Seeded lifecycles (LB10).** `lobbyLifecycle.test.ts` runs 20 seeds of 300 random steps (open, drop, hello, deck, ready, concede, bye, rematch, alarms, time, hibernate, redeploy) and checks four invariants after each step. Without the LB2 grace the first three seeds break.
- **A failed page (LUX1).** The host's `fail()` calls the bridge's new `onFailed`, and the online adapter closes its lobby link without a bye, so the opponent's grace and forfeit clock start. The seat's identity is kept for a reload.
- **Ids.** The finding ids here are the reliability audit's, not the 2026-10-27 security audit's ids of the same names (its LB1 to LB8 are other findings). Fifteen mutant rows declare the guards above under `REL-<id>` decisions (`bun run mutants --decision REL-`); each is killed by the suites it names.

### 2026-10-28 — Deck saves that survive a lost response, and client request deadlines

Phase 2 of the 2026-10-28 testing and reliability audit. Web client only: no API behaviour, route or migration changed. The lost-response fix landed first, because every later change here (the write deadline, the automatic retry and the keepalive flush) makes more saves whose outcome is unknown.

- **A lost save is recognised by its own 409 (AP1, APIX1, decision 4).**
  - A PATCH that committed but whose response was lost used to be re-sent with the stale version; the 409 reloaded the server copy and dropped every edit since, blaming another device.
  - The builder now remembers every save since the last acknowledged one that got no answer. A 409 whose deck, run through the same `savedSnapshot`/`toSnapshot` rules and serialization as the acknowledged copy, equals one of them is that save landing: the builder adopts the 409's version and sends the current edits in the same save, or nothing more when they are exactly what landed. Any other 409 reloads as before. The comparison is safe against any of the remembered snapshots, since the server then holds content this tab sent.
  - An unknown outcome also makes the saver forget its acknowledged key and the builder its acknowledged copy, so an undo back to the old state is still sent and counts as unsaved until it is.
  - Unknown outcome means no response (a network failure or a client timeout) or a 5xx, which a gateway can send after the Worker committed. A 4xx is a refusal before any write. This is wider than the audit's "a rejection without an HTTP status", since AP6 retries 502-504.
- **Request deadlines (WC3, decision 6).** Every `api.ts` call runs under a deadline that aborts its fetch and rejects with `RequestTimeoutError`, not an ApiError: 20 s for reads and the auth calls, 30 s for deck writes, 60 s for the card index (one large body). Create lobby gives up after 10 s. The deadline covers the body read too, and it rejects even if a fetch ignores its signal. No call is retried because of a timeout, except a deck autosave through AP6; the screens already offer Retry. Deck create and delete also get the 30 s deadline: a create that times out after it committed shows an error while the deck exists, which the library's reload then shows, and that beats a create that never settles.
- **Autosave retries passing failures (AP6, decision 5).** A save that failed with no response, a timeout, or a 502, 503 or 504 is sent again after 2, 4 and 8 s, still "saving", before it is reported failed. A 500 and every 4xx are reported at once. The count of rejections in a row is shared with the 429 backoff.
- **Unsaved edits across the page lifecycle (WC5, decision 7).** Pending edits are sent at once with a keepalive PATCH when the tab is hidden, on `pagehide` and on `beforeunload`; a failed save is sent again on `online`. A save already in flight or waiting out a backoff is left to finish. No localStorage draft. The beforeunload condition is `LuaBuilder.hasUnsavedEdits`, which also counts a save queued or in flight (a revert made during a flight matches the acknowledged copy but is not sent yet), and the listeners live in `src/host/unsavedEdits.ts`. If a browser refuses keepalive for a preflighted request, the save fails like any network error and AP6 retries it without keepalive.
- **Saver guards (WC6).** Seven deckSaver cases for ordering rules no test held, and one bug they found: a queued snapshot that converged onto the flight it waited for left the status at "saving" for good; it now reports "saved". The saver, the builder's lost-save handling, `request()`, the deadlines, the retries, the lifecycle flush, the simulator save, the host shell and the reload throttle have mutant rows (AP1, APIX1, AP4, AP6, WC3-WC6, WC8, WC9), all killed.
- **Contract suite (AP4).** `src/seam/api.test.ts` runs `api.ts` against the real Hono app on a real D1, with a fetch that adds the page's Origin and keeps a cookie jar, asserting the statuses and bodies the screens branch on. The api exports what it needs as `@colorless/api/testing/contract`. It uses the seam typecheck project and root dev dependencies that Phase 1 introduces for its own seam suite, with the same lines. `stubFetchResponse` now hands each call its own copy of the body.
- **Stale simulator save (WC8).** A save write that does not stick removes the stored save unless it is an earlier point of the same match. With two tabs on the simulator, the other tab's match counts as another one, so a failed write in one tab can remove the other's save.
- **Host shell (WC4, WC9).** `HostShell.onPrint` catches and reports anything a command throws (love.js swallowed it, with the next command), the bridge reports a command's rejection, and a copy without `navigator.clipboard` says it failed. No window `error` or `unhandledrejection` listener was added: `reportError` only writes to the console, which the browser already does for both. HostShell has its first suite, and the reload-once-a-minute rule moved to `src/host/reloadThrottle.ts` with a test.

### 2026-10-28 — API failure paths: D1 write failures and stalled upstreams degrade to sensible answers

Phase 3 of the 2026-10-28 testing and reliability audit. API only, except the new OAuth code's message in the web client. Every change is backward compatible with the current web client, so the API deploys first.

- **Shared failing-D1 helper (AP9).** `failingD1(db, { sql?, batch?, error? })` in `apps/api/src/testing/d1.ts` fails the statements whose SQL matches, alone or inside a batch, or every batch, with a spent-quota error by default; every other statement runs on the real D1. It replaces the hand-written Proxies in the deck, auth, rate-limit and sweep suites; the race and counting Proxies stay, since they wrap a call rather than fail it.
- **OAuth callback on a D1 failure (AP2, user decision 8).** A failure in find-or-create or the session insert used to answer a raw JSON 500 at the API origin. It now logs `evt = "auth", kind = "oauth_unavailable"` (error level, `errorFields`) and redirects back to the app with `?authError=unavailable`. The code is in `OAUTH_FAILURES`, and the account screen says "Sign-in is unavailable right now. Try again later." A client that does not know it shows the generic Google failure.
- **Logout and expired sessions (AP3).** Logout clears the cookie with a 204 even when its delete fails, logging `logout_failed`; the row then ends at its expiry. An expired session's cleanup delete runs after the response, and its failure is logged `expired_session_delete_failed` (throttled), so the caller still gets the 401 with the cleared cookie. Sign out everywhere still answers 500 when its delete fails, because there the 500 is true.
- **Upstream deadlines (AP5).** The token exchange, the userinfo request and the asset-origin fetch each give up after 10 s, body included, through the existing `exchange`/`profile` redirect and the asset 502. `apps/api/src/http/deadline.ts` aborts the fetch and also races it, so a fetch that ignores its signal is bounded too. A mirrored image whose body breaks off mid-read is now a 502, not a 500.
- **Cached shapes (AP7).** `apps/api/src/catalog/cacheShape.d1.test.ts` fingerprints the response schema of each KV-cached route (`CACHED_RESPONSE_SCHEMAS` in `catalog/routes.ts`) together with the card index columns and version, pinned per `CACHE_VERSION`. The card index columns are pinned again per `CARD_INDEX_VERSION`. Both tables are append-only: a shape change fails until the version is bumped and a row added. A census request to every catalog GET route checks that the pinned routes are exactly the ones that store in KV. Changes to derived values still need the manual bump, as the `CACHE_VERSION` comment says. The card index payload's `version` is now `CARD_INDEX_VERSION`, and `prepareCardIndex` refuses any other: the decklist import falls back to `/cards`, the builder's text search fails with its Retry, and the next load fetches the index again. That is the only guard for browser and CDN copies, which a `CACHE_VERSION` bump does not reach.
- **Redirect property (AP11).** `oauth.test.ts` keeps a 2,000-case seeded hostile-redirect property, about 0.2 s, built from the audit's probe.
- **Guards.** Mutant rows `REL-AP9`, `REL-AP2`, `REL-AP3`, `REL-AP5` and `REL-AP7`, all killed.
- **Deploy.** No migration, binding or secret. Deploy the API first, then the web client; the two can ship in one `bun run release`.


### 2026-10-28 — Data ops and the release pipeline: a restore recipe proven locally, recovery per release step

Phase 5 of the 2026-10-28 testing and reliability audit. Scripts and docs, plus two ingest behaviour changes (the tcgdex transport retries, and a legality guard). Nothing to deploy; the new release order takes effect on the next `bun run release`. Nothing was run with `--remote`.

- **Restore runbook (OP1, DOX1, user decision 9).** `docs/workstreams/backend-data.md` §3.7.3 now has a recipe that a local drill followed command for command. It exports the whole database with `--no-schema`, which brings the catalog and the `d1_migrations` rows. Chunks are picked by table with `grep`, parent first: the catalog, then users, folders and decks together (the favourite and parent-folder cycles), then deck_cards, then sign-in rows. Each chunk starts with `PRAGMA defer_foreign_keys=TRUE` and stays under 600 KB (`split -C`). `d1_migrations` and `sqlite_sequence` are left out. The data statements of every migration newer than the export are re-run (today 0003 and 0012). A repair into the live database uses `INSERT OR IGNORE`, never `OR REPLACE`.
  - The drill (`tmp/rel/probe/phase5/drill.md`): a source at migration 0011 with synthetic users (60 users, 400 decks, 12,000 deck cards) and the full catalog was exported, rebuilt into a fresh database at 0012 and checked with `db:check`. Every table matched the source migrated in place, `name_prompt` included. It also reproduced each failure the runbook warns about. Importing the export as written fails on `d1_migrations.id`. Without the backfill, 16 of 60 users keep the wrong `name_prompt`. A chunk that references a later chunk imports nothing. A plain INSERT into the live copy aborts. `INSERT OR REPLACE` of the users chunk cascaded away every deck card and session in the database.
  - The audit recommended `INSERT OR REPLACE` for repairs; the drill showed it deletes children through `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so the runbook says `OR IGNORE`, and to overwrite damaged values from a local copy or with Time Travel.
  - After a Time Travel restore the runbook runs `release-gate.ts migrations` first. A restore to the release bookmark rewinds `d1_migrations`, so a listed file means: roll the Worker back, fix the bad migration, then release, rather than following the gate's hint to migrate.
  - The remote Time Travel rehearsal on a throwaway `colorless-drill` database (decision 9c) is a checklist in §3.7.3 and has not been run. No restore script, no periodic drill.
    - Amended 2026-11-10 (build audit DEP2, BLD-P0): the remote rehearsal was run on 2026-10-28 with the user's OK and held (backend-data.md §3.7.3, "Remote Time Travel rehearsal"). A Worker rollback has still never been rehearsed.
  - `docs/conventions.md`: a migration's data statements come after its DDL, each starting a line, so the recipe can re-run them.
- **Release order (OP3, OP4, OP5, decision 10).** `bun run release` is now check → `release:gate` → `build` → `db:migrate:remote` → `api:deploy` → `release:health` → `deploy:web`. The web bundle is built before production changes. `release-gate.ts clean-tree` runs again right before each `wrangler deploy`: inside the API `deploy` (after `migrations`) and in the new root `deploy:web`, which deploys the prebuilt `dist/`. `bun run deploy` is `build` then `deploy:web`, so a bare web deploy now refuses a dirty tree or a HEAD that is not `origin/main`, as the API deploy does. No worktree-based release.
  - `release:health` (`release-gate.ts health`) fetches the deployed `/health/db` from `.env.production`'s origin (`API_ORIGIN` overrides), three tries five seconds apart with a 10 s deadline each. It fails unless D1 was read and the migration count equals `drizzle/`'s files, and prints the rollback commands.
  - §3.7.2 has a recovery row per step, plus "never revert a migration applied remotely" and the note that an API rollback across a `MATCH_RECORD_VERSION` change retires in-flight matches. §3.7.4's stale migration count is fixed.
- **Migration guard (OP10).** `scripts/migration-guard.ts` also fails on `ALTER TABLE … DROP` or `… RENAME` (a drizzle rebuild's `__new_` rename stays the rebuild check's) unless the file is in `CONTRACTS_REVIEWED` with the commit since which no code reads the old name. `0001_elite_jackpot.sql` is listed: it dropped the R2 url columns in the API's first deploy.
- **Ingest run decisions (OP2).** `mapBounded`, the rows-written tally, the epoch-skip rule, the exit status and the rerun command moved to `apps/api/src/ingest/run.ts` with tests. A failed apply now makes the run's count unknown (so it stamps the epoch): a remote import can commit and still exit non-zero. A failed run prints `bun scripts/ingest.ts --sets <failed> [--remote]`. Every wrangler call from `scripts/d1.ts` and the ingest is killed after five minutes.
- **tcgdex transport (OP6, decision 11).** Each request runs under a 15 s deadline; the fetch transport aborts it, and under the SDK the deadline only stops the wait. Failures are `SourceRequestError`s that say whether they may pass: network errors, timeouts, a 200 with no JSON body, and under fetch 408, 429 and 5xx. `createSource` wraps either transport in `withRetries`: up to 3 retries, honouring `Retry-After` (at most a minute), else full-jitter backoff of up to 1, 2 and 4 s, each attempt paced and counted. Tests use the recording sleep; `source.server.test.ts` runs the fetch transport against a local server (503 then 200, a stalled request cut at its deadline, a 404 not retried).
- **Legality guard (OP7).** A set in which a card stored as legal in some format arrives without `legal` is aborted, unless `--allow-legality-change`. Only cards lacking `legal` are looked up (none today). A card never stored passes, so a new set published before its legality still ingests. The audit's alternative, a budget on flipped flags, was not added: a yearly rotation flips whole sets on purpose.
- **Optional items.** `db:check -- --persist-to <dir>` checks a local copy (OP8). `escapeSqlText` refuses a lone UTF-16 surrogate, which the UTF-8 SQL file would turn into U+FFFD (OP12).

### 2026-10-28 — Engine safety nets: real-card fuzz, a wider shape pin, and version 41 retired

Phase 4 of the 2026-10-28 testing and reliability audit. Tests only, except retiring version 41, which changes what the engine migrates and ships in the API with the engine. The web client is unaffected: it never migrates a state.

- **Real cards in the fuzz (EN1, EN2).** `packages/engine/src/actionFuzzReal.test.ts` plays the four bench decks against each other (four pairings) on mulberry32 and on ChaCha20, to game over (at most 400 accepted steps; every game ends well before). Every accepted walk step, and every hostile wire action accepted at every eighth state, must pass `afterAccept` (log, both redactions, JSON-safe, every card kept, the generator kept), keep the board rules (no uid in two zones, at most six Prizes and the Bench ceiling, whole counters, no empty body, face-up Prizes among the Prizes, no turn going back, a game over whose condition holds), answer a Prize take or a prompt only with what it offered and as the seat it asked, and give the same state and events when applied to a JSON copy of its input (warm versus cold: the DO loads a fresh state per action, the simulator keeps one in memory). Each game then replays from a JSON copy of its start to the same end, and `createGame` is run twice on each seed. It asserts properties only, since an ingest can rewrite the pool, plus floors against a vacuous pass (some prompt answered, Prizes taken, a game over, hostile actions accepted). About 6 s alone, in its own file so it runs on its own worker. A seeded identity-memo fault (an extra event on the 50th action per card pool object) fails all eight runs.
  - The bench pool moved to `packages/engine/src/fixtures/benchPool.json` (`BENCH_POOL`, `benchDeck` from `@colorless/engine/testing`), so an engine suite can read it without a relative import across a workspace root; `bun run bench` reads the same file and its fingerprint is unchanged.
  - The hostile generators and `afterAccept` moved from `actionFuzz.test.ts` to `fixtures/fuzz.ts`, with the new board and prompt checks; `actionFuzz` sends the same actions as before.
  - The bench fingerprint stays manual (decision 13); it is not in `check`.
  - No engine bug was found.
- **Install records and parks in the shape pin (EN5, decision 12).** `persistShape.test.ts` pinned a fresh game, whose install fields are all null, and one parked op. Its skeleton now also takes fixed samples, through a JSON round trip as stored: a body and a side with every `clearedAttackEffects`/`clearedSideInstalls` field set by the op that installs it (`INSTALL_OPS`), and one program parked on each prompt kind, two of them after a first answer (Tyme's guess, a hand attach's card pick with its recorded target). Two cases fail when an install field has no op or a prompt kind has no park; a new prompt kind fails to compile. The snapshot only grew; `GAME_STATE_VERSION` stays 51. The audit's probe, a required key added to the `attackBlock` record, now fails the pin. A field that only an unsampled op writes is still not pinned; conventions.md says so.
- **Version 41 retired (EN7, decision 14).** Version 41 was the last version whose Trainer faces were refreshed from D1 on migration, and the only one that could hold a setup from before the bonus Bench step (added 2026-10-06, during 41). Its step is gone and 42 is the oldest migratable version.
  - A v41 match record: `migrateGameState` returns null, `loadMatchRecord` answers `stale`, and the DO retires the match as it retires any unreadable record (deleted, lobby back to selecting, `match-error` to both seats, `match_retired` logged). A clean refusal, never a throw; a DO case drives it. No v41 record should still be readable anyway: the 2026-10-27 seat-credential cutover wipes a lobby stored before it on its next upgrade or alarm.
  - A v42 record still migrates and plays on (a new DO case, and `matchLoad.test.ts` for every version 42 to 50).
  - A local simulator save carries no state version: it is refused whenever its `engineVersion` differs, and a save at this engine version was made by the current setup flow. `simulatorSave.ts` needed no change.
  - With no step needing catalog rows, the pool refresh is removed: `migrateGameState(raw, fromVersion)` takes no pool, `poolIdsNeededFor` and `migratablePoolIdsOf` are gone, and `loadMatchRecord(slot)` no longer reads D1 or re-reads the slot around that read. The DO's `loadPool` stays for match creation.
  - The legacy setup flow goes with it: `setup:place.stage` and `setup:drawExtra.bonusCards` are required, and `setupDrawExtra` and `finishSetup` no longer reserve missing Prizes (each seat's opening Ready does). `concede`'s own game-over guard, unreachable behind `dispatchAction`'s, is removed (the audit's dead line).
  - Neither `GAME_STATE_VERSION` nor the engine version moves: no shape a 51 deploy writes changed, and the bench fingerprint is identical, so no local save is invalidated. Releasing it: one release, API and web together as usual; a rollback to the previous deploy reads every record this one writes.
  - Versions 42 to 50 are equally unreachable since the cutover; they stay until a step needs retiring.
- **A real record at the next hand-written step (EN6).** Retiring 41 is not a bump and adds no step, so no record is captured now. conventions.md, where bumps are described, now asks the next hand-written step for one parked mid-turn record written by the pre-change engine from a bench-deck walk, migrated and played 20 steps with the `fixtures/fuzz.ts` checks, and deleted when its version retires.
- **Not done.** EN7's optional `coverage:engine` script: bun's coverage run has four bun-only failures on the engine suites, and an unenforced script that fails would only mislead.
- **Mutant rows.** Five rows on the removed pool-refresh code are dropped, five migration rows re-anchored on `migrateGameState`'s new signature, and one re-pointed from the deleted `poolMigration.test.ts` to `persist.test.ts`. `REL-EN7-v41-migrated-again` gives v41 a step back and is killed. Every row on a file this phase touches (190) was run: all killed.

### 2026-10-28 — The Lua frontend's browser smoke, named mailbox rows, and a page that says what happened

Phase 6, the last, of the 2026-10-28 testing and reliability audit. Web-only: `host.js`, the Lua UI and the TypeScript adapters ship together in the web bundle, so the mailbox change needs no slot kept for an older client. Nothing touches the API's runtime.

- **`bun run e2e` (LU2, LU10, user decision 15).** `scripts/e2e.ts` builds the Lua package and the site against its own `wrangler dev` on free ports, with a fresh D1 migrated under a temporary directory and seeded only with `@colorless/api/testing/fixtureCatalog`: the committed tcgdex payloads for Miraidon ex, Nest Ball and Fire Energy, mapped by the ingest's own functions, without images, and `FIXTURE_DECK`, a 60-card Expanded deck of them (`fixtureCatalog.d1.test.ts` keeps it legal in Expanded and refused in Standard). It never reads the dev D1. Each account is registered and its deck created through the API in its own browser context. Headless Chromium then boots the page and presses a key Lua announces, plays a sandbox game to turn 3, crashes Lua with a snapshot cut inside a card row and checks the new wording and the reload that resumes the turn, watches a third page whose game.data the static server never answers show the stall note and the menu links, and plays an online match from create through concede to a rematch both players ask for at once, then a new match id on both tabs. It reads mailboxes through `WIRE_ROWS` and drives Lua through bridge lines. Every process starts in its own process group and is stopped (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL) on success, failure or interrupt; a failed run keeps the temporary directory and prints it with the API log tail. `--gpu` also builds `effects_gpu.lua` and reads its `GPU PASS` headlessly (about 40 s more). `CHROMIUM` names the browser, else chromium is looked up on PATH, else the script stops with the Nix command to get one.
  - Opt-in: not in `check`, `release` or CI (CI cannot build game.data). §3.7.2 of `docs/workstreams/backend-data.md` names it as the recommended step before `bun run release`. About 70 s. The first 13 runs passed 9: in each failure the guest's Rematch reached its table source before Lua had opened the table (the route and the mailbox change first; a busy machine held the open back for seconds), a press no player can make. The smoke now waits for Lua's `simulator:open` on both tabs, after which 7 runs in a row passed (67-73 s each).
  - The audit suggested seeding sessions directly to stay clear of the auth limiter. With a fresh database per run the limiter never fills, so the smoke registers through the API, which costs two password hashes and keeps it on the real sign-up path.
- **Named online, sandbox and account rows (LU4).** `WIRE_ROWS` now declares `online` (header, `P` seat, `D` deck), `sandbox` (header, `D` deck) and `account` (header). The three adapters emit through `wireRow` and `online.lua`, `sandbox.lua` and `account.lua` read by field name; no column moved. `wireFixtures.test.ts` pins an online, a sandbox and an account snapshot that `wire_fixtures.lua` feeds to the real parsers, and the TypeScript tests index by `wireColumn`. The online header's `canKick` stays: the adapter still takes a `kick` command, though the Lua modal no longer offers it.
- **A crash says what happened (LU7, user decision 17).** A Lua error or abort after `COLORLESS:ready` now reads "Something went wrong. Reload to continue; a game in progress is saved." instead of "The menu could not start", which stays for a failure before ready. Nothing is reported off-device.
- **Boot watchdog (LU9).** 45 s without loading progress adds a `stalled` class: the status line and the menu links show and the bar stays; any progress, ready or a failure takes it down. Nothing is aborted. The e2e run checks it.
- **Snapshot fuzz (LU8).** `frontend/lua/tests/sim_fuzz.lua`: 200 seeded mutations (a row dropped or repeated, a field dropped, garbled or added) of the late-game, prompt and setup snapshots must parse and draw. Truncation is left out, and no row-length guard was added to `Simulator:sync`: the host writes each mailbox whole, and a truncated snapshot is the e2e run's crash trigger. It needs no Balatro source, so CI runs it.
- **QA extras.** The builder's landscape Retry button (after a failed save or load) is 18 px tall and 5 px below Clear instead of 2 px under it. The ~3 s "not found" during a slow first lobby socket is not fixed: its cause is client-side (`LobbyClient` starts the 4 s not-found window at mount), but a dead code and a slow first socket are the same silence to it, since the server refuses an unknown code's upgrade and the channel reports no connect outcome. Telling them apart needs a new channel-to-client signal, which is more than a small fix. (Fixed by the follow-up entry below, REL-QA4.)

### 2026-10-28 — Online lobby follow-ups: a faster sweep while two seats are held, and a definite "not found"

Two items from the reliability audit's browser QA (`tmp/rel/qa1/results.md` items 3 and 4), ids `REL-QA3` and `REL-QA4`.

- **The opponent of a half-open player is told within 20 s (REL-QA3).** The DO notices a socket whose keepalives stopped only by polling (`sweepSilentSockets`), since the runtime answers pings without waking it. The poll ran every 30 s and a socket counted silent after 15 s, so a half-open player showed as away up to 50 s later (sweep, silence and the 5 s grace), and QA's 30 s outage ended before the host saw anything.
  - `livenessSweepMs` (`apps/api/src/lobby/logic.ts`): every 5 s (`SEATED_LIVENESS_SWEEP_MS`) while both seats are held, the versus screen or a match, and every 30 s otherwise. A lone host has nobody to tell.
  - `SOCKET_SILENCE_MS` drops from 15 s to 10 s, the client's own silence limit (`channel.ts`): a client whose path died has already given the socket up for a new one by then. A hidden tab's timers still fire once a second; a frozen tab or a network switch loses the TCP connection whatever the limit.
  - Detection is now at most 5 + 10 + 5 = 20 s. The cost: a two-seat lobby wakes 12 times a minute (720 an hour) instead of twice, each wake an alarm run and an alarm write, and 5 s is under the runtime's ~10 s hibernation idle, so the lobby stays in memory, billed for duration, between moves. `INACTIVE_LOBBY_MS` bounds both per lobby. Answering pings in the handler instead (no auto-response) stays rejected: about 40 wakes a minute per socket.
  - Tests: `logic.test.ts` (the cadence and the 20 s budget), `lobbyDO.test.ts` (a silent guest mid-match is closed and the host's snapshot shows it away, forfeit armed, within 20 s; a lone host stays on 30 s), and the seam suite (a guest whose link goes half-open and stays down shows as away on the host's table within 20 s and is reseated when it returns; alarms fire every 30 s for a lone host and every 5 s with a guest). Mutant row `REL-QA3-seated-sweep-at-idle-cadence`.
- **"Not found" only on the server's word (REL-QA4).** The client read 4 s without an answer as "not found", and a slow first socket overran it: QA saw "Lobby not found" for about 3 s before the lobby appeared. A dead code and a slow socket were the same silence, because the DO refused an unknown code's upgrade with a 404, which a browser reports like any failed connection.
  - Server: a socket for a code that names no lobby (never created, expired, or a legacy lobby just wiped) is accepted and closed at once with `LOBBY_WS_NOT_FOUND` (4004, `packages/schema/src/lobby/protocol.ts`). It is accepted with the standard `accept()`, outside hibernation, so the lobby never counts or sweeps it, and nothing is stored or scheduled: a guessed code still costs one wake and one `ws` rate-limit token, as the 404 did. A malformed code is still a 404 from the Worker, which no client sends.
  - Client: the channel stops reconnecting on 4004, as on 4000, and the client shows "not found". Silence now stays "connecting" (the Lua "Joining lobby..." step) with the channel's backoff and 10 s connect timeout. A first connection with no answer in 20 s (`UNREACHABLE_MS`, a connect timeout plus a retry) ends on a new `unreachable` status and step, "Can't reach the lobby. Couldn't connect to lobby CODE. Check your connection, then try again.", with Back to online. A later answer still takes the client back. The mailbox's `step` field is a string, so `WIRE_ROWS` is unchanged; `online.lua` gains the info entry.
  - An HTTP existence check before the socket was the alternative. It costs a second request and DO wake per join, and still leaves the socket's own silence to interpret.
  - Old clients: the API ships first. A current web client takes the 4004 close as a dropped link, reconnects with its backoff, and its 4 s window still shows "not found", as with the 404 before. A new client against an older API (a rollback) shows "Can't reach the lobby" for a dead code after 20 s.
  - Tests: `lobbyDO.test.ts` (a never-created and a legacy code are answered 101 and closed with 4004; nothing is held, stored or scheduled), `channel.test.ts`, `lobbyClient.test.ts` and `online.test.ts` (silence stays connecting and joining, 4004 is not-found at once, 20 s of silence is unreachable, a late answer recovers), the seam suite (a dead code is not-found within 300 ms and opens no second socket; a 6 s first socket shows only joining then versus), `frontend/lua/tests/online.lua` (the step's text and fit), and `bun run e2e` (`/online/ZZZZ` shows not-found within 3 s through the real Worker). The harness gains `upgrade()`, which reports an accepted, closed-at-once or refused answer. Mutant rows `REL-QA4-*`.

### 2026-10-29 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 1: the deck pages speak, keyboard focus shows, and a loading deck is not "unavailable"

Phase 1 of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit (report `tmp/ui/ui-audit.md`, gitignored). Web-only: Lua in game.data, `src/host/shell.ts` and the builder, library and sandbox adapters ship together in the web bundle. Nothing touches the API. User decisions taken with the report: button looks and button text do not change (no focus ring, no label recolouring; LC5 dropped), the canvas will render at the full device pixel ratio rather than upscale (BT6, Phase 6), and frames cap at 60 fps with a Settings option for 144 (LC1, Phase 5). Every other recommendation in the report's section 6 was accepted. Mutant ids are `UI-<finding>-*`.

- **A deck that is loading is not "unavailable" (UI-ST1).** `LuaBuilder.load()` emitted phase=ready before `hydrate()` fetched the card briefs, so every entry showed for one round trip as "Unavailable card", with a red verdict and a working `x` whose `discard` deleted the entry and autosaved. Entries now publish as a new wire row kind `builder.pending` (tag `L`: cardId, name, quantity), named "Loading card...", with the validation line "Loading cards...". Lua draws them without the discard control and refuses discard and remove for them; `LuaBuilder` refuses both too. "Unavailable card" stays for ids GET /cards answered without and for a failed request (with the retry message, which a successful retry now clears). Before the first ready emit, `hydrate()` fills cards the page already holds: the library's `cardPool` fetches (`cachedCard`, a peek that never fetches) and an already-loaded card index (`indexedCard`, which never starts a load); only the rest go to `/cards?id=`, so a complete deck opened from the library shows no placeholder. Ready does not wait for the request, which would delay the whole deck view. An add or export made while entries load waits for them instead of being refused or writing a partial decklist; with nothing loading both stay synchronous, so an add and a remove in one frame keep their order.
- **Keyboard focus shows, with each control's own hover look (UI-PG3, reduced by the user's decision).** `Button.draw` still ignores `focused`. Controls that only took hover (Settings sliders, toggles and fields, the builder's HP sliders and fields, the online code field, the library and sandbox search fields) now take "hovered or focused" through the same parameter, and call sites that passed `false` pass the real focus. `Controls.lit(hover, focus, i)` lights the control under the pointer, else the focused one, so two controls never look active at once (home, Settings, library, builder, sandbox, online, the table and its overlay). A key press parks the pointer at -1000,-1000 until the next real move, so keyboard takes the highlight over. A keyboard close of Settings returns focus to its opener (Settings or Account); a pointer close leaves none. Known gap: the disabled Email field and a disabled Save on the Account tab are Tab stops with no visible focus, because disabled controls have no hover look.
- **Hover does not stick (UI-LCX2).** The pointer leaving the canvas, a lifted finger and a touch-made release park the pointer; a drag keeps it live and parks on release off the canvas.
- **Readable page errors (UI-PG10).** Red on the grey tray measured 2.11:1. `Button.errorLine` draws the same red text on a `T.ink` pill (about 5:1) for the library page and dialog errors, the two online messages and the sandbox hint. Settings messages are paper on the tray (5.27:1) and unchanged.
- **A focused card keeps its face (UI-QA1).** `Card:shaderValues` tilted any hovered card toward the pointer, so a card active through keyboard focus with the pointer far away drew as a blank skewed slab. `Card:update` takes `focused`; a focus-only card keeps its ambient tilt (builder grid and deck view, the table's focused card, overlay cards).
- **Every deck-page control says what it is (UI-PG1).** `Builder:describe`, `Library:describe` and `Sandbox:describe` follow Online's and Settings': "Deck name: Fire Deck. Enter to edit.", "Charcadet, SV04 31, 3 in deck" (the set from the id prefix, so two prints differ), "Remove one Charcadet, 3 in deck", "Category: All, left and right change it", "Deck 1, selected for Player 1, 60 cards". Pager labels ("Previous page, page 1 of 3") and the one-page skip live in `ui/pager.lua`. A one-page pager is skipped through a `skipFocus` flag read by `Controls.unfocusable` rather than marked disabled, because `disabled` repaints it in `T.panel`; it still draws and answers the pointer as before. Each builder filter is one Tab stop (its `>` arrow) that Left/Right step, as in Settings and Online; before, Left/Right moved focus off it. The builder's names and searches edit in a one-line `<input>` labelled from `BUILDER_FIELD_LABEL` (spellcheck and auto-capitals off, enterkeyhint done or search, inputmode search); only the decklist keeps the textarea.
- **Builder dialogs and changes are spoken (UI-PGX1).** Each dialog announces what it draws when it opens; the card dialog re-reads once its rules text arrives, never the "Loading card details..." placeholder. A sync announces only a changed option, format, sort, page or quantity ("4 Charcadet in deck"; "N cards in deck" for an import or clear), never during a drag.
- **Pages say where you are and what happened (UI-PG2).** `applyPage` announces "Home"; the builder, library and sandbox announce themselves and their state on open and on each phase change ("Deck library, 14 decks.", "Deck library. Could not load your decks. Check your connection, then try again."), and their messages (rename validation, library full, save failed, session expired, "No matches", "Nothing here yet", "No valid decks"), de-duplicated against the last spoken unless the same refusal is triggered again. A session check that cannot reach the server now sets "Check your connection, then try again." instead of an empty sub-line, and the library and sandbox load failures use the same text.
- **Tests.** `frontend/lua/tests/focus_visible.lua` and `focus_main.lua` (focus on every page, one highlight, pointer parking, the Settings focus return, focus-only tilt), `announce.lua` (every focusable control on builder, library, sandbox and online in both layouts says at least a word, Tab reaches what it should, one-page skip, dialog, sync and page announcements), `builder.lua` (loading rows), `builder.test.ts` (loading entries, local fill, failed hydrate, add and export while loading), `hostShell.test.ts` (the builder proxy), `library.test.ts`, and the `builder_pending_snapshot.txt` wire fixture. `scripts/test-lua.ts` now accepts test file names, so a mutant row can name one Lua file as its killer. A throwaway call-log comparison over 802 hover, focus and modal states showed the builder, library and sandbox draw pixel-identical graphics calls before and after the speech work. Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa1/results.md`, Chromium and Firefox 157) found the card dialog never read the rules text of a card with no simulation gaps (`gapLine` returned nil inside an `ipairs` list); fixed with a red-first `announce.lua` case. 81 mutant rows (UI-ST1 13, UI-PG3 17, UI-QA1 4, UI-LCX2 5, UI-PG10 5, UI-PG1 18, UI-PGX1 9, UI-PG2 10), all killed.

### 2026-10-29 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 2: the table speaks, plays by keyboard, and holds online buttons while sending

Phase 2 of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit. Web-only: Lua in game.data, the simulator and online-table adapters and `src/host/shell.ts` ship together in the web bundle; nothing touches the API. User decisions: the card tray shows a one-line instruction and pile trays a title (decision 9); an online send only disables controls, and a dropped card is not parked where released (decision 10, keeping the no-optimistic-updates online design); button looks and text styles are unchanged (a new Retreat row uses the attack-row style).

- **What changed is announced (UI-SM1).** The simulator header gains `announce`, `messageSerial` and `warning`. `announcement()` (`simulatorRows.ts`) builds the screen-reader line from what changed since the last emit: log rows after their player's name (newest 6), every Knock Out ("Knocked out: X"), Prizes from the change in each seat's prize count ("+N Prizes, M left" / "<name> took N Prizes, M left"), the game's end and why (in place of the log's win row), a changed title, and a newly opened prompt's title, note and effect hint, keyed on kind, title and note so picks do not re-read it; coin rows give way to the coin prompt. Lua speaks it when new, at parse time so a Knock Out presentation cannot hold it back, and no longer re-reads the static title on every snapshot. A Prize take is also read from its own log rows: dropping the duplicate would mean reading rendered log text or a new wire cue.
- **One frame, one message.** Lua prints a frame's lines in one task, so the snapshot's line and a tray opening in the same frame replaced each other in the live region. `HostShell.announce` joins a frame's distinct announcements (flushed in a microtask after the frame's prints) and passes the result through `nextAnnouncement` unchanged.
- **A refusal pops once (UI-SM4).** Every refusal goes through `refuse()`, which moves `messageSerial`; Lua pops the banner, sound and jiggle, and speaks, once per serial. A standing warning (save failed, seat lost) rides `warning`, drawn on the turn panel, the phone hint line and the game-over panel without a pop, popped once when it first appears unless a refusal is showing.
- **Waiting on the opponent (UI-SM6).** Online, a choice the opponent makes outside their own turn titles the header "Waiting for <them>", the turn panel reads "Waiting" in `effect:choose`, and the desktop turn panel draws the hint whenever no targeting prompt shows (it was empty).
- **Sends in flight (UI-SM3).** A player send sets the online `pending` lifecycle flag (L row), cleared by the next frame, a refusal, or the reconnect frame after the LB3 half-open watchdog; automatic steps never set it. `LuaSimulator` ignores dispatch and invoke while it is set; Lua disables the panel and attached buttons (existing disabled look) and shows "Sending..." in the hint slot after 1 s.
- **Focus survives snapshots (UI-SMX1).** The table keeps keyboard focus by key (a control's action, label and owner; a public card's uid; a hidden card's owner and id; a Prize slot), so snapshots, list changes and the hot-seat flip no longer move or drop it. A vanished attached button falls back to its card, announced.
- **Keyboard play (UI-SM2).** The viewer's Active gains a Retreat K action (Colorless pips or "Free", greyed when the engine's `retreat.can` is false) whose token opens the existing Bench pick through an optional `ActionHost.retreat`, so it rides the generic attached-button path with its revision. The wire carries no refusal reason, so Lua reads one off what the table shows (already retreated, Asleep or Paralyzed, no Bench, too little Energy, else "an effect prevents it"). Prize prompts add the face-down Prizes to the ring ("Prize 3 of 6"); Enter on a card focuses its first enabled button and reads the unavailable ones, Escape returns to the card; V inspects the selected or focused card and the canvas label says so; the hover popup follows keyboard focus.
- **Trays, inspector and log speak (UI-SMX2).** `ui/sim_speech.lua` builds focused-card summaries ("Quaxly, opponent's Active, 60 of 60 HP, 1 Water Energy, Asleep"), the inspector paragraph (stage, type, HP, attacks with cost and damage, ability, weakness, retreat) and tray picks ("Fuecoco, selected, 1 of 2"). The Game log reads its newest entries on open, and Up and Down read the rest ("Entry 2 of 3: ..."). Its `<` could never page back, because every build forced the last page; fixed.
- **Tray captions (UI-SM5, UI-SM11).** An untitled card tray keeps its compact grid with one jokerGrey line above the cards (the prompt's note, else during an effect prompt the header hint or the played card), widened to fit, and announced on open. Pile trays are titled "Your discard (12)" / "Misty's discard (12)", an empty discard pile is disabled, and piles are Tab stops.
- **Reduced Motion (UI-SM7).** A static "KNOCKED OUT: <name>" marks each victim (visual only; the TS line announces it), and coins land at once with no lift or spin (`Overlay.coinPose`).
- **Tests.** `frontend/lua/tests/sim_keyboard.lua`, `sim_table_speech.lua`, `announcement()` unit cases and a re-emit sweep that asserts a re-emit announces nothing (`simulatorRows.test.ts`), hot-seat KO, last-Prize, refusal-serial and failing-save cases (`simulator.test.ts`), double click, next frame, refusal and automatic-step cases (`onlineTable.test.ts`), a half-open-link seam case, Retreat and label cases, and the one-frame join (`hostShell.test.ts`). 69 mutant rows (UI-SM1 13 + 1 for the join, UI-SM4 7, UI-SM6 3, UI-SM3 10, UI-SMX1 4, UI-SM2 13, UI-SMX2 7, UI-SM5 4, UI-SM11 4, UI-SM7 3), all killed.
- **Browser QA fixes (UI-QA2).** Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa2/results.md`, Chromium and Firefox 157; the fixture deck cannot reach a Prize choice, a coin or the online "Waiting for" header, so those rest on unit and Lua tests) found seven speech and placement bugs, fixed red-first. A log page turn moves the log's reading cursor to the page shown, and reading onto another page rebuilds, so the pager follows it. The Reduced Motion Knock Out notice is placed by the victim's owner and zone in the new layout, mirrored after a hot-seat flip (it sat on the attacker). `announcement()` keeps the batch's first action and every damage row besides Knock Outs, and only other rows count against the cap of 6; "Knocked out: X" says whose ("your X", "Brock's X"). `runCardAction` rebuilds quietly, so a button no longer reads its card's pre-action summary. `joinAnnouncements` ends each joined part as a sentence. Card and pile trays and the log say their own opening words in Lua, and the TS line no longer repeats them. A card button's target pick announces its question ("Attach to which Pokémon? Click a highlighted card."). `refuse()` drops the engine's "(§N)" rulebook references from user-facing refusals. The e2e watchdog step now mutes only incoming frames: muted both ways, the server's two-seat silence sweep could close the socket before the page's watchdog did, which made the step flaky. 18 mutant rows, all killed; four Phase 2 rows re-anchored. Left as observations: the tray confirm reads only "2/2", "{L}" tokens and "->" are read literally, closing an overlay says nothing, the hot-seat setup hand-over is not spoken, the keyboard popup can overlap the refusal banner, and online setup offers Place Active on an Energy card (the server refuses it).

### 2026-10-29 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 3: the host page checks, counts and compresses its boot, and the canvas follows the page's state

Phase 3 of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit. Web-only: `index.html`, `frontend/lua/web/host.js`, the game package, `src/host/shell.ts` / `router.ts` and `public/` ship together; nothing touches the API. User decisions: Tab past home's last button leaves the canvas (decision 1, option a); the canvas is `role="application"` (decision 2); crawlers index `/` only, with a share image from the wordmark (decision 13).

- **Capability check (UI-BT4).** Without WebAssembly nothing downloads. WebGL is probed on a throwaway canvas right after the love.wasm request goes out (the first context can take about 0.5 s, which would otherwise delay every boot), and the request is cancelled when there is no context. SDL's alert and its OpenGL error line before ready, a throwing start-up and a love.wasm that will not download all show a specific failure ("Colorless needs WebGL and WebAssembly. Turn on hardware acceleration or try another browser."); the first failure's message stays.
- **One boot bar (UI-BT2, UI-BT3).** love.wasm and the package are counted through a TransformStream (the streaming compile stays), each weighed by sizes `build.mjs` bakes into host.js: downloads fill 90%, the unpacked package 95%, Lua's ready 100%, and every chunk re-arms the 45 s stall watch. The live region gets milestones only (25/50/75%, "Starting…", stall, failure); `aria-valuenow` moves on whole percents.
- **Compressed package (UI-BT1).** Workers static assets give `.data` no Content-Type, so Cloudflare sent game.data uncompressed (confirmed on production). The build also writes `game.data.gz` (1.52 MB to 0.998 MB, sent as `application/gzip`) and patches game.js's `fetchRemotePackage` to hand the download to host.js (`Module.fetchPackage`), which unpacks it with DecompressionStream, accepts a body the server already decoded, and falls back to the plain `game.data`. The build throws if love.js's loader changes shape. bench:boot cold ready: 2375 to 2049 ms at 20 Mbps, 6180 to 5300 ms at 5 Mbps.
- **Ready after the first frame (UI-QA12).** main.lua prints ready from the first `love.update` after a `love.draw`, so no black frame shows between the bar and the menu.
- **Keys the browser keeps (UI-BT5, UI-LC3).** A window capture listener in host.js keeps F5, F6, F11, F12, Ctrl/Cmd+R, =, +, −, 0, L, F, Alt+Left/Right and Ctrl+wheel from SDL without preventing them (Lua binds none), and gives Tab to the browser whenever the running canvas does not have focus; focused text proxies keep their own key handling.
- **Tab leaves home (UI-LC3, decision 1a).** main.lua prints `COLORLESS:focus-edge:first|last|none` as home's ring moves (none under a popup, during a wipe, or off home). A capture-phase listener in `shell.ts` keeps that one Tab (at last) or Shift+Tab (at first) from SDL with `stopImmediatePropagation`, never off home, under Settings or a wipe, or while a text proxy edits. Coming back, the canvas resumes at the button it left. README, `#instructions` and the shell comment now say so.
- **Degraded state (UI-BR1).** `ColorlessBridge.onFailed()` became `onDegraded("stalled" | "recovered" | "failed")`, replayed to a bridge that connects late. HostShell derives the canvas's role, `aria-busy`, tab stop, name and `#instructions`, and the nav's `inert`, from one state, writing only what changed: before ready the canvas is busy and "Colorless is loading"; while stalled or failed it leaves the Tab order, a failed canvas gives up focus, and the links (plus a Home link on deep links) do real page loads. The overlay gets a native Reload button, shown without focus on a stall and focused on a failure; the failure text is written assertively once the overlay is back in the accessibility tree; Reload after a boot failure clears `EM_PRELOAD_CACHE`; lost graphics in a background tab reload once the tab is shown (at most once a minute).
- **Canvas semantics (UI-BR4, UI-BR3).** The canvas is `role="application"` with `aria-roledescription="game"`. Dialog and `aria-modal` are dropped rather than moved to a wrapper: the nav's `inert` already confines focus, and `aria-modal` could hide the `#announcement` live region outside it. `pageLabel` gives short, stable names ("Deck builder: <name>", "Deck builder: loading the deck"; no card count), `pageInstructions` describes each page, Settings and the sign-in form, and a visually hidden `<h1 id="page-heading">` names the page. "Colorless is ready" is said once, and `#announcement` clears 5 s after each message (the no-break-space repeat and per-frame joining stay).
- **Previews, crawlers, no-JS (UI-BT11, decision 13).** Static og and twitter tags, `og:url` and `og:image` made absolute at build from `VITE_SITE_ORIGIN`; `public/share.png` (1200x630, the wordmark on ink), `apple-touch-icon.png`, `favicon.ico`; `robots.txt` allows `/` and disallows `/decks/`, `/online/`, `/sandbox/play`; a `<noscript>` message (`noscript.css`) that shows the links. The deploy skips `love/versions.json` and `love/asset-manifest.json`, which nothing fetches. No Worker script.
- **Tests.** `scripts/hostScript.test.ts` (host.js in a `node:vm` fake page, 39 cases), `hostShell.test.ts` and `router.test.ts` cases, `frontend/lua/tests/boot_ready.lua` and `focus_edge.lua`, e2e steps for the Tab out and a stalled deep link (live links, Reload button); 57 mutant rows (UI-BT1 to UI-BT5, UI-LC3, UI-BR1, UI-BR3, UI-BR4, UI-QA12), all killed. Not verified here: a manual NVDA pass of the application role.
- **Browser QA fixes (UI-QA3).** Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa3/results.md`, Chromium 154 and Firefox 157, production build) passed every check and found three small bugs, fixed red-first. "Colorless is ready" waits up to `READY_JOIN_MS` (400 ms) for the page's first line and is said with it, instead of overwriting it or being overwritten 12-200 ms later. The focus-edge print carries the edge button's label, and a keyboard return to the canvas after Tab left it says that button (a click back in says nothing, since Lua drops focus on a click). The no-JS page shows Home on every route. A gzip package download that fails because the page is being left (`pagehide`) no longer starts the plain fallback.

### 2026-10-30 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 4: data in flight keeps what is on screen, and the Sandbox button no longer waits on the engine

Phase 4 of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit. Web-only: the TS adapters, Lua screens and a new `src/decks/deckStore.ts` ship together in the web bundle; nothing touches the API. User decisions: the engine is prefetched on intent only (decision 14); buttons keep their look and text, so no pending look on the clicked menu button, no busy relabel of Play, Back is not relabelled while a save flushes, and the builder's Retry save / Retry loading buttons keep their place, size and text.

- **Sandbox without the engine (UI-ST2).** `hasSimulatorSave()` (`src/game/simulatorSaveKey.ts`, which imports nothing from the engine) reads only the save key and version, so the eager bundle answers `simulator:resume`: no save routes straight to the picker, a save goes through the LazyScreen as before. Fast 3G click to chooser about 3.1 s to about 1.4 s, with no engine chunk. `LazyScreen.warm()` fetches the table chunks fire-and-forget once the sandbox picker has its decks and when an online lobby reaches countdown. A chunk failure while `navigator.onLine` is false no longer reloads; a new `notice` mailbox lets Lua draw and say "You're offline. Check your connection, then try again." under the home menu.
- **Background session checks (UI-ST4, UI-STX1).** Boot and Retry checks show busy; navigation and Settings checks of a known session set neither busy nor a message and publish only when status or user changed, and one that cannot reach the server keeps the known session. Sign out, save and delete supersede a background check instead of being refused. `refresh()` returns the check in flight and recovery (`LuaAccount.recover`) awaits it, so an expired session no longer sends its request twice. `LuaOnline.session` publishes only on a new status or user.
- **One deck list (UI-ST3, UI-ST5).** `deckStore` holds one GET /decks in flight, the list with a revision and verdicts judged once per revision; screens show the cached list at once and revalidate, the first fetch starts when the account resolves signed in, and a fetch from before a write never overwrites it. The sandbox and online pickers keep their decks while revalidating (no busy flag: in the sandbox picker busy greys every control and relabels Play) and say loading only with nothing to show. Library create, rename and delete apply the server's answer to the store at once and reconcile in the background; each builder save's PATCH answer goes into the store too.
- **Online during an outage (UI-PG13).** Account `error` stays its own state, so online offers no Sign in prompt or button; seated lobby steps announce "Connection lost, reconnecting." and "Reconnected.".
- **Builder searches keep their results (UI-PG5).** API-backed changes (category and pip filters, sort, format, size) keep the results on screen; after 150 ms they draw with the existing greyed card look and the pager says "Searching...". They are replaced when the page lands and cleared only on failure or no match; greyed results still open and drag. Index-served searches still publish once.
- **Save caption (UI-PG6).** Beside the deck name (narrowed by 138 px for it; on portrait's catalog tab at the end of the message line): "Saving..." (also for pending edits), "Saved" for 2.2 s, "Retrying..." (the deck saver's new `retrying` status) and "Not saved" in red. The existing Retry save button is unchanged and still the way to retry. A failure and its recovery ("Deck saved.") are each announced once.
- **Portrait builder (UI-PG4).** Messages draw on a reserved line above the catalog pager (hidden while portrait's full-width Retry button covers it) and under the deck tab's Import / Clear row; the portrait deck panel moved down 19 px for it. Portrait sorts from an eighth cycle in the Filters dialog's Cards tab that reuses the filter cycle (`sort` accepts ±1); landscape keeps its Sort button.
- **Card details and catalog options (UI-ST6, UI-ST8).** Details publish the rules text when `GET /cards/:id` answers and add the gap row when the engine (warmed on the first detail) arrives: first detail text 926 to 182 ms on a throttled link. Full Cards go through cardPool's per-id cache (`fetchCard`). Facets, sets and series are memoised module-wide for an hour like the card index, forgotten on failure, started alongside `GET /decks/:id` (options shown 403 to 233 ms) and reused by decklist import. Text fields remember the font size their value fits, keeping the builder's per-frame measurement budget.
- **Tests.** Adapter suites, `deckStore.test.ts`, Lua `host_notice.lua`, `online.lua`, `builder_status.lua`; 83 new UI- mutant rows, and `bun run mutants --decision UI-` kills all 314.
- **Browser QA fixes (UI-QA4).** Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa4/results.md`, Chromium and Firefox, production builds of `ui` and `main`) passed every check, with button pixels identical to main apart from the portrait deck tab's row of filter and −/+ buttons, which sit 15 px lower with the panel. It found three small bugs, fixed red-first. A picker whose deck check failed stays on its error: the held list's late judgement no longer shows decks under it (sandbox and online). The builder's "…Your edits are kept. Retry." goes once the save is tried again, as the caption then says Retrying... with no Retry button shown. The page notice is never under 12 device pixels, and on a home screen whose gap under the menu cannot hold it that large (a 667x375 phone) it shows above the logo. Five UI-QA4 mutant rows; the sweep kills 319.

### 2026-10-30 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 5: a 60 fps cap, still frames skipped, and less work per frame and per turn

Phase 5 of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit. Web-only: Lua, the TS adapters and the schema package's card index ship in the web bundle; nothing touches the API. User decisions: cap at 60 fps with a Settings option for 144 (decision 8); skip identical frames under reduced motion and on static home/Settings, never throttle the animated vortex; buttons keep their look, text, size and place.

- **Frame cap (UI-LC1).** `love.run` is LÖVE 11.4's default loop plus `ui/frame_pacer.lua`, which runs update and draw at most 60 (or 144) times a second on any display while events are pumped every animation frame. The default loop's `love.timer.sleep(0.001)` is dropped: love.js busy-waits it on the main thread. Motion stays dt-based; a 60 fps update on a 144 Hz display moves a card within 0.24 tiles of a steady 60 Hz one (LC11).
- **Max Frame Rate.** A new cycle in Settings' Graphics tab (60 FPS / 144 FPS), the CRT Bloom control's kind, in the free row under it; existing controls unchanged. The new preference key is appended as a ninth value, and eight-value records still load with it defaulted.
- **Redraw gate (UI-LC1).** Home and its Settings under reduced motion skip draw and present while nothing changes (`still()`, `Settings:still()`). Any event, mailbox write, layout, page or popup change, notice, click flash or wipe draws again; the first still frame is drawn, then once a second as a safety net. Other pages and motion-on always draw. Chromium and Firefox keep the last frame through a frame with no GL calls. Intel HD 630: reduced-motion home 20% to 3-6% main thread, 66% to 9-21% browser CPU; Settings 25% to 3%; SwiftShader 378% to about 60% CPU. Every screen still drawing at 60 is cheaper too (builder 117% to 35% browser CPU, table 94% to 28%), mostly from the dropped sleep.
- **Per-frame allocations (UI-LC7).** The CRT pass refills its tables and sends its ten static uniforms only on a layout, CRT, bloom or reduced-motion change; constant popup, text-field and Settings colours are created once. Idle-frame garbage: home 2.6 to 0.01 KB, Settings 3.1 to 0.38 KB.
- **Lazy shaders (UI-LC8).** `love.load` compiles splash and CRT; background, dissolve and played compile one per update after ready, or at once for a deep-linked table or collection page and for a wipe, and a shader read before it compiled compiles on the spot. Warm-boot ready time is within noise on a real GPU.
- **Hot-seat flips keep card faces (UI-SM8).** A flip re-created every card (13-19 canvases, 3-6 scan decodes, 3.9-10.4 ms native per End turn), and the evolution heuristic misfired on the mirrored table and released a canvas twice. Public cards keep their uid across a flip, so `placeCards` finds them under the mirrored owner; each becomes a new card on its new side (no slide, no ghost) that adopts the canvas it already painted (`Card.new(item, index, class, texture)`) when its card and face-up state are unchanged. Hidden cards are positional and always repainted. The evolution heuristic is skipped on a flip and the takeover no longer releases before `rebuild`. Per flip: 2-7 canvases, 2-5 decodes, 1.3-6.4 ms; double releases per game 72-126 to 0.
- **The card index loads when idle, a set at a time (UI-ST7).** Fetching, parsing and preparing the 1.9 MB index ran as one task right after the builder's first open (64 ms; 142-207 ms at 4x CPU). It now starts in the first idle period after the first search page shows (`whenIdle` in `src/lib/idle.ts`: `requestIdleCallback` with a 2 s deadline, else a 200 ms timer for Safari), and `prepareCardIndex` runs `cardIndexSteps` (a generator yielding after each set and after the name order) in slices of about 8 ms. The longest index task is now the JSON parse (22 ms; about 69 ms at 4x). No Worker: handing a parsed index back costs about as much as parsing it, and the builder's own first Lua frames dominate the open. A free-text search made before the idle load starts the same memoised load and waits for it, so the index is still loaded before the first free-text search.
- **Menu music only when heard (UI-BT8).** Lua asks for `music1.ogg` (`COLORLESS:music:want`) once at start when the stored preferences say it would be heard, and again when the player unmutes or turns a volume up. The host downloads a start ask after ready as before (decision 11: keep "after ready") and skips it under Save-Data; a change ask downloads at once whatever the connection, since the player asked for music. Muted players and players at volume or music volume 0 no longer download 2.9 MB. Quality unchanged.
- **Transition scans fetched after ready (UI-BT9).** The eight fallback scans leave `game.data` (gzip copy 1.00 to 0.51 MB) and ship as `/love/transition_cards/*.png?v=<hash>` (immutable, listed in `versions.json`); the host writes them into `/assets/transition_cards` after ready (decision 12: deferred fetch). The fallback lists only scans already written; until one arrives, or if none decodes, the wipe's card stays face down and dissolves, so the first wipe on a slow cold visit never waits or errors. The 317 KB of effect sounds stay in the package.
- **Tests.** Lua `frame_pacer.lua`, `frame_loop.lua`, `settings_frame_rate.lua`, `screen_uniforms.lua`, `shader_warmup.lua`, a flip case and an evolution takeover in `frame_budget.lua`; `music_want.lua`, cold cases in `transition_art.lua` and `screen_wipe.lua`; `idle.test.ts`, `cardIndex.test.ts`, catalog, builder and `hostScript.test.ts` cases. 65 new UI- mutant rows (UI-QA12's ready row and REL-AP7's index-version row re-anchored).
- **Browser QA fixes (UI-QA5).** Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa5/results.md`, Chromium and Firefox, production builds of `ui` and `main`) passed all ten checks: no frozen or late frame behind the redraw gate (every interaction drew within one frame in Chromium, 50 ms in Firefox), old preference records load, hot-seat flips match a fresh paint, buttons unchanged against main apart from the new Max Frame Rate control. One bug, fixed red-first: under the 144 cap a steady 165 or 170 Hz display ran every frame (about 160 a second), because the pacer's quarter-interval early carry let any display up to 1.2x the cap run every frame. The early carry is 0.45 now, which narrows that to about 1.03x; 165 Hz under 144 runs about 141 a second, and displays at the cap still run every frame through jitter. `frame_pacer.lua` checks steady 165, 170, 72 and 75 Hz beats. The 3x17 px difference on the sandbox chooser's Back label is not Phase 5: it appears only after a long scripted session and main shows the same 1 px text-edge flicker.


### 2026-10-30 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 6: full-density rendering, an Interface Size setting, phone legibility and text fields at their place

Phase 6 of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit. Web-only: Lua, the host page and the TS shell; nothing touches the API. User decisions: render at the full device pixel ratio with no upscale and no cap (decision 5); an in-app Interface Size setting (decision 6); short landscape phones get (a) CRT mitigation and (b) the cheaper fallbacks, not a new layout (decision 7); text proxies at their fields; buttons keep their look, text, size and place, so every minimum-target item is an invisible hit area around the unchanged face. LC5 (label colours) was dropped by that rule.

- **Reduced motion lives in the primitives (UI-LC6).** `Button.chosen` takes a `reduced` flag and holds the marker at its rest offset (bob 0). Under reduced motion DynaText skips pop-in (letters whole, pop-in counted as done) and pop-out (letters stay whole while attention texts fade by transparency). Fx keeps the flag: under reduced motion it draws the attention backdrop at the size and tilt motion on reaches when the text's hold ends (the largest pose motion-on players see, so reduced motion shows no new look) and holds it through the fade, with no growth or spin. Callers pass the real time and the flag: Settings, online and builder lose their frozen marker times, the sandbox tab marker stops bobbing, and the online and HUD pop-in workarounds go. The online countdown now shows its backdrop under reduced motion like every other attention text, at the same held pose. Settings alone keeps its caret steady under reduced motion, since main.lua's redraw gate skips its frames. With motion off, every draw call is identical to before (601,462-line draw log over 91 frames, byte-identical). 15 UI-LC6 mutant rows.
- **Full-DPR canvas (UI-BT6; decision 5: render fully, no cap).** The host sends `w h reduced pw ph` (device size = CSS x `devicePixelRatio`) on boot, resize, zoom and a resolution media-query change; `ui/viewport.lua` parses it and Lua renders at the device size with the same virtual layout. DPR 1 keeps the resizable window and is pixel-identical (15 page/viewport shots). A denser canvas is sized only by Lua; one beyond the GPU texture limit renders at the largest size that fits.
- **Same look in CSS pixels.** Scanlines come from the CSS height; `Screen.source` patches `CRT.fs` at load so the RGB fringe and the vertex centre distance divide by `pixel_density` (Balatro's values at DPR 1); pointer mapping mirrors the patch. The notice's minimum is 12 CSS px. Clicks, touch and a table drag map identically at DPR 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2 and 3 (Chromium) and in Firefox.
- **Fonts and card faces keep their virtual-size rasters** with nearest filtering, so text is drawn as crisp blocks of today's glyph shapes: rasterizing m6x11plus at device size changes glyph advances (up to 7.5% at 22 px) and so every label's width, which the button rule forbids. The logo (836 px, smooth filtering) and the 272x374 card art set the remaining sharpness limit.
- **Safe area (UI-BT7).** `index.html` drops `viewport-fit=cover`; the browser keeps the canvas inside the safe area.
- **Cost** (Intel HD 630): CPU per frame unchanged; 60 fps up to about 4 MP (2x laptop, 3x phone); 8-9 MP (4K at 150%, 1280x800 at 3x) runs 31-37 fps on motion-on home and the table. Reduced-motion home still draws once a second. 15 UI-BT6/BT7 mutant rows.
- **Interface Size (UI-LQX2; decision 6).** Settings' Game tab gains an Interface Size cycle (100/125/150%, the CRT Bloom control's kind) in the free row under Reduced Motion; a tenth positional preference value, 8- and 9-value records load with every setting kept. Each layer lays out in the virtual canvas divided by the largest step its content fits and draws that much larger over the same screen area (`ui/interface_size.lua`, `ui/display.lua`): home in landscape (a compact arrangement down to 533 tall), Settings up to 125% on 16:10, the decks/builder/sandbox panel without its 0.9 inset (111%). The table, online lobby and wipe fill the canvas and have no smaller arrangement, so they stay at 100% (compact layouts were out of scope); portrait layers stay at 100%. 100% takes the old code paths (pixel parity).
- **Short landscape (UI-LC4; decision 7 (a)+(b)).** (a) On layouts under 0.6 CSS px per virtual px, scanlines follow the layout height so they no longer cut glyph rows. (b) The portrait fallback was measured and rejected: both layouts are height-bound on a short landscape phone and portrait draws about 6% smaller. Instead a touch screen (new coarse-pointer viewport field) held sideways under 0.6 shows "Turn your phone upright / for a larger table" in the table HUD's top band, and below 0.7 the table's small HUD and attached buttons get invisible hit areas up to 44 CSS px (24 on a phone held upright), never over a card or another control. (c) not done.
- **Phone portrait text (UI-QA11).** The portrait prompt stops shrinking at 0.24 and moves an overflowing note into a strip under the HUD (two lines, more rather than under 11 CSS px); portrait HUD labels and pile card counts are at least 11 CSS px on phones. Button labels unchanged.
- **Small targets (UI-PG8).** `Button.hit` tests an optional invisible `hitRect` (`ui/hit_area.lua`): about 34x34 virtual, 24 CSS px where the screen is small, grown around the unchanged face and stopping at neighbours. Applied to Manage, deck-row -/+ and the builder's Retry; Retry reaches 26 virtual (23.4 CSS) between Clear and Back, since reaching 24 CSS would mean moving buttons. 32 new UI-LQX2/LC4/QA11/PG8 mutant rows.
- **Text proxies at their fields (UI-BR6).** Lua's `input` and `edit` prints carry the edited field's box after their own fields (whole CSS pixels from the canvas's top-left, through love.draw's offset and scale, Settings' Interface Size layer and the collection panel's transform, over the device pixel ratio), and a `field-box` print follows when the box moves (resize, a dialog or page sliding in). The shell fixes the proxy over that box, still clipped and pointer-events none, so the IME window and a phone keyboard's pan anchor at the field rather than the canvas's bottom-left.
