Labels and roles are unchanged; a print without a box leaves the proxy where it was. Room sway and the CRT bend are left out (up to about 5 CSS px at a phone screen's edges) so the box does not move every frame. Rect matches checked in Chromium at 1280x800 DPR 1 and 2, 390x844 DPR 3 touch and Interface Size 125%, with IME composition; real Android panning and CJK IME placement are not yet checked on a device, and a `visualViewport` keyboard inset is a follow-up. 33 UI-BR6 mutant rows.
- **Browser QA fixes (UI-QA6).** Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa6/results.md`, Chromium and Firefox, production builds of `ui` and `main`) passed all eight checks. At DPR 1 every page and dialog is pixel-identical to main at 1280x800, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and 390x844, apart from the new Interface Size control, the rotate hint and the 844x390 scanlines. Downscaled from DPR 1.25-3 the layout is unchanged. Hover edges match to the pixel at DPR 1, 2 and 3, and move by 1 px at a few places at 1.25 and 1.5. A live DPR change re-renders in 160-450 ms. Buttons are unchanged. One bug, fixed red-first: the portrait HUD's hint line ("Attach Energy, play Trainers…") still drew at 4-5 CSS px on phones, because `fit` shrank it back into its one 230 px line. With no prompt up, the hint (or a warning in its place) now wraps in the prompt's box under Log and Options, as landscape carries it in the prompt's place. It shrinks to fit no further than the prompt note's floor, which is 11 CSS px on a phone. `table_targets.lua` measures HUD text as drawn after `fit`, with real glyph proportions. Five UI-QA6 mutant rows.
- **Pixel budget for large screens (UI-BT6B; decision 5 revised by the user 2026-10-31: "use lower res for larger screens instead").** At 8-9 MP the GPU fell to 31-37 fps, so the canvas now renders at most 2560x1600 device pixels, about 4.1 MP. A denser screen renders at the highest pixel ratio within that, and the browser scales the canvas up. 1280x800 at DPR 3 renders at DPR 2, and 4K at 150% at about 1.05. It never goes under one pixel per CSS pixel, which is what main drew before full-density rendering, so no screen draws softer or slower than it did before Phase 6. A 4K screen at 100% or 125% renders as before. Screens within the budget render in full: phones at DPR 3, a 2x 1280x800 laptop, 2560x1440 at DPR 1. The budget sits in `Viewport.parse` beside the GPU texture limit, so the layout, scanline count, CRT fringe and pointer mapping follow the rendered ratio the same way. `viewport_density.lua` covers the sizes, the drag mapping at DPR 3 and a zoomed-out canvas. Browser check (`tmp/ui/qa6b/results.md`, HD 630, Chromium; Firefox for canvas size and clicks):
  - 1280x800@3: home 33 → 60 fps, table 30 → 57-59.
  - 2560x1440@1.5: home 36 → 60, table 34 → 57.
  - 1440x900@2: home 53 → 60, table 47 → 56-57.
  - Control at 1280x800@2: unchanged, the table's 56 is a CPU limit on both builds.
  - Clicks, hover edges and table drags land; a live DPR change across the budget re-renders. Downscaled, there is no layout shift and the CRT fringe and scanlines are unchanged; edges are slightly softer.
  - The wrapped portrait hint draws at about 12-13 CSS px, inside the HUD at 390 and 360 px wide, and a targeting prompt replaces it.

  Four UI-BT6B mutant rows.

### 2026-10-31 — Frontend & UI audit Phase 7: shared page widgets, simulator and page splits, spatial arrows, history tooltips, form errors and small guards

Phase 7, the last phase of the 2026-10-29 frontend and UI audit. It is web-only: Lua, the host page and the TS shell, with no API change.

User decisions:
- Spatial arrows with linear Tab (decision 15).
- Leave the eager bridge chunk alone (BR13, decision 16).
- No change to how buttons look or what they say.

The splits are pure moves, proven by recorded draw traces.

- **Shared page widgets and the builder/lobby splits (UI-PG15, UI-PG16).** Each copied widget now comes from one helper: the option cycle (`Button.optionCycle`/`cycleArrows`), the pagers (`Pager.arrows`/`Pager.draw`, one spec per page), the titled deck pile (`DeckGrid.drawPile`) and the icon glyphs (`Button.icon`). Where the copies had drifted (pip row widths a float bit apart, label narrowing, arrow sizes), the helpers take parameters, so each page draws what it did. builder.lua (1,012 → 776 lines) coordinates `ui/builder_deck.lua`, `ui/builder_filters.lua` and `ui/text_area.lua`, which keeps the decklist's wrap until its text or width changes. The Energy face moved to `ui/energy.lua`. online.lua (698 → 593) uses `ui/lobby_seats.lua` and `ui/deck_picker.lua`, shared with the sandbox. A recording love.graphics stub logged every call, its arguments and each page's controls in 209 states of library, builder, online, sandbox and settings, landscape and portrait. Its 453,899 lines matched the original code after every commit. There are 17 new UI-PG15/PG16 mutant rows, 5 rows moved with their code, and all 504 UI- rows are killed.
- **Table simulator split (UI-SM14).** `ui/simulator.lua` (1,965 lines) keeps `new`, `open`, `layout`, `invoke` and the frame's `update` (246 lines). It mixes in nine parts, each a table of methods copied onto `Simulator`, and a name defined twice fails at load: `sim_draw`, `sim_drag`, `sim_controls`, `sim_focus`, `sim_targeting`, `sim_juice`, `sim_presence`, `sim_input` and `sim_reconcile`, with `popupOffset`, `groupLabel` and `cardKey` in `sim_shared`. `src/lua/simulatorSidebar.ts` takes the hot-seat and remote sidebars and the Undo/Redo footer behind a narrow `SidebarHost`; the command handlers stay in `LuaSimulator`, since they mutate its private state. Every code line moved unchanged, and a recorded trace of every graphics call, shader send, texture method and host callback (`tmp/ui/p7b/draw_trace.lua`: setup, a turn, drags, targeting, popups, KO and juice frames, every overlay, game over, online presence, in four layouts with and without reduced motion) is byte-identical after each commit. 37 UI mutant rows moved with their code (the cardKey row now names `Shared.cardKey`), and all 489 UI rows are killed.
- **Arrow keys move by layout (UI-PG14; decision 15).** On home, the library, builder, sandbox, online lobby and Settings the arrows move to the nearest control in their direction (`Controls.grid`, Android FocusFinder's beam rules). Tab and Shift+Tab keep list order. Down on the library's 6-column grid reaches the pile below, since a pile's Manage button is left to Tab. Where an arrow already meant something it keeps it: online's Left/Right switch the menu tabs, and Left/Right still step the sliders, option cycles and tab rows, while a field that is being edited keeps every key. The table's card ring stays linear. The library and builder draw Back first, but Tab now starts at their content and reaches Back last (`ringLast`), and Shift+Tab from nothing lands on the last control rather than the one before it. The builder gains '/' (open the card search), 'D' (focus the deck list) and '-'/'+' (one copy less or more on a focused deck row, card or row button), listed in the canvas descriptions; the shell keeps '/' from Firefox's quick find. Buttons look and read as before. 30 UI-PG14 mutant rows (one UI-PG8 row re-anchored).
- **History tooltips (UI-SM12).** After 400 ms of hover or keyboard focus on a hot-seat history icon, its spoken label (Undo turn, Undo action, New game, Redo action, Redo turn) shows in the card popup's frame (`ui/tooltip.lua`). It sits above the icon, or below or beside it where above has no room or would cover a control without a tooltip (End turn, Log, Options, a board card; a hand card is not a control, so in portrait it may overlap the hand's lower few pixels), clear of the face and inside the board in landscape, portrait and an 844x390 phone, at 12 CSS px or more. It grows in over 0.12 s with motion and appears whole under reduced motion. It is never hit-tested and goes as soon as the pointer or focus moves or an overlay opens. A disabled icon under the pointer shows it too. A touch screen's pointer (`Display.coarse`) never raises one, though keyboard focus still does. 13 UI-SM12 mutant rows.
- **Sign-in errors (UI-PG9).** A refused sign-in, registration or deletion reports every refused field at once (`formProblems` reads all zod issues), and the account snapshot gains `invalid` (the refused fields; the server's 409 marks the email, a wrong deletion password the password) and `failures` (failed submits so far, the server's refusals included). Lua rings each refused field in the page errors' red on ink, opens the first one for editing, draws the message at 22 px (when it wraps, it takes the largest size down to 16 px whose lines and 3 px shadow fit the 45 px between the faces of the submit button and Continue with Google, centred there) and announces every failed submit, an identical repeat too. Typing in a field clears its ring; opening it does not. The shell sets `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` (a hidden `#auth-problem` holding the message) on the refused proxies. Register keeps the 8-character rule in the password label, "Password (at least 8 characters)", and in the proxy's name. Buttons are unchanged. 24 UI-PG9 mutant rows.
- **Sandbox empty state (UI-PG11).** The sandbox header gains `hidden`, the number of the user's decks the chooser leaves out. With decks but none playable, the tray says "2 of your decks aren't ready: a deck needs exactly 60 cards and a Basic Pokémon." under the unchanged Create a deck button. The chooser announces the empty state when it opens on it, comes to it, or the reason changes. 8 UI-PG11 mutant rows; UI-PG2-sandbox-no-decks-silent and UI-ST3-sandbox-ignores-store re-anchored.
- **Blank deck name (UI-PGX3).** The shell tells the builder how each name record ends. A name edit that ends blank puts back the last name the deck held and says "A deck needs a name. Kept <name>." (a long name cut to keep the message on its line, ending in "..." with no space before them and no period after). Builder and library share `deckNameOrNull` (trimmed, 1-100 characters). The proposal's visible label for filled name and search fields is not done. 5 UI-PGX3 mutant rows.
- **Password managers (UI-BR7).** After a successful login or registration the shell calls `navigator.credentials.store(new PasswordCredential({id, password, name}))` where the browser has it (Chromium), guarded so it never throws or rejects unhandled. The three auth proxies sit in a `<form>` with `display: contents` and a prevented submit, so nothing visible and no Tab stop changes; only the edited field is shown, as before. Not verified: a manual run with a saved credential (whether Chromium now offers to save, and whether it fills both fields while one is hidden); Firefox and Safari, which have no PasswordCredential and rely on their heuristics. 9 UI-BR7 mutant rows.
- **Router guard (UI-BR11).** A push to the page the router is on replaces the history entry and skips the page change, keeping the query (an open Settings form). 1 UI-BR11 mutant row.
- **Edition guard (UI-LC10).** `Card:draw` draws the ordinary face alone when an edition has no compiled shader, or none at all. 1 UI-LC10 mutant row.
- **Browser QA fixes (UI-QA7).** Browser QA (`tmp/ui/qa7/results.md`, Chromium and Firefox, `ui` against `main`) passed all seven checks. 81 of 85 page and dialog pairs at 1280x800, 390x844 and 844x390 are pixel-identical to main; the rest differ only in the random lobby code and a blinking caret. Buttons are identical everywhere. Five bugs, fixed red-first:
  - The landscape history tooltips covered End turn, and New game's hid its whole label. `Tooltip.place` now takes the table's controls and picks the first of above, below, right and left that keeps off every control without a tooltip of its own. In landscape, with End turn above the row and Log below it, labels go beside or below their icon; they may cover a fellow icon, whose own tooltip takes over there.
  - Down on the builder's catalog moved sideways. The fanned row sets its outer cards a few pixels lower, so `Controls.grid` took them as "below". A candidate's near edge must now lie past the source's middle, not merely past its near edge as in FocusFinder. A few diagonal jumps the QA noted as surprising now land elsewhere (Settings' Reduced Motion Left, two sandbox seat-tab moves), none of them kept behaviour.
  - After a field opened by Enter (on main too) or the builder's `/` was closed with Escape, the next Enter or `/` did nothing and the Escape after it left the page. The key's release landed on the focused proxy, which swallowed it, so SDL thought the key held and dropped the next press as a repeat. `captureKeys` now lets through the release of a key that went down outside the proxy.
  - At 390x844 a two-line sign-in message ran into Continue with Google, clipping descenders. A wrapped message now shrinks until its lines and 3 px shadow fit the 45 px between the faces, centred there; one-line messages are unchanged.
  - A shortened kept deck name read "Long ...." in speech; it now ends in three dots, with no space before them and no period after.
  A re-check in Chromium and Firefox (`tmp/ui/qa7fix/results.md`) confirmed all five, with e2e passing in both. 10 UI-QA7 mutant rows (6 earlier rows re-anchored); all 605 UI- rows are killed.

### 2026-11-09 — Backend and API audit safety nets, and what production says

Phase 0 of the 2026-11-09 backend and API audit (39 findings: 4 medium, 16 low, 19 info, no high). The user accepted all 12 of the audit's recommended decisions:

- Google's Cancel (`error=access_denied`) returns to the app with no error message, and any other provider error reads as the existing `exchange` failure.
- The Google sign-in probe stays, behind a deadline.
- `userSchema.email` becomes as permissive as what OAuth stores.
- A 404 save is terminal, offers Export, and a second Back leaves after any failed save.
- The builder slices every page from its card index once the index has loaded.
- Google sign-in drops the `profile` scope but keeps the userinfo call.
- The catalog limiter residual is tightened, but only after the builder change is live.
- The deck saver spaces save starts about 1 s apart.
- Every server response keeps its schema parse.
- Success and audit log lines get `logInfo`.
- A failed lobby alarm pass waits a fixed 5 s, in memory.
- The probes are kept until the phases land.

- **Deck writes that fail partway through their batch.** The `failingD1` helper refuses a batch before it runs, so nothing showed what happens when a statement fails after earlier ones in the same batch have written. Two tests in `decks/routes.d1.test.ts` add a trigger that raises ABORT:
  - On PATCH, the version update fails after the card delete and upsert have run. The answer is a JSON 500 and the deck reads back unchanged.
  - On POST, the card insert fails after the deck row was inserted. No deck is left behind.
  - Two mutant rows (API-DECK7-patch-unbatched, API-DECK7-create-unbatched) run those statements one at a time instead of as one batch, and both are killed (DECK7).
- **What production says** (read-only, the GraphQL Analytics API and `wrangler d1 insights`; `tmp/api/phase0/checks.md`):
  - **CPU.** In 30 days colorless-api answered about 2,900 requests with no `exceededCpu` outcome and no error. CPU was p50 3.4 ms, p99 23 ms and max 111 ms. The account is on Workers Free (10 ms per request), and the platform still served those requests. So EDGE1 (the card index ETag) keeps its place in Phase 3.
  - **D1 reads.** The peak was 2.44M rows read in one day (2026-10-23, 1,422 queries), 49% of the 5M free quota. The top queries are the builder's catalog pages and their `count(*)` over `cards`, about 5,084 rows per run, a full scan. This is CAT1's mechanism in production data, so CAT1 stays first in Phase 2.
  - **Workers Logs.** The Workers Free cap is 200,000 events a day with 3 days of retention. Cloudflare moves Workers Logs to Observability pricing on 2026-12-01, so recheck the allowance then (OPS5, Phase 5).

### 2026-11-09 — Backend and API audit Phases 1-4: a lobby alarm that backs off, builder pages from the card index, saves that can end, cheaper catalog tags, and sign-in that reads its errors

Four branches from `api`, built by worktree agents, merged on `api`, and following the decisions recorded in the Phase 0 entry above. The API deploys before the web app. No migration, binding or secret is needed. CAT2 (the catalog limiter after the cache) waits until this web release is live.

**Lobby alarm and online moves (API-P1).**
- A failed alarm pass waits 5 s (LOB1). The catch in `alarm()` sets an in-memory `retryNotBefore` (`ALARM_RETRY_MS`), and `scheduleMaintenance` never arms an earlier alarm. A good pass clears it. The alarm still always re-arms and never rethrows (2026-10-27). A deterministic throw now costs one pass every 5 s instead of a back-to-back loop over D1.
- A match that throws while starting is a failed start. The seed, both shuffles and `beginMatch` sit in a try that reports `match_refused` with the error, mirroring MI3. Both players read "This match couldn't start. Ready up to try again." and are un-readied. `match.put` stays outside the guard, and the floor covers it.
- A Bench card dropped on its own slot sends nothing (LOB2). `moveToAction` returns null, so the online table never waits on a reply the server does not send.
- Comments on where events interleave are corrected (LOB4). onAction and onBye await only storage, and the file header names the three D1 awaits where other events can run.
- Tests: two lobbyDO tests, one replacing the old "re-arms at the past deadline" test, plus one moveToAction test. 5 API-P1 mutant rows, all killed. No row for clearing the floor: once the delayed alarm fires, the floor is already in the past, so that mutation changes nothing a test can see (D420).

**Builder: catalog pages from the card index, and saves that can end (API-P2).**
- Pages come from the card index (CAT1). Once the builder's card index has loaded (`cardIndexLoaded()`), every page is sliced from it, so filter changes and prefetches send no GET /cards. Before then, pages 1-3 still come from the API. The trade-off is accepted: a re-ingest reaches an open builder up to INDEX_REUSE_MS later. This removes the catalog `count(*)` scans that Phase 0 found dominate D1 reads.
- A save answered 404 ends saving (DECK1). The builder says "This deck no longer exists. Export to keep it.", keeps later edits on screen unsent, and lets Back and in-app navigation leave. There is no "Save as a new deck". A 404 caused by another account signing in shows the same notice.
- A second Back leaves after a failed save. After any reported save failure, the first Back retries the save and arms the next press ("Not saved. Press Back again to leave anyway."), and the next press leaves without the edits.
  - A press within 350 ms of arming is ignored, as with Leave match.
  - A save that lands disarms it.
  - There is no timed window, so a slow retry cannot disarm it.
  - "Retrying..." does not arm it.
  - Leaving anyway gives the edits up. The saver is disposed and nothing is marked unsaved, so the page's close, reload and online handlers send nothing later. Browser QA found that, as first written, an abandoned edit was saved once the API answered again (B1).
- A blank name is saved as the kept name (DECK2). The PATCH body and every saver key come from one snapshot that puts the kept name in place of a blank one, so a lost save made then is still recognised as this tab's own. Blanking the name alone sends nothing.
- Saves start at least 1 s apart (DECK3). The saver's `MIN_SAVE_SPACING_MS = 1000` keeps steady editing within the 60 a minute deck write limit (it was about 93 a minute). An explicit flush (Back, page hide) does not wait. The routes.ts comment now says so.
- The library tells a 429 apart (DECK8): "Too many changes. Wait a minute."
- Write answers use read order (DECK5). POST and PATCH answer with cards in card id order, as reads do.
- 13 API-P2 mutant rows, all killed. The 83 existing rows on the touched files still die.

**Catalog and asset server (API-P3).**
- A cache hit is tagged by its key (EDGE1, CAT3). A KV-cached catalog response carries a weak ETag hashed from its KV key, on hits and misses alike, so hono/etag no longer hashes the 1.9 MB card index on every hit and 304. Measured locally (Node, not edge CPU), a hit fell from about 5.6 to 2.6 ms and a 304 from about 11 to 0.9 ms.
  - The key holds CACHE_VERSION and the catalog epoch, so a re-ingest or a version bump changes the tag.
  - A bypassed response keeps the body digest.
  - Each browser refetches every catalog route once after the deploy.
- Only 404 and 410 from the asset origin mean a missing image (CAT4). Those are still cached for an hour. Any other status below 500 that is not a 200 is an uncached 502, logged as `assets origin_status`. A short tcgdex rate limit no longer hides art for an hour.
- An R2 read error falls through to the origin (CAT5). It is logged, throttled, as `assets r2_get_failed`, and the image is served from the origin instead of a 500.
- One binding period (EDGE6). The lobby and catalog limits use `BINDING_PERIOD_SECONDS`. The catalog limiter runs on the shared `bindingRefuses` and keeps its `limiter_error` kind. Behaviour is unchanged.
- Left for later:
  - CAT2 (the limiter after the cache, a `/facets` limit, a miss tally) waits until Phase 2's web release is live.
  - Answering a 304 before the KV read was optional and was not done.
- Nine API-P3 mutant rows, all killed.

**Accounts and sign-in (API-P4).**
- Any stored email is returned (AUTH1). `userSchema.email` is now a non-empty string of at most 254 characters instead of `z.email()`. Google verifies some addresses that `z.email()` refuses (`ivan@example.xn--p1ai`, `a&b@example.com`), and those accounts got a 500 on every /auth/me. Google sign-in refuses an address over 254 characters as `profile`. Register and login keep the strict check.
- Cancel on Google is not an error (EDGE3, decision 1a). The callback reads `?error=` only with this browser's matching state, and logs `oauth_denied` with the code (null if it does not look like a code). `access_denied` returns to where sign-in started with no `authError`, and any other code reads as `exchange`. Without the matching state it is still a `state` failure, so the new branch skips no check.
- Google sign-in asks only for `openid email` (AUTH6). No profile claim has been read since AU3. The userinfo call stays.
- The Google start probe has a deadline (EDGE2). It runs under `withDeadline` (20 s, like WC3), so it cannot wedge the account form. `createLobbyCode` keeps the HTTP status, and a 429 reads "Too many new lobbies. Try again in a minute."
- Sign-in reads Retry-After (AUTH5). A 429 reads "Too many attempts. Try again in N minutes.", rounded up; a wait under 60 s, or no header, stays "Wait a moment." The delete form uses the same copy. A 503 on sign-in reads "Sign-in is unavailable right now. Try again later." instead of "Could not connect".
- 14 API-P4 mutant rows, all killed. AUTH1 is its own commit (e7d346a9), so it could go out alone.

### 2026-11-09 — Backend and API audit Phase 5 and CAT2: every catalog miss spends the search budget, and failures say why

The last two branches of the 2026-11-09 audit, built by worktree agents and merged on `api` after Phases 0-4 were released. CAT2 waited for CAT1's web release, which went live with Phases 0-4. Both are API-only, with no migration, binding, secret or wrangler change.

**Every catalog KV miss spends the search budget (API-CAT2, decision 7a, after CAT1's web release went live).**
- `CATALOG_SEARCH_LIMIT` (30 a minute per IP) now runs after the KV cache on `GET /cards`, so a hit never spends it and every miss does. That covers:
  - cacheable pages;
  - KV bypasses;
  - keys past KV's 512 bytes;
  - each repeat once the day's KV writes are spent.
  `id` and `nameExact` lookups stay exempt. `GET /facets` spends it on a miss. `/sets`, `/series`, `/sets/:id`, `/cards/:id` and `/cards/full` stay unlimited (small tables or index lookups).
- `catalog_reads` tallies every `/cards` miss, so cacheable misses show in Workers Logs.
- Catalog query values are capped at 100 characters, and `name`, `nameExact` and `text` at 300 (the builder's field limit). A longer value is a 400. Free text is 300 rather than about 100 because the client parses its local searches with the same schema.
- KV `get_failed`, `put_failed` and `epoch_failed` carry `errorFields`, so a spent KV quota reads `quota: true` (OPS1, catalog half).
- Client cost:
  - Before its card index loads, a builder spends up to 3 misses per new filter combination (page 1, plus prefetching pages 2-3), and none after. Browser QA measured 14 misses (12 `/cards`, 1 `/cards/index`, 1 `/facets`) for 8 quick filter changes on a throttled network, while the index took about 21 s. Two such sessions from one address within a minute could reach the limit; on a normal network the index loads in a few seconds.
  - Deck hydration (`?id=`), the online picker and the sandbox (`/cards/full`) are never limited.
  - A 429 shows the search's existing retry message.
  - No wrangler change.
- 13 API-CAT2 mutant rows, all killed. The API-P3 rows still die.
**Logging, diagnostics and docs (API-P5).**
- Failure lines carry their cause (OPS1). `health_failed`, the `after_response` failure line and the catalog KV `get_failed`, `put_failed` and `epoch_failed` lines spread `errorFields`: error and cause names, the SQL and the quota flag, never a message or a bound value. So release:health's 503 and a KV failure tell a spent quota apart from a schema or binding fault, and the runbook rows diagnose by `quota: true`.
- Validator 400s are logged (EDGE4). A throttled `http invalid_request` line carries the route pattern, the `target`, the issue count and the first five issue paths. It never logs messages, which can quote the input. `cross_site_refused` logs the route pattern instead of the raw path (OPS4).
- Three levels (OPS2, decision 10a):
  - `logInfo` (console.log) for success and audit lines: the sweep, sign-ins and account changes, lobby forfeit and wipe, and the `catalog_reads` tally.
  - warn for refusals and handled degradations.
  - error for failures nothing handled, now including `sweep_failed`.
  Saved queries filter on `evt`, so none change.
- `action_threw` is throttled per action type (LOB7, `logEventThrottledBy`), so one engine bug's line no longer hides another's for 10 minutes.
- `db-check --upstream` reads each set on its own (OPS3, `src/ingest/staleCards.ts`). A 404 reports that set's cards as stale ("set gone"), any other failure becomes a row, and the run always prints its results. `SourceRequestError` now carries the HTTP status.
- `oauth_state` checks the state before the code, so an error callback with a forged state logs `mismatch`, not `no_code` (browser QA nit).
- Docs:
  - backend-data.md §6 lists the WebSocket refusals (403, a 101 closed with 4004, the 503s) and the `CATALOG_SEARCH_LIMIT` binding row.
  - D1 reads run out first, and the per-miss cost (CAT1).
  - The Workers Logs cap of 200,000 events a day, kept 3 days, to recheck at the 2026-12-01 pricing change (OPS5).
  - About 42 ms per PBKDF2 in local workerd (AUTH8).
  - The new log kinds and levels in the saved-query table.
  - `ApiError`'s status comment.
- 17 API-P5 mutant rows, all killed. `MI3-throw-refused-silently` was re-pointed at the new `action_threw` line.

### 2026-11-09 — Builder prefetch from the index only, page input once the wipe has committed, and springs that do not ring on slow frames

Two items left open after the backend and API audit, fixed at the user's request.

- **The builder prefetches only pages the card index answers (API-FU).** Before the index loads, only the visible page costs a GET /cards. That is 1 KV miss per filter change instead of 3, so quick filtering before the index loads no longer approaches CATALOG_SEARCH_LIMIT. Once the index has loaded, neighbours are prefetched from it as before. Prefetching from the still-loading index was not done: it would load and parse the 1.9 MB index while the builder opens, which the idle-deferred load avoids. The cost is that, before the index loads, pages 2-3 show "Searching..." for about a second. Browser QA, throttled: 8 filter changes made exactly 1 GET /cards each.
- **Input waits only while the wipe's card covers the screen.** `main.lua` ignored every press, key and wheel for the whole 1.8 s wipe, including the 1.1 s dissolve after the destination is already on screen. Now `covering()` locks input only until the wipe commits, and the shell hears `transition:closed` at that frame. A page asked for during the dissolve waits in `pendingPage` and opens after the wipe, under its own wipe.
  - `screen_wipe_navigation.lua` pinned "input unlocks after removal". It now pins unlocking at commit, and gains a during-dissolve case.
  - UI-LCX2-parked-drag was re-pointed to `covering()`.
  - Right after the commit the dissolving card still covers much of the screen, so a click can act on a control not yet visible. That trade-off is accepted.
- **The dropped tap was the springs, not the wipe.** The builder never wipes. Its entrance slide is a `Motion` spring stepped once per frame, and main.lua caps a frame's dt at 0.1 s. At that step the explicit spring overshoots and rings: it crossed its place 13 times and settled after about 1 s of game time, which is 1-3 s of real time at the 100-300 ms frames seen while the card index parses. So Back and the name field moved out from under a still pointer between press and release.
  - `Motion:update` now runs a frame as equal substeps of at most `MAX_STEP = 1/50` s. At 50 frames a second or faster that is one step, so 60 fps motion and every recorded draw trace are unchanged. At 0.1 s frames the slide crosses its place twice and settles by 0.3 s, the 60 fps path.
  - `tests/motion.lua`'s step-by-step comparison with Balatro's `moveable.lua` now steps the reference with the same substeps, and a new case fails if any frame length makes a spring ring.
  - Browser QA on fresh page loads, every click a real 40 ms press: Back right after an add dropped 0/14 (was 1/10), and a first click on the name field 1/40 (was 10/40). The one miss was the earliest click of the run, 167 ms after Lua was ready, while the slide was still genuinely moving in. In-app opens: 0/10.
- 4 API-FU mutant rows, all killed. All 136 rows on `main.lua` and `builder.ts` behave as before (135 killed, plus UI-FU2-main-portrait-grows, declared equivalent). Web-only: no API change.

### 2026-11-09 — Dependency audit safety nets: Lua for the mutants job, a weekly full-tree report, one Workers runtime in the lock, and only production packages in the Worker

The dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`, 35 findings) found the npm tree healthy and the process around it stalled. Phase 0 adds checks only; nothing that ships changes (decision id DEP-P0).

- **Main's CI was red because the mutants job had no Lua (PRC1).** Lua-killed mutant rows arrived on 2026-10-29. The mutants job spawns `bun scripts/test-lua.ts`, but only the lua job installed `lua5.1`, so every sampled Lua row reported "baseline is RED" and the job failed on all 9 pushes since. The job now installs `lua5.1` before the sample. CI is not the release gate (`release` runs the local `bun run check`), so nothing shipped untested.
- **A weekly report of the whole tree (PRC5).** GitHub's dependency graph does not read bun.lock: it lists 22 manifest ranges and no transitive package. So Dependabot raised 0 alerts while `bun audit` reported 42. The comment beside the prod-only CI audit (decisions.md "Bump hono, zod and @hono/zod-validator past their advisories") assumed "dev tools are left to Dependabot", and those alerts never arrive. `.github/workflows/deps-report.yml` runs every Monday and on demand. It writes the full `bun audit` and `bun outdated -r` to the run summary and never fails. The `--prod` gate stays as it was. Upstream repository advisories that are not yet in GitHub's database (hono's of 2026-11-01 returned 404) are seen only by reading the release notes of the 5 shipped packages, which the upgrade routine (Phase 1) does.
- **One miniflare and one workerd in bun.lock (VER1).** `scripts/lock-check.ts`, in `check:static`, fails when the lock resolves two versions of miniflare, workerd or a workerd platform binary. Dependabot's wrangler PR #6 would have nested `wrangler/miniflare` 5 beside the exact miniflare 4 pin. The D1 suites would then have run one workerd and `wrangler dev` another, with about 200 MB more per install. The check passes today. Phase 2 moves the pin.
- **Only production dependencies in the Worker (USE5).** `worker-bundle.ts --check` reads the dry-run source map's packages and fails on any outside the `dependencies` of apps/api, packages/engine and packages/schema. Today the bundle holds @hono/zod-validator, @noble/ciphers, drizzle-orm, hono and zod. A planted `import TCGdex from "@tcgdex/sdk"` in the Worker entry failed it, naming the SDK and its 4 dependencies.
- **No Node globals in Worker source (USE6).** @types/node reaches the API's typecheck through undici's types (via miniflare in the test harness). So `process` or `Buffer` would typecheck, then throw in a Worker without `nodejs_compat`. A Biome override on `apps/api/src/**` denies `process`, `Buffer`, `__dirname`, `__filename`, `require` and `global`. It excludes tests, `src/testing/`, and the two bun-run ingest file helpers (`payloadCache.ts`, `sqlFile.ts`). No production file used them. The override must be carried through the Biome 2 migration (Phase 7).
- User decisions on the audit's open questions:
  1. Keep the Balatro assets and credit their author (no replacement, no access gate).
  2. Give the project a free-use licence (MIT).
  3. Follow wrangler's miniflare with `convertV4MiniflareOptions`.
  4. Deploy the Worker with `--minify` and uploaded source maps.
  The remaining decisions take the report's recommendations.
- 3 DEP-P0 mutant rows, all killed.

### 2026-11-09 — Dependency audit Phase 1: grouped Dependabot, Node 24 actions, one bun and one Node from laptop to CI, a release-age gate, and a monthly routine

Phase 1 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`) changes how updates arrive and are taken. It changes dev and CI only; nothing that ships changes, and bun.lock is byte-identical (decision id DEP-P1).

- **Grouped Dependabot (PRC2, decision 5).** Dependabot had 8 PRs open since 2026-10-28, with none merged, and sat at its limit of 5, so its weekly runs did nothing. Coupled bumps came apart (wrangler #6 without workers-types #8), and majors that cannot pass (TypeScript 7, #4) used up slots. `.github/dependabot.yml` now groups the bun updates into `cloudflare` (wrangler, miniflare, @cloudflare/*), `vite` (vite, vitest, @vitest/*), `types` (@types/*), `production` (hono, zod, drizzle-orm, @hono/zod-validator, @noble/ciphers; minor and patch) and `dev` (everything else; minor and patch). The limit is 10. Majors of typescript (Phase 5), vitest (Phase 6), @biomejs/biome (Phase 7) and @types/node (decision 8) are ignored, each with a comment naming its phase. The bun ecosystem supports `cooldown`: dev updates wait 7 days, and the five shipped packages are excluded, so a security patch for the Worker arrives in the next weekly run. Actions are grouped with a 7-day cooldown. Groups' `dependency-type` is not supported for bun, so the dev catch-all is the pattern `*` after the named groups. The bun ecosystem at `/` already covers the workspaces: #5, #6 and #8 edit apps/api/package.json. check-jsonschema validates the file.
- **Node 24 actions (VER11).** actions/checkout v4.4.0 and actions/cache v4.3.0 were Node 20 actions that GitHub force-ran on Node 24. ci.yml and deps-report.yml now pin checkout v7.0.1 (`3d3c42e5`) and cache v6.1.0 (`55cc8345`), the SHAs of Dependabot #2 and #1. Both tags point directly at those commits. Their release notes change no input this repository uses. Cache v5 and v6 move to Node 24 and ESM, and keys, paths and restore-keys work as before. Checkout v6 keeps credentials in a separate file, and v7 refuses fork checkouts under `pull_request_target`/`workflow_run`; CI uses `pull_request`. `fetch-depth` and `filter` are unchanged. actionlint is clean.
- **One bun (VER12).** CI installed and tested on 1.3.13 and audited through `bunx bun@1.4.2`, while local dev and `release` ran 1.4.2. `.bun-version` and `packageManager` are now 1.4.2 and `engines.bun` is `>=1.4`. Bun 1.4.2 has `audit --prod` (it checks 5 packages) and `outdated -r`, so CI and the Dependency report call plain `bun`. `bun install --frozen-lockfile` and a plain `bun install` both leave bun.lock byte-identical (sha256 6309797726ea…).
- **One Node (PRC4, decision 8).** tsc, vite, vitest, wrangler and biome start with `#!/usr/bin/env node`, so CI ran whatever Node the runner image shipped. `.node-version` is `22.23`, the local minor. It resolves to 22.23.4, the current 22 LTS and a security release, which the runner image already has. Every ci.yml job runs `actions/setup-node` v7.1.0 (`949feb24`) from that file: static, lua, test, mutants and bench all start Node tools. setup-node caches automatically only for npm, so it adds no cache. `engines.node` is `>=22.12`, vite's floor. @types/node stays as it is until Phase 2 sets `^22`.
- **A release-age gate (PRCM2).** `bunfig.toml` sets `[install] minimumReleaseAge = 259200`. Bun 1.4.2 takes only the number of seconds; `"3d"` fails to load. Tested in a scratch project. `bun add hono@^4.13.0` got 4.13.16 instead of the 2-day-old 4.13.17, an exact `hono@4.13.17` was refused ("blocked by minimum-release-age"), and `--minimum-release-age=0` installed 4.13.17. A frozen install of a lock that holds 4.13.17 succeeded, with an empty cache too. Bun 1.4.2 has no `--minimum-release-age-excludes` flag, but bunfig's `minimumReleaseAgeExcludes` works (with `["hono"]`, the same add got 4.13.17). The urgent-patch override is `--minimum-release-age=0` for one command.
- **Frozen installs in release (PRC8, decision 7).** `release` and `deploy` start with `bun install --frozen-lockfile`. It does not dirty the tree, so the clean-tree gate still passes. `release` makes no other network call for dependencies.
- **`bun run deps:check` (PRC8, PRC11).** `scripts/deps-check.ts` is a report and always exits 0. It needs the network, so it is not in `check:static`. It prints `bun outdated -r`, the full `bun audit` and CI's `--prod` gate, and lock-check's verdict. It scans every bun.lock package against the registry for deprecated or unpublished versions. Today that finds drizzle-kit's two @esbuild-kit loaders, as the audit did. It compares the local bun and node with the pins, lists specs that differ between workspaces (none today), and reports both `compatibility_date`s, flagging one older than 92 days (2026-06-01, 161 days, today). It ends with the advisory and release pages of the five packages the Worker ships, derived from the manifests. A run takes about 9 s.
- **The routine is written down (PRC7, PRC8, PRC11, USE7; decisions 12 and 17).** A "Dependencies" section in `docs/conventions.md` covers:
  - Range policy: carets, hand bumps raise the floor, and exact pins only with a reason (@tcgdex/sdk D188, @noble/ciphers MI1, miniflare equal to wrangler's own). A dependency declared twice uses one spec.
  - The root owns vitest, typescript, biome and @types/node.
  - The toolchain pins.
  - The release-age gate and its override.
  - The Dependabot groups.
  - The monthly routine, with the gates per group. `production` merges weekly and then releases. `cloudflare` and `vite` need a local `check`, `build` and `e2e`, because CI skips build:lua and e2e.
  - How a major is taken: a go/no-go branch.
  - The `compatibility_date` process: both dates move together, with each cloudflare bump and at least quarterly. The gates are `bun run check` with its d1 suites, e2e and a `wrangler dev` smoke.
- 6 DEP-P1 mutant rows, all killed.

### 2026-11-09 — Dependency refresh: wrangler 4.158 and its own miniflare 5 together, hono past its advisories, in-range dev tools, @types/node on the Node 22 line, and bun's default install-script trust

Phase 2 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`) is the one lock refresh. It changes only the dependency blocks of the two manifests, bun.lock, the 3 Miniflare constructors, and one conventions line (decision id DEP-P2). It supersedes Dependabot #3 (vite), #5 (drizzle-orm), #6 (wrangler) and #8 (@cloudflare/workers-types).

- **The D1 harness follows wrangler's own miniflare (VER1, user decision 3).** Wrangler moved to miniflare 5 at 4.117.0 (2026-07-31). Every stable wrangler since has shipped it, so `wrangler dev` and deploy already run on it. apps/api pinned miniflare 4.20260708.0 exactly, which held wrangler at 4.109.0. A wrangler bump alone would have nested a second miniflare and a second workerd (about 150 MB more per install, as Dependabot #6 did). Miniflare 5 rejects the v4 single-worker options ("Unrecognized keys: modules, script, d1Databases ... expected array at workers"). So the 3 `new Miniflare(...)` calls (`apps/api/src/testing/d1.ts`, `db/migrations.d1.test.ts`, `db/namePromptMigration.d1.test.ts`) now pass their options through miniflare 5's `convertV4MiniflareOptions`. The pin stays exact, at the version wrangler itself depends on: wrangler pins its miniflare exactly, so any other version is a second copy. lock-check (DEP-P0) fails when the pin and wrangler drift apart. Bump wrangler, miniflare and @cloudflare/workers-types together. Miniflare 5 is published as a beta, but stable wrangler ships it. `convertV4MiniflareOptions` is a compatibility layer and may be removed in a later release; getPlatformProxy (option B) stays the fallback. The 23 D1 files (338 tests) pass. `wrangler dev` still persists under `apps/api/.wrangler/state/v3/d1/miniflare-D1DatabaseObject`, so `scripts/lib/localD1.ts` finds the local database unchanged.
- **Versions, before → after.**
  - wrangler 4.109.0 → 4.158.0 in both manifests. The range floor moves from `^4.0.0` to `^4.158.0`, so a re-resolve can never land below the harness's miniflare 5.
  - miniflare 4.20260708.0 → 5.20261104.0-beta (exact, the registry's dependency of wrangler 4.158.0); workerd 1.20260708.1 → 1.20261104.1.
  - @cloudflare/workers-types 5.20260708.1 → 5.20261104.1. This is the floor of wrangler 4.158.0's peer range `^5.20261104.1`, and the date matches workerd. 5.20261106.1 was the newest version at least 3 days old.
  - hono 4.13.13 → 4.13.17 (VER2: GHSA-h7mg-cq63-vhpq and the other 4.13.16 fixes).
  - drizzle-orm 0.45.2 → 0.45.4; drizzle-kit 0.31.10 → 0.31.11. Both stay on 0.x (user decision 13).
  - vite 8.0.10 → 8.3.7 (rolldown 1.0.0-rc.17 → 1.2.18); vitest 4.1.9 → 4.1.12 (not 5).
  - @types/node 25.6.0 → 22.20.5. The range is `^22.20.5`, the Node 22 line that dev and CI run (PRC4, user decision 8). No type changes were needed.
  - zod 4.6.7 and @hono/zod-validator 0.9.1 were already the latest; @hono/zod-validator stays (USE3 not done, user decision 16). typescript 6.0.3 and biome 1.9.4 stay.
  - Transitive packages refreshed in range with `bun update`, run after the shim was in: undici 7.28.0 → 7.30.1, sharp 0.34.5 → 0.35.5, postcss 8.5.13 → 8.5.32, nanoid 3.3.12 → 3.3.20, @vitest/mocker 4.1.9 → 4.1.12, esbuild (wrangler's) 0.28.1 → 0.28.2. bun.lock has 249 entries before and 246 after.
  - Every version respects the 3-day release-age cooldown that Phase 1 introduces (PRCM2):
    - wrangler 4.158.0 and miniflare 5.20261104.0-beta: 2026-11-05.
    - workerd 1.20261104.1 and workers-types 5.20261104.1: 2026-11-04.

    wrangler 4.159.0 (2026-11-09, miniflare 5.20261109.0-beta) was skipped because it was published the same day. This is the set the audit tested. To re-lock one set without moving the rest, pin the three packages exactly, run `bun install`, then restore the caret ranges; `bun install` keeps the locked versions.
- **`bun audit`: 42 → 1 (VER1, VER3).** Before: 42 (15 high, 23 moderate, 4 low). After: 1 moderate, esbuild 0.18.20 (GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99). drizzle-kit 0.31's deprecated @esbuild-kit/core-utils pins it. It affects only esbuild's `serve`, which drizzle-kit never calls. This is the known residual until drizzle 1.0 (VER9, decision 13); no override is forced. `bun audit --prod` stays clean (5 packages).
- **`"trustedDependencies": []` (TRN5).** The old `["@biomejs/biome"]` trusted only Biome 1.9.4's postinstall, which does nothing. Because a list was set, bun's default trust no longer applied. Measured with clean `bun install --frozen-lockfile --verbose` runs on bun 1.4.2:
  - With the old list, only biome's script ran.
  - With the field absent or `[]`, bun's default trust applies, and only workerd's postinstall runs. It checks that the platform package is present and hard-links the native binary over its JS shim. It downloads from npm only when that package is missing.
  - Bun runs no postinstall for esbuild, although esbuild is default-trusted. sharp 0.35.5 has no install script.
  - The audit's verifier wrote that `[]` keeps behaviour unchanged. That is not true here, because workerd's script now runs.
  - Biome's script is blocked and listed by `bun pm untrusted`.

  After a clean install, biome, wrangler (`--version`, `dev`, the dry-run bundle), esbuild (a transform), workerd (`--version`) and drizzle-kit all work. The field still lists nothing: no package needs a script to work.
- **Measured.**
  - `bun run check`: green. 740 files and 17,258 tests in 72.3 s.
  - `bun run e2e`: PASS in 90.6 s; boot ready in 2360 ms.
  - `bun run build`: OK.
  - `worker-bundle --check`: 951.4 → 952.3 KiB of the 1024 KiB budget.
  - `node_modules` on a fresh install: 436 → 457 MB. The workerd binary is about 30 MB larger, while miniflare and wrangler shrank by 12 MB. The audit's "634 MB" duplicate case does not happen.
  - Web entry chunk: 193.22 → 203.78 kB (gzip 59.85 → 63.62), because rolldown folds the api and deckRules chunks into it. There are 10 chunks before and 8 after, and total JS shrinks. Accepted (user decision 11).
  - `bun run bench:boot` (measured with wrangler 4.159; the vite build does not involve wrangler), ready at 20 Mbps (median of 3): 1852 → 1836 ms. At 5 Mbps: 4547 → 4546 ms. love-start: 769 → 774 ms and 957 → 926 ms. Nothing gates on these numbers; love.wasm and game.data dominate boot.
  - `bun run api:dev` smoke: it migrates the local D1 and serves `/health` 200 and `/health/db` 200 (`{"ok":true,"migrations":13}`).
- 1 DEP-P2 mutant row, killed. It removes the conversion in `createTestD1`; consistency.d1 and routeAccess.d1 fail with miniflare 5's "Unrecognized keys" error.

### 2026-11-09 — Deploy the API Worker minified with its source maps, gate the bundle the deploy builds, and pass zod refinement messages as `error`

Phase 3 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`), decision id DEP-P3. It ships: the API Worker gets smaller, and @colorless/schema changes in both the API and the web app.

- **The Worker shipped unminified while the budget gated a minified build (USE1).** The apps/api `deploy` script ran a bare `wrangler deploy` (1937.8 KiB, 421.7 KiB gzip). `worker-bundle.ts --check` measured a `--minify` dry run (951.4 KiB, 257.3 KiB gzip) that never shipped, so its 1024 KiB budget would already have failed the real artifact. Per audit decision 4:
  - The `deploy` script now runs `wrangler deploy --minify`. `wrangler dev` (and so `bun run dev` and the e2e suites) stays unminified, which a `minify` key in wrangler.jsonc would not.
  - `apps/api/wrangler.jsonc` sets `"upload_source_maps": true`, so Workers Logs resolves a minified stack trace to source. wrangler 4.109's config schema has the key; a misspelled one draws "Unexpected fields found". `keep_names` stays at wrangler's default (true), so `error.name` in the error log keeps its names. `observability` is unchanged (`unstable_readConfig` reads `{ enabled: true }` as before).
    - Amended 2026-11-10 (observability audit ERR1, OBS-P0): Cloudflare reads the map only for an uncaught exception, and a stack a Worker logs itself is not resolved. Only `scheduled()` and the Durable Object let an exception escape, so the map serves the cron and the lobby. An HTTP route's throw is caught by `onError`, whose `http unhandled` line carries names and no stack.
  - The uploaded map is 5.2 MB (about 1.3 MB gzip), beside a 257 KiB gzip script.
- **The gate builds what the deploy builds.** `worker-bundle.ts` now reads the flags of the `wrangler deploy` step in the apps/api `deploy` script and runs its dry run with them, so the bundle it measures is the one that ships. `--check` also fails, before building, when that step lacks `--minify` or wrangler.jsonc does not set `"upload_source_maps": true` on a live line. Dropping `--minify` fails twice: once by name, and once over budget at 1937.8 KiB. Setting `"upload_source_maps": false` fails by name; wrangler then writes no map, which the gate needs to read.
- **zod 4's deprecated `message` parameter (VER13).** The two refinements that used it, `withoutControlChars` (`packages/schema/src/text.ts`) and the empty-patch guard (`packages/schema/src/decks/requests.ts`), now pass `error`. The issues are byte-identical (`{ code: "custom", path: [], message }`). No test pinned either message, so `text.test.ts` and `decks.test.ts` now pin both word for word. The other `message:` keys are `ctx.addIssue` issue objects, which are not deprecated. conventions.md now says to use `{ error }`.
- **`wrangler types` (VER8) waits for Phase 2's wrangler and workerd bump**, so the generated types come from the new workerd.
- 6 DEP-P3 mutant rows, all killed. One is killed by running `worker-bundle.ts --check` itself: a dry run without the deploy script's flags goes over the budget.

### 2026-11-09 — Dependency audit Phase 4: an MIT LICENSE, served third-party notices with a Credits button, and love.js 11.4.1 pinned by hash

Phase 4 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`) settles licensing and the build's two inputs from outside the repo (decision id DEP-P4). It ships a web release: a new file under `/love/`, a Credits button and a Credits link. The Worker does not change.

- **The Balatro assets stay, credited (TRN1, decision 1).** The build still copies 37 files from the Balatro source tree: 8 shaders, 2 textures, the font, 25 sound effects and the menu music. Nothing is replaced or gated. The notices, both READMEs, `LICENSE` and `assets/branding/SOURCES.md` credit Balatro to LocalThunk, published by Playstack. They also say that the files remain their owners' and are not covered by this repository's licence. The notices add that the Lua interface follows Balatro's own code in places, and that the wordmark is modelled on Balatro's logo (SOURCES.md records that pass 3 edited the original sprite). Nothing claims a permission. m6x11plus is credited to Daniel Linssen (managore), quoting his page (managore.itch.io/m6x11): "free to use with attribution". It is still Balatro's copy of the font.
- **MIT for the project's own work (decision 2).** `LICENSE` is MIT, `Copyright (c) 2026 Jofr3`. Jofr3 is the dominant git author: 1825 commits, against 624 by Claude. A section under the licence text limits it to the repository's own code and original assets. It names what it does not cover: the Balatro assets, m6x11plus, LÖVE and love.js, TCGdex data and images (with the transition-card scans), and the Pokémon trademarks and artwork. The root package.json has `"license": "MIT"`. No manifest's dependency block changes, and bun.lock is unchanged. No trademark disclaimer existed anywhere. `LICENSE`, the notices and the root README now carry one fan-project line: "not affiliated with, endorsed, sponsored or approved by" Nintendo, Creatures Inc., GAME FREAK inc., The Pokémon Company, LocalThunk or Playstack.
- **Third-party notices, served (TRN4).** `scripts/third-party-notices.ts`, run by `build.mjs` after the asset manifest, writes `public/love/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (97 KB). Vite copies it to `dist/love/`, and it is served at `/love/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt?v=<hash>`: versions.json versions it like every `/love/` file, under the year-long immutable cache. Its sections:
  1. Colorless's LICENSE.
  2. Balatro, with each copied file listed by kind from build.mjs's own `assets` list.
  3. m6x11plus.
  4. TCGdex's MIT licence, and who owns the card artwork.
  5. The page's npm packages: @noble/ciphers and zod.
  6. The Worker's packages: @hono/zod-validator, @noble/ciphers, drizzle-orm, hono and zod.
  7. love.js (MIT, Tanner Rogalsky), read from the installed package.
  8. LÖVE 11.4. This section has the libraries love.wasm carries that LÖVE's own licence file leaves out (Lua 5.1, SDL2, FreeType's FTL credit, PhysicsFS, Box2D, the Xiph codecs, and the Bitstream Vera licence taken from love.wasm), then LÖVE 11.4's `license.txt` verbatim.

  Package texts are read from node_modules. drizzle-orm's and @hono/zod-validator's tarballs carry no LICENSE, so their repositories' texts are kept in `scripts/notices/` with LÖVE's, TCGdex's and Vera's (`VENDORED` records each source). The file starts with a UTF-8 BOM: Vite's dev server and preview send `text/plain` with no charset. `wrangler dev` sends `text/plain; charset=utf-8`.
- **The notices cannot fall behind the bundle.** The page's list is the production dependencies of the root, packages/engine and packages/schema. A Vite plugin (`noticed-packages`) fails a build whose bundle carries any other npm package. It runs in CI's vite build too, which has no Lua build. The Worker's list is worker-bundle.ts's `SHIPPED` allowlist (now exported), which `worker-bundle --check` already enforces.
- **Credits in the app.** Settings' Game tab has a blue Credits button (200×38) under Interface Size, drawn with the existing `Button.draw`. Its activation prints `COLORLESS:credits`. The shell opens the Credits link's versioned href through a new `openTab` port, which calls `window.open` with `opener` cleared and falls back to this tab when blocked. Settings stays open. The native accessible menu gains `<a id="credits-link">` after Settings, and the noscript page shows it too. Screen readers hear "Credits. Opens in a new tab." The e2e stall check now counts 7 live menu links. Screenshots are in `tmp/deps/qa/p4/`.
- **love.js pinned (VER10, decision 14).** `scripts/love-inputs.ts` pins love.js 11.4.1 and the sha256 of the four files the build runs or copies:
  - `index.js` 578f3dab…
  - `src/game.js` 223c6f5c…
  - `src/compat/love.js` 12db012a…
  - `src/compat/love.wasm` ecc9e098…

  build.mjs runs it before staging. A different version or hash stops the build, naming each file, with the fix ("reinstall love.js@11.4.1, or update LOVE_JS for a deliberate runtime change"). The 11.5 GitHub build is not taken.
- **The Balatro tree is recorded (VER10).** `asset-manifest.json` is now `{inputs, files}`. `inputs.balatroSource.git` holds the tree's commit (f01aba9f… today) and whether `resources/` differs from it. It is null when the tree is not the root of a git checkout. `inputs.balatroSource.resources` is one sha256 over every copied file's name and hash. Nothing reads the manifest, and the deploy still skips it.
- **README asset list corrected.** It said "six audio files" and seven shaders (background was missing). It now lists the 37 files, records where the music ships, describes the manifest's new shape, and says that 11.4.1 is love.js's final npm release and the loader patch is local.
- CI is unchanged: it builds with `LOVE_VERSIONS_OPTIONAL=1` and without the source, and that build passes with the new plugin (checked with public/love moved aside).
- 12 DEP-P4 mutant rows, all killed:
  - love.js hash and version checks, 2 rows;
  - source commit and dirty flag, 2 rows;
  - vendored texts, asset grouping and the web package list, 3 rows;
  - the Vite notice guard, killed by a production vite build;
  - the Credits button, 2 rows (credits.lua);
  - main.lua's wiring (focus_main.lua);
  - the versioned URL (hostShell.test.ts).

### 2026-11-09 — Generate the API Worker's types with `wrangler types`, check them in check:static, and drop @cloudflare/workers-types

Phase 3 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`), finding VER8, decision id DEP-P3b. Nothing that ships changes: the minified dry-run bundle is byte-identical (sha256 19d3fd35…, 952.2 KiB).

- **Generated runtime and binding types (VER8).** Cloudflare recommends `wrangler types` over the `@cloudflare/workers-types` package for Worker code. The package's v5 has only the latest runtime types, while `wrangler types` matches the compatibility_date and the installed workerd. `apps/api/worker-configuration.d.ts` (16,285 lines, workerd 1.20261104.1 at 2026-06-01) is committed. `bun run --cwd apps/api types` regenerates it with `rm -f worker-configuration.d.ts && wrangler types --env-file=/dev/null --env-interface=GeneratedEnv`:
  - `--env-file=/dev/null`: a bare `wrangler types` reads `apps/api/.dev.vars`, so a local secret's name would be committed. The main checkout has one.
  - `rm -f`: wrangler keeps an existing runtime body whose header still matches, so a hand edit survived regeneration.
  - `--env-interface=GeneratedEnv`: no global `Env` that is stricter than ours, so a file that forgets to import `Env` fails to compile.
- **`Env` reads its bindings from the generated file.** `apps/api/src/env.ts` keeps its shape and comments, with each binding typed as `GeneratedEnv["..."]`. The 6 RateLimit bindings and the vars stay optional, because the code handles their absence and unit tests run without them, so no test subtype is needed. The secrets stay hand-listed: wrangler.jsonc does not name them. A binding added to wrangler.jsonc that `Env` does not list fails to compile, naming it; a removed or renamed one fails at its lookup.
- **tsconfigs.** apps/api and `tsconfig.seam.json` list the generated file under `include`, not `types`. `tsc -b` decides a project is up to date from its included files only: listed under `types`, a regenerated file left the old build standing. The packages already had `"types": []`, and the root and the other tsconfigs did not use the Workers types.
- **@cloudflare/workers-types removed.** Nothing references it now; it is an optional peer of wrangler and drizzle-orm. It is gone from both manifests, and bun.lock loses only its entry. The re-resolve that removed it needed `--minimum-release-age=0`, because the cooldown also refuses re-resolving a locked version under 3 days old (hono 4.13.17); no locked version moved. The conventions now say so. "Bump together" (conventions, lock-check) is now wrangler and miniflare, then regenerate the types.
- **Staleness check.** `scripts/worker-types.ts`, in `check:static`, needs no network: it passes under `unshare -rn`. It takes about 1.5 s alone and 2.5–3.3 s in parallel with the other steps. It checks two things:
  1. The header records exactly the `types` script's command.
  2. `wrangler types --check` passes with the script's flags. Wrangler 4.158's `--check` reuses the header's `--env-file` but not its `--env-interface`, so a bare `--check` reports the committed file as stale.

  A changed compatibility_date and a renamed binding both fail it. `--check` compares the bindings hash and the runtime header, not the body, so a hand edit to the runtime part is not caught; the `types` script's `rm -f` means a regeneration removes one.
- **Biome.** The file is in `files.ignore` (it raises 21 lint and format errors). `lint-coverage.ts` lists it in a `GENERATED` set of files it does not require Biome to see.
- **Measured.** `bun run check`: green. `worker-bundle --check`: 952.2 KiB, byte-identical.
- 2 DEP-P3b mutant rows, both killed. One accepts any `wrangler types` header and is killed by `workerTypes.test.ts`. The other runs a bare `--check` and is killed by running `worker-types.ts` itself.

### 2026-11-09 — TypeScript 7 for every typecheck, with the TypeScript 6 parser kept for the mutation scripts

Phase 5 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`), finding VER4, user decision 9: a go/no-go branch. Decision id DEP-P5. Dev only: nothing that ships changes.

- **TypeScript 6.0.3 → 7.0.2.** The 6.x line ended at 6.0.3 (2026-04-16), and 7.0.2 dates from 2026-07-08. TS 7 is the Go-native compiler. Its npm package exports only the version at `.` and keeps the API under `./unstable/*`. That is why Dependabot #4 failed: its only errors were in the 3 mutation scripts that call the compiler API (`binding-gate.ts`, `reanchor.ts`, `symbol.ts`; `createSourceFile`, `forEachChild`, `ScriptTarget`, the `is*` guards). Those 3 now import `@typescript/typescript6` 6.0.2 (Microsoft's TS 6 under its own name; it resolves `typescript@6.0.3` as `@typescript/old`). Every typecheck runs TS 7. Port the scripts and drop the alias when TS 7.1's API is stable; 7.1.0-beta came out on 2026-10-20.
- **Go criteria, all met:**
  - `tsc -b` has 0 errors on all 8 projects (`typecheck-coverage` lists the same 1051 files).
  - A planted type error is caught in a TS file and in a `// @ts-check` JS file.
  - `mutants:precheck` reports the corpus runnable.
  - The binding-gate and symbol suites pass.
  - `bun run check` is green: 742 files, 17,298 tests, 76/76 Lua.
  - `tsc -b --force` takes 6.7 s, against 47.7 s on TS 6 (7.1x). An incremental `tsc -b` in check:static takes 1.3 s.
  The configs already avoided what TS 7 rejects: no `baseUrl`, `moduleResolution: bundler`, ES2022.
- **CI.** The tsc cache key prefix is now `tsc7-`, so no TS 6 build info is restored into TS 7. Dependabot still ignores typescript majors: an 8 gets its own branch, as 7 did.
- 2 DEP-P5 mutant rows, both killed: `symbol.ts` and `binding-gate.ts` importing plain `typescript` again.

### 2026-11-09 — Vitest 5, with the module cache's new key and path

Phase 6 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`), finding VER6: a go/no-go branch. Decision id DEP-P6. Dev only: nothing that ships changes.

- **vitest 4.1.12 → 5.1.0** (2026-11-02), with @vitest/mocker and @vitest/spy following. Engines `^22.12 || ^24 || >=26` match the pinned Node 22.
- **The module cache left `experimental`.** `vitest.config.ts` sets `fsModuleCache` and `fsModuleCachePath` at the top level; the old place still works but prints a deprecation. The cache is now `node_modules/.vitest-cache` (75 MB after a full run), so ci.yml's vitest cache step caches that path instead of `.experimental-vitest-cache`, which Vitest 5 no longer writes. CI would otherwise have missed silently. The mutation harness still points `VITEST_MODULE_CACHE_PATH` at `tmp/mutation-vitest-cache`. A sample run wrote only there and left `node_modules/.vitest-cache` untouched.
- **Go criteria, all met:**
  - The whole suite passes: 744 files, 17,298 tests, the same counts as Vitest 4. A full `bunx vitest run` took 1m12s.
  - The same seeded mutant sample (`sample.ts --random 40 --seed dep-p6`) selects the same 40 rows and gives the same verdict per row on both versions: 38 killed, 2 known survivors.
  - The one `nameFilter` row (API-OPS2-login-warn) still selects its tests under Vitest 5's `-t` and is killed.
  - `clearMocks` now defaults to true. The shared projects already restore mocks after each test, and nothing failed.
- Dependabot still ignores vitest majors: a 6 gets its own branch, as 5 did. No mutant rows: the change is configuration, and the sample comparison above is its check.

### 2026-11-10 — Biome 2.6.6, with the migrated config and its lint fixes

Phase 7 of the dependency audit (report `tmp/deps/deps-audit.md`), finding VER5: a go/no-go branch. Decision id DEP-P7. Biome 1.9.4 had had no release since 2024-10-17.

- **@biomejs/biome 1.9.4 → 2.6.6** (2026-11-06), pinned exactly: `biome ci --error-on-warnings` is a check:static gate, and a patch can change formatter output or add a warning. The pin's reason is in conventions' list.
- **Config.** `biome migrate --write` turned `include`/`ignore` into `files.includes` with `**/` prefixes and `!` exclusions, moved `noRestrictedImports` from nursery to style and `organizeImports` into `assist`. By hand: duplicate exclusions dropped, and the six `noRestrictedGlobals` entries (USE6's override on apps/api/src) given real messages in place of the migration's TODO placeholders. New: `!**/*.md`, because Biome 2 formats Markdown and the docs are hand-wrapped (audit decision 10), and `noTemplateCurlyInString` off for `scripts/mutation/mutants.ts` and `binding-gate.ts`, whose patches and fixtures are code strings (382 of the 385 warnings).
- **Fixes, none of which changes behaviour:**
  - 21 `noUnsafeOptionalChaining` errors, all in engine tests: `(x?.[0] as T).field`. Each became `(present(x)[0] as T).field`, with `present()` new in `fixtures/values.ts`. It throws on null or undefined, so a missing value still fails the test, as the old TypeError did. Rewriting these as `?.field` would have let a `toBeUndefined()` assertion pass on a missing value.
  - 17 `useIterableCallbackReturn` errors: 16 `forEach` arrows that returned their call's value now have block bodies, and `upserts.test.ts`'s `map` throws after its exhaustive switch.
  - 4 `useOptionalChain` warnings applied, among them `sanitizeRedirect`'s guard (`!raw?.startsWith("/") || …`), which is equivalent for null, undefined and "".
  - 18 suppressions Biome 2 no longer needs (`noDelete` and `useTemplate` left `recommended`). Each `noDelete` suppression became a plain comment that keeps its D124 reason.
  - 2 test titles that quote the `${uid}:${name}` key format keep a `biome-ignore`.
  - 5 files reformatted (3 engine tests, `schema/src/lobby/equals.ts`, `schema/package.json`).
- **`scripts/lib/generated.ts`.** Biome 2 refuses a second root config below the one it started from, so `biomeFormatted` writes `files.includes` and runs Biome from its temp directory. Run from the repo root, every generator's `--check` failed.
- **Go criteria, all met:**
  - `biome ci . --error-on-warnings` is clean (1098 files).
  - lint-coverage OK (1058 files).
  - Planted violations still fire: `process` in a Worker file, and the engine imported in `lobby/`. A test file using `process` is still exempt.
  - `bun run check` passes: 744 files, 17,298 tests.
  - The seeded sample `sample.ts --random 40 --seed dep-p7` killed all 40 rows.
  - The 6 rows anchored on the changed engine lines and the 5 older Biome-related rows (D212 ×2, D414, D416, D460) are killed. D212's two rows were re-anchored on the `**/` globs.
- **No release needed.** The shipped files changed are a comment, block bodies and one equivalent guard, so they ship with the next release.
- 4 DEP-P7 mutant rows, all killed: the generator run from the root, Markdown back in, the template rule back on for the corpus, and the redirect guard losing its slash check.

### 2026-11-10 — Build and CI/CD audit safety nets: CI keeps its static signal, the mutant sample cannot silently lose its diff, and the release's Lua check refuses Lua 5.2

Phase 0 of the build system and CI/CD audit (report `tmp/build/build-audit.md`, 27 findings, 0 high). Decision id BLD-P0. The user took all 12 recommendations of the report's section 6. CI and dev only; nothing to release.

- **Static checks run after a failed audit (CIM1).** The static job's "Static checks" step has `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`. A new advisory with no fix yet fails the audit step, but no longer hides tsc, Biome and the guards.
- **Main pushes are never cancelled (CI7).** `cancel-in-progress` is true for pull requests only. The mutant sample diffs each main push against the one before it, so a cancelled push's files would only have been drawn at random.
- **Checkouts keep no credentials (CI8).** All 6 checkouts (ci.yml, deps-report.yml) set `persist-credentials: false`. First, `scripts/mutation/sample.ts` diffs with `--no-renames` (new `changedSince`): rename detection fetched blobs the mutants job's `blob:none` partial clone lacks, which the verifier reproduced as exit 128 with origin unreachable, after which the sample silently fell back to the random draw. A base that exists but whose diff still fails now stops the job. A missing base (a first push, a force-push) still falls back to the draw, as before. actionlint is clean.
  - Left to the user: in the repository's Actions settings, require SHA pinning and allow only `actions/*` and `oven-sh/setup-bun` (every action is already SHA-pinned).
- **`check:lua` refuses anything but Lua 5.1 (ENV4).** `--require-source` is the release's only run of the source-comparing Lua tests, and `scripts/test-lua.ts` used to fall back to the system `lua` (5.2.4 on the dev machine) with a printed note. Now it exits 1 with the apt and nix hints. Plain `test:lua` keeps the lenient fallback.
- **Comments and docs (ENV6, DEP2).** `apps/api/src/env.ts`: APP_ORIGIN's comment had the direction backwards (wrangler.jsonc holds production; `dev` overrides it), and the Google secrets are no longer "absent until registered". backend-data.md §3.7.2 and conventions.md ("Database migrations"): a Durable Object migration blocks `wrangler rollback` to any API version before it, so it ships in a release with no other API change. The stale "rehearsal has not been run" line from the 2026-10-28 Phase 5 entry is amended.
- **Local cleanup (BLD3).** Stale `packages/*/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` (from before build info moved under `dist/`) and orphan declarations under `packages/*/dist` were deleted locally and rebuilt with `tsc -b`. Nothing tracked.
- 2 BLD-P0 mutant rows, both killed: the sample diff without `--no-renames`, and a failed diff read as no changes. The Lua 5.1 refusal has no row, because the killing command would depend on which interpreters the machine has.

### 2026-11-10 — Release identity and gates: every deploy names its commit, deploy:web cannot ship a stale dist, and the release checks that the new code answered

Phase 1 of the build system and CI/CD audit (report `tmp/build/build-audit.md`), findings ENV1, DEP4, DEPM1, DEP1, CI1, ENV5 and ENV6. Decision id BLD-P1. The user took decisions 1b, 6, 7 and 12. **Needs a release (API and web).** The first `bun run release` with this phase is its proof.

- **Deploys carry the commit (ENV1).** Both Workers deploy through `release-gate.ts … -- wrangler deploy <flags>`. After its gates pass, the script runs wrangler with `--tag <short sha> --message <subject>`. The subject is cut to Cloudflare's 100 characters. The sha comes from release-gate.ts and not from a `$(git …)` in package.json, because `scripts/worker-bundle.ts` reads the deploy script's flags for its dry run. `deployFlags()` now also reads a step that ends in `-- wrangler deploy`.
- **`/health` names the running version.** apps/api/wrangler.jsonc gains a `version_metadata` binding, `CF_VERSION_METADATA`, and worker-configuration.d.ts was regenerated with `bun run --cwd apps/api types`. `GET /health` returns `version: {id, tag}`, which is `null` where the binding is absent, and it sends `no-store`. `healthResponseSchema` requires the field.
- **The release record (ENV1, ENV5; decisions 6 and 7).** `release:gate` has a new `versions` step that records each Worker's running version. The new `release:finish` step, the last one of `release`, does four things:
  - It appends a line to `tmp/releases.log`: the time, the tag, the commit, each Worker's version before and after, the D1 bookmark, and the balatro-source commit from `dist/love/asset-manifest.json`. That manifest stays out of the deployed assets, so this line is now its durable record.
  - It prints the release notes, which are `git log --format=%s <previous release-* tag>..HEAD`. There are no GitHub Releases.
  - It tags HEAD `release-YYYYMMDD-HHMM` (UTC). The tag is annotated with the log line, and finish pushes it.
  - It drops the pin.
- **What ships is what was checked (DEP4, DEPM1).**
  - A Vite `build-info` plugin writes `dist/build-info.json` with `{commit, dirty, apiOrigin, siteOrigin, loveStrict}` and adds `<meta name="build" content="<sha>[-dirty]">` to the page. It never fails the build, so `bun run e2e` (a production build against a local API) still passes. public/.assetsignore keeps the JSON out of the deployed assets.
  - `deploy:web`'s new `web-dist` step refuses a dist built at another commit or from a dirty tree, a lenient Lua build, origins other than `.env.production`'s, and a site origin that is not the API's `APP_ORIGIN`. A bare `deploy:web` with the 2026-11-10 dist (built at `c44b7f3e`, HEAD `31c66261`) printed "dist/ was built at c44b7f3e, not HEAD 31c66261" and exited 1 before wrangler ran.
  - `release:gate` starts with a new `pin` step, which writes HEAD to `tmp/release.json`. While the pin is under 15 minutes old, every `clean-tree` requires HEAD to be the pinned commit, and a release of another commit is refused. A pin of the same commit is taken over, so releasing again stays the recovery after a failure. `release` ends in `|| release-gate.ts abort`, which drops a pin of HEAD and exits 1. `unlock` drops a dead pin.
  - `clean-tree` compares HEAD with `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` and no longer with the local `origin/main` ref, which a merge on GitHub leaves behind.
- **The health checks prove the new code (DEP1).**
  - `release:health` now runs three checks, each tried up to 6 times, 5 s apart. First, `/health`'s tag must be the released commit. Then `/health/db` must count the migrations, as before. Last, `GET /sets` must answer 200 and `GET /auth/me` 401, both as JSON. `HEALTH_TAG=<tag>` (`*` for any) replaces the tag it waits for, for use after a rollback or against a local `wrangler dev`.
  - `deploy:web` ends with a new `web-health` step. The deployed page's meta must be the released commit, and every `/assets/` and `/love/` reference must answer 200. A script or style served as `text/html` counts as missing, since that is how the SPA fallback answers a missing file.
- **The audit gates the release; CI's result is printed (CI1, decision 1b).**
  - `scripts/audit.ts` runs `bun audit --prod --audit-level=moderate` with the dated ignore list in `scripts/audit-ignore.txt`. Each line there is `<id> <YYYY-MM-DD> <reason>`, and an entry older than 30 days is printed as a reminder. CI's "Audit production dependencies" step and the release's new blocking `audit` step both run it.
  - The `ci` step prints CI's conclusion for HEAD (static, test, mutants) from `gh api repos/Jofr3/colorless/commits/<sha>/check-runs`, and it never blocks. It runs in `release:gate`, in the API `deploy` and in `deploy:web`, so `bun run deploy` and `api:deploy` print the line too. The verdict function (`ciVerdict`) reads each job's latest run and is unit-tested.
  - Phase 2 folded the lua job into static, so `CI_JOBS` in `apps/api/src/releaseChecks.ts` lists static, test and mutants (fixed at merge).
- **Google secrets are gated (ENV6, decision 12).** `REQUIRED_SECRETS` adds `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`. requiredSecrets.ts and the README's "API secrets" now give the reason: a missing Google secret answers a loud 503, but only to someone who tries Google sign-in. The `secrets` failure tells you where to get each kind of secret.
- **Source maps (ENV5).** The `sourcemap: "hidden"` comment in vite.config.ts no longer cites error reporting, which does not exist. Production stack traces are resolved by checking out the release's tag and rebuilding, since the bundles are byte-identical across rebuilds. Nothing is archived.
- **Layout.** The pure verdicts live in `apps/api/src/releaseChecks.ts`, beside requiredSecrets.ts, so vitest runs their tests (`releaseChecks.test.ts`). `scripts/releaseScripts.test.ts` pins the package.json wiring. `scripts/audit.test.ts` checks that ci.yml runs `scripts/audit.ts`.
- **Docs.** backend-data.md §3.7.2 has the new order, steps, "Which commit is live", "A dead pin" and an updated recovery table, with a `release:finish` row. The restore and "After every deploy" snippets use `HEALTH_TAG='*'`. conventions.md (the release sentence, and the monthly routine now reads audit-ignore.txt) and docs/status.md (updated: live is the 2026-11-09 release `be8cf9b0`, performance is deployed, where the live commit shows from now on) are refreshed too.
- **Gates.**
  - `bun run check`: 747 files, 17,338 tests, Lua 76/76 under 5.1.
  - `bun run build`: dist/build-info.json at HEAD, clean, strict.
  - `bun run e2e`: PASS in 66.8 s.
  - `worker-bundle --check`: 952.4 KiB, minified, read through the new `--` form.
  - `release:health` against a local `wrangler dev`: OK with `HEALTH_TAG='*'`, and refused the untagged local version without it.
  - `pin`, `abort` and `unlock` were exercised locally. `versions` and `ci` were run read-only against production and GitHub.
- **To confirm on the first release.** `/health` shows `version.tag` equal to the release's short sha. `wrangler deployments list` shows the tag and message for both Workers. `release-*` is pushed. `tmp/releases.log` has its first line, and the summary prints "no earlier release tag".
- 18 BLD-P1 mutant rows, all killed:
  - clean-tree: trusting the local ref, ignoring the pin.
  - The pin: refusing nothing, never expiring.
  - web-dist: stale, dirty and local-origin builds.
  - Health: any tag accepted, the SPA fallback taken for a present file.
  - The CI verdict hiding a failure.
  - The audit: dropping the ignore list, accepting an impossible date.
  - Wiring: release:gate without `audit`, deploy:web without `web-dist`, release without `|| abort`.
  - The dry run losing the `--` deploy flags.
  - An ungated Google secret.
  - `/health` without its version.
- DEP-P3-deploy-flags-ignored was re-anchored on the new `deployFlags` and is still killed.

### 2026-11-10 — CI efficiency and secret scanning: one setup action, a bounded install cache, three jobs, and gitleaks on every push

Phase 2 of the build system and CI/CD audit (report `tmp/build/build-audit.md`), findings CI2, CI5, CI6, CI3 (its CI part) and CI11, user decisions 2 and 3. Decision id BLD-P2. CI only; nothing to release.

- **One setup action (CI6).** `.github/actions/setup/action.yml` is the toolchain every ci.yml job runs after its own checkout: setup-bun (from `.bun-version`), setup-node (from `.node-version`), Bun's install cache, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, optionally Lua 5.1 (`lua: "true"`), and the ISO week as an output for the weekly vitest cache keys. Its actions stay SHA-pinned, and every checkout keeps `persist-credentials: false`. A toolchain change is now made once. deps-report.yml keeps its own two setup steps: it needs no Node and no cache. Dependabot's github-actions entry lists `directories: ["/", "/.github/actions/*"]`, since `/` alone scans only `.github/workflows`.
- **Bun's install cache is bounded and saved once (CI5).** It is keyed on `bun.lock` alone, with no `Linux-bun-` fallback key: the fallback carried every older tarball into each new entry (162.7 → 334.3 MB). Each job restores with `actions/cache/restore`; only the static job saves, with `actions/cache/save` right after its install and only on an exact miss. On a lockfile change, 4 of the 5 jobs used to spend 4-8 s failing to reserve the key. The cost: on a lockfile change, each job installs cold from the registry once. The report's "one uncached run" measurement cannot be made before a push, so the cache stays; the first lockfile-change run measures the cold install (below).
