- **Three jobs: static, test, mutants (CI2, decision 2).** The lua job (Lua tests under Lua 5.1, the bundle) and the bench job are steps of the static job now. Every check step after the audit runs with `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, so one failure does not hide the others' results, as Static checks already did (BLD-P0, CIM1). The engine benchmark is the last step, runs on main pushes only (`github.event_name == 'push'`), and has step-level `continue-on-error: true`, so it never fails the job. It writes its table to the run summary, as before.
  - **No docs-only skip.** A `changes` job would itself bill about 1 minute on every run, more than the skip saves (13 of 126 main pushes were docs-only, about 8 minutes each). `paths-ignore` would skip the whole workflow, so a docs-only push would get no decision-index check (`check:static` reads `docs/decisions.md`) and no secret scan, and the next push's scan starts after it.
- **Secret scanning in CI (CI3, decision 3).** The static job downloads gitleaks 8.30.1 (linux x64), checks its sha256, and scans `github.event.before..HEAD` on a push and `origin/<base>..HEAD` on a PR, with `--redact`. A base that is not in the clone (a first push, a force-push) scans HEAD's commit alone, with a warning. The static checkout is now `fetch-depth: 0` with blobs, since the scan reads patches (the clone is about 121 MB packed). `.gitleaks.toml` extends gitleaks' default rules and allows `generic-api-key` only in `packages/engine/src/**/*.test.ts` and `packages/engine/src/fixtures/`. Its header holds the version and sha256 that the CI step reads (`# gitleaks-version:`, `# gitleaks-linux-x64-sha256:`), so the Phase 4 pre-push hook can run the same binary with the same config.
  - **Full-history scan, run once locally** (all refs, 2406 commits, 3.3 GB of patches, 5 min 18 s): gitleaks' default rules found 7 matches, all `generic-api-key`, all in `packages/engine/src/censusAtHead.test.ts` (7 commits, 2026-10-08 to 2026-10-23). Each is a test title naming card keys next to a long identifier, not a credential. With `.gitleaks.toml`: 0. A scan of HEAD's tree found the same file's one current match. Planted values checked the allowlist's scope: a `generic-api-key` in an engine test is allowed, while a `github-pat` in the same file and a `generic-api-key` in an apps/api test both fail the scan.
  - A real finding is handled by rotating the secret, not only by rewriting the branch: the push has already reached GitHub.
- **Workflow lint in CI (CI11).** The static job also runs actionlint 1.7.12 (pinned, sha256-checked), which runs the runner's shellcheck over every `run:` script. Locally it is clean on ci.yml and deps-report.yml, with shellcheck 0.11.0 on the path. zizmor 1.32.0 (`--offline`, run locally only) reports 3 low "self-repository" hints for `uses: ./.github/actions/setup`, which ask for a newer `$/` syntax; `./` is kept, and zizmor stays out of CI. luacheck stays deferred.
- **Expected effect.** A warm main push was billed about 9 minutes in 5 jobs (static 37 s, lua 24 s, bench 40 s, test 115 s, mutants 192 s, each rounded up: 1 + 1 + 1 + 2 + 4); the report's November average was 9.8. Now: static about 90 s (2 minutes, with the Lua install, the full clone, actionlint, gitleaks and bench), test 2, mutants 4, so 8. A PR drops from 9 to 8 too (static about 70 s without bench). A lockfile-change run drops from about 11 to about 10. That is about 1 billed minute per run, about 10%, or about 110 minutes a month at November's pace. The roadmap's 20-25% counted the folded steps alone; the secret scan and the workflow lint take part of it back. Wall time does not change: mutants (about 3 min) is still the critical path.
- **Re-measure after a week** (with `gh run list --workflow ci.yml` and each run's job timings):
  - Each job's duration and its rounded-up minutes, main pushes and PRs apart. Static must stay under 3 minutes and under mutants.
  - The bun cache entries (`gh cache list --key Linux-bun-`): one per lockfile, each about the size of one install (the clean first entry was 162.7 MB), and no "Unable to reserve" in any job.
  - On the first lockfile-change run: the cold `bun install` time in test and mutants against the 3-9 s restore. If a cold install is no slower, delete the cache from the setup action (CI5's first option).
  - The Secret scan step's time and any false positive. A false positive gets a scoped `[[allowlists]]` entry (by rule and path), never a broad one.
- Left to the user (repository settings, already recorded at BLD-P0): require SHA pinning and allow only `actions/*` and `oven-sh/setup-bun`. The gitleaks and actionlint binaries are fetched by `curl` from their GitHub releases and checked by sha256, so that allow-list does not affect them.
- No mutant rows: the change is workflow YAML and a TOML config, with no script logic a test can run. The planted-secret scans above check the allowlist, and actionlint checks the workflow.

### 2026-11-10 — Lua build: the package uuid comes from game.data, so the build is reproducible and the IndexedDB package survives releases that leave it unchanged; `dev` rebuilds Lua on change

Phase 3 of the build system and CI/CD audit (report `tmp/build/build-audit.md`). Decision id BLD-P3. The user took both recommendations: BLD1, and BLD4 through decision 11. Web only. It ships with the next release and is not urgent.

- **Deterministic package uuid (BLD1).** love.js writes `package_uuid: uuid()` into game.js (`love.js/index.js:117`), so two builds of one commit never matched. game.js, then host.js and versions.json, which carry game.js's hash, and so index.html all differed. `build.mjs` now passes the patched game.js through `withPackageUuid` (new `frontend/lua/packageUuid.mjs`). It replaces the one `"package_uuid":"…"` with the first 128 bits of sha256(game.data), formatted 8-4-4-4-12, and throws unless exactly one stamp occurs, as the loader patch does.
  - **Why game.data's bytes alone are the right key.** The loader caches only the package bytes. They go in IndexedDB `EM_PRELOAD_CACHE` under `package/<path>game.data`, with `{uuid}` stored beside them, and it reuses them only when `PACKAGE_UUID === result.uuid` (game.js `checkCachedPackage`). The file table (names, offsets, audio flags) is always read from the new game.js. If game.data is byte-identical, the cached copy is the same data, so reusing it is correct even when game.js changes for other reasons. If any byte changes, the uuid changes too. The loader then fetches the package again and overwrites the cached copy, so a stale package cannot be served unless sha256 collides on its first 128 bits.
  - **Effect.** Returning players keep their cached package across every release that leaves game.data unchanged, including API-only releases (`release` always rebuilds and redeploys the web). The first release with this change switches every uuid once, from random to derived.
  - **Proof.** `build:lua` twice: all 19 files under public/love are identical (sha256 list digest `5d2ca598…`; game.js `f7c73fb4…`, host.js `be45417d…`). Full `bun run build` twice: all 44 files under dist are identical (digest `0d06bf0d…`; index.html `c14c3f34…`). game.data `e965103d…` gives uuid `e965103d-0592-2dd9-b171-1e392a990780`. Appending two comment lines to main.lua gave `7b0d439f-be48-0c0f-…`, and reverting the edit brought back `e965103d-…`.
- **Lua watcher in `dev` (BLD4, decision 11).** `dev` is now `bun run build:lua && node frontend/lua/watch.mjs vite …`. `watch.mjs` runs the command it is given (Vite) and passes on Vite's exit code and SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP. It watches `frontend/lua` recursively and reruns `build.mjs` 200 ms after the last change under `main.lua`, `conf.lua`, `ui/`, `assets/` or `web/`. `tests/`, `tools/` and the README rebuild nothing.
  - Each rebuild is a whole `build.mjs` in its own process, so build.mjs's unlink, 500 ms pause and re-add of love.js and love.wasm still run. Changes during a build queue one more build. A failed build is reported and the watch continues.
  - Vite is the last item on the command line on purpose. `dev-tailnet.ts` runs `bun run dev --port N`, and bun appends that flag to the script. `bun run --parallel` would have handed it to the shell as a command of its own. bun 1.4.2's `--parallel` also fails to run an inline `node <file>` command ("Script not found").
  - Checked by hand on port 5299: two quick edits gave one rebuild, `/love/love.js` and `/love/love.wasm` still served 200 with their own content types afterwards, an edit under `tests/` or to the README did not rebuild, and both a group SIGINT (Ctrl+C) and a SIGTERM to the watcher alone stopped Vite.
  - Documented in README ("Scripts") and frontend/lua/README.md. The README's cache table now says when the IndexedDB package is reused.
- **Typecheck coverage.** `packageUuid.mjs` and `watch.mjs` are `// @ts-check` and listed in `tsconfig.node.json` and `typecheck-coverage.ts`'s `CHECKED_JS`.
- **Go criteria, all met:** `bun run check` passes (745 files, 17,306 tests, check:lua ok). Both determinism checks above pass. `bun run e2e` (Chromium 154) passes (100.7 s). An earlier run under load average 10 on 4 cores missed the dead-code step's 3000 ms budget by 223 ms.
- 3 BLD-P3 mutant rows, all killed by `scripts/packageUuid.test.ts`: the uuid ignores game.data, only a missing stamp is refused, and the stamp pattern loses its `g` flag. Nothing in a row checks that `build.mjs` calls `withPackageUuid`, because the only test that would catch it is a double build, which needs the Balatro source the CI mutants job lacks. The determinism check above covers it.

### 2026-11-10 — An opt-in pre-push hook runs Biome, the decision index and, when installed, gitleaks on the commits being pushed

Phase 4 of the build system and CI/CD audit (report `tmp/build/build-audit.md`): finding CI4 and the hook part of CI3, decisions 3 and 4. Decision id BLD-P4. Dev only; nothing to release.

- **The hook.** `.githooks/pre-push` is a POSIX sh stub that execs `bun scripts/pre-push.ts`. It is opt-in: `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, once per clone. The setting lives in the clone's shared config, so every worktree uses it, and each worktree runs its own checkout's copy. A branch from before the hook runs nothing. `git push --no-verify` skips it. README's setup block and conventions.md ("Pre-push hook") say so.
- **It checks what is pushed, not the working tree.** For each pushed commit it `git archive`s the tree into a temp directory outside the repo (about 0.25 s, with the repo's `node_modules` symlinked in). It then runs `biome ci . --error-on-warnings` and that tree's `scripts/decision-index.ts --check` there, concurrently. So in a shared checkout another agent's unfinished file neither fails the push nor stands in for the pushed version. Biome runs on the whole tree rather than the changed files: that would save about 2 s, but a changed-files run has to get first pushes, `biome.json` edits and deleted files right.
- **Secret scan.** It runs only when a `gitleaks` binary is on PATH and the pushed tree has `.gitleaks.toml` (from BLD-P2): `gitleaks git --redact --verbose` over `<remote tip>..<tip>`, or `<tip> --not --remotes=<remote>` for a new ref or a remote tip this clone has never fetched. Otherwise it prints which of the two is missing. Deleted refs are neither checked nor scanned.
- **Mutation journal.** It refuses while `tmp/mutation-journal.json` (or `MUTATION_JOURNAL_PATH`) exists, as `check.ts` does, because a commit made from a mutated tree may carry a mutant. The test is presence only. `check.ts`'s pid-liveness wording was not moved into a shared module, because three mutant rows are anchored in `check.ts`.
- **Measured** in a throwaway clone with `core.hooksPath` set only there, pushing to a local bare repo:
  - Time. The hook costs Biome's time plus about 0.3-0.5 s for the export and the index (CPU 10.2 s against Biome's 9.9 s). Biome alone takes 2.66 s on an idle machine, so the hook takes about 3 s. The machine was shared with other agents during the test (load 4-14 on 4 cores): 4.0 s at the lightest load, 5.6 s at load 5.5, 8.0 s at load 13 (Biome alone 7.4 s at that load).
  - A clean push passes, including with an untracked file in the working tree that fails Biome.
  - A committed `any` fails Biome, the push is rejected and the remote does not move. The hook also caught a real slip: an unformatted line in its own first edit.
  - A present journal refuses in 0.04 s, before anything runs.
  - `--no-verify` pushes the red commit in 0.04 s.
  - gitleaks 8.30.1 (release tarball, sha256-checked, kept in the scratchpad; nothing installed): skipped with a note when the tree has no config. Clean over a 1-commit range (1.5 s). A planted `ghp_` token fails both an existing ref's range and a new ref's `--not --remotes` range, and the finding is printed redacted.
- `bun run check` passes: 745 files, 17,312 tests.
- 6 BLD-P4 mutant rows, all killed by `scripts/prePush.test.ts`: the refusal printed but not enforced, a deleted ref's zero sha checked, tips not deduplicated, an unfetched remote tip trusted as a range base, a deleted ref given a scan range, and the scan run without a config. Running Biome in the working tree instead of the export has no row, because only a push from a real clone shows it; the clone test above covered it.

### 2026-11-10 — Production only, no staging environment; the build audit's merge and what is left

The build system and CI/CD audit's decision 10 (finding ENV3), plus the record of how Phases 1-4 merged. Decision id BLD-P5.

- **No staging environment (ENV3, decision 10B).** There is one set of remote resources: the `colorless` and `colorless-api` Workers, the `colorless` D1 database, and one KV namespace and R2 bucket. No wrangler `env` blocks. Pre-production is local: miniflare-backed `bun run dev:all` and `bun run e2e`, which runs a fresh temporary D1. Recovery is D1 Time Travel to the release bookmark plus `wrangler rollback`, both in backend-data.md §3.7.2-3.7.3. A staging environment would mean its own D1, KV, R2, rate-limit namespace ids and web Worker, and keeping them in step on every binding change, which buys little for one developer with no external users. Revisit it when the third-party-cookie sign-in item is worked on, the main behaviour local dev cannot reproduce.
- **Merge.** Phases 1-4 ran as worktree agents from BLD-P0 (3d6b1a12) and merged into `build` in order P1, P2, P3, P4. mutants.ts append conflicts were resolved by keeping both sides, and one README conflict (the `dev` line and the hook setup line) by keeping both. Integration fix: `CI_JOBS` drops `lua`. On the merged branch: `bun run check` green (749 files, 17,354 tests, 76/76 Lua under 5.1), `bun run e2e` PASS, and all 30 BLD mutant rows killed (P0 2, P1 18, P3 3, P4 6), plus the re-anchored DEP-P3-deploy-flags-ignored row.
- **Left for later, each needing the user:** the Cloudflare token split (DEP3, decision 5: a read-only ambient token and a deploy token only `release` loads); the web Worker's 404 script for missing assets (DEP6, decision 9, verified first on a throwaway Worker); the web rollback rehearsal (DEP2, decision 8, after DEP6); and the repository Actions settings (require SHA pinning, and allow only `actions/*` and `oven-sh/setup-bun`).

### 2026-11-10 — Two Cloudflare tokens: a read-only ambient one, and a deploy token only production writes load

The build system and CI/CD audit's decision 5 (finding DEP3). Decision id BLD-P5.

- **What the audit missed.** The ambient `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, the account-owned token named `agents` that the dotfiles load into every fish shell, held about 300 permission groups across the whole account. It covered far more than Workers and D1 write: Billing Write, Account API Tokens Write, Zero Trust, DNS and more. It was not only "a production-write token".
- **The tokens.** Both were created through the API with the old token's Account API Tokens Write, because the dashboard was not reachable from the dev machine. Both are account-owned and restricted to this account.
  - `colorless-agents-read` has 8 Read groups: Workers Scripts, D1, Workers KV Storage, Workers R2 Storage, Account Settings, Account Analytics, Workers Observability and Workers Tail.
  - `colorless-deploy` has 7 groups: Workers Scripts, D1, Workers KV Storage and Workers R2 Storage Write, plus Account Settings, Workers Tail and Workers Observability Read.
  - Both are stored age-encrypted to the dotfiles' recipient and decrypted into `~/.local/state/secrets/` (mode 600). The read token replaced the old value of `secrets/CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN.age`, the one the dotfiles load into every fish shell. The deploy token is `secrets/CLOUDFLARE_DEPLOY_TOKEN.age`, file-only. The old `agents` token is kept, file-only, as `secrets/CLOUDFLARE_AGENTS_TOKEN.age`: on the user's instruction it is not revoked yet, because other projects may still use it (dotfiles 97b2b314).
- **Checked with each token.** Both tokens passed every read-only command the release and the audits use: `deployments list`, `versions list`, `secret list`, `d1 list`, `kv namespace list`, `r2 bucket list` and `d1 migrations list --remote`, plus the gates `secrets`, `print-keys`, `migrations` and `bookmark`. The audit expected `print-keys` and `migrations` to need D1 Write; they do not.
- **The wiring.** `deployTokenEnv` (apps/api/src/releaseChecks.ts) swaps in the deploy token from `~/.local/state/secrets/CLOUDFLARE_DEPLOY_TOKEN` (or `CLOUDFLARE_DEPLOY_TOKEN_FILE`), and refuses a missing or blank file. release-gate.ts's `-- wrangler deploy` uses it for both Workers, and every gate before it keeps the ambient token. `db:migrate:remote` and hand-run writes (rollback, `secret put`, `time-travel restore`, `ingest.ts --remote`) go through `apps/api/scripts/with-deploy-token.ts`. backend-data.md §3.7.2 has a "Cloudflare tokens" paragraph.
- **Limits.** The split stops a mistaken write, not a deliberate one: any process running as the user can read the file. The deploy token can still deploy any Worker in the account, because Cloudflare tokens cannot be scoped to one script. Its first real use is the next `bun run release`.
- 2 BLD-P5 mutant rows, both killed: the deploy keeping the ambient token, and a blank token file accepted.

### 2026-11-10 — The web Worker's 404 script, a web rollback rehearsal, locked-down Actions, and lexio on the deploy token

The rest of Phase 5 of the build system and CI/CD audit: decisions 8 and 9 (findings DEP6, DEP2), the repository settings from CI8, and the token split carried to lexio. Decision id BLD-P5. Released as `release-20261110-0911` (0a8feadf; API 99fbfdba, web b0ed6d08). That release ran with the read-only token as the ambient one, so only the deploy token wrote.

- **A missing build file is a 404 nothing stores (DEP6, decision 9).** The web Worker has a script now (`src/notFoundWorker.ts`, root wrangler.jsonc `main` plus an `ASSETS` binding). It runs only on an asset miss that is not a page navigation.
  - Under `/assets/` and `/love/` it answers `404`, `cache-control: no-store`, with nosniff and CORP set, because `_headers` does not reach a script's responses.
  - Any other path gets index.html through the binding.
  - It was first deployed to a throwaway `colorless-drill-404` Worker, since deleted. That run showed why the second rule is needed: a script that answered every miss with 404 also refused `/decks/abc` asked for without `Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate` (Safari before 16.4, link previews). Navigations never reach the script.
  - Production after the release: a missing chunk or `/love/` file gives 404 no-store; `/decks/abc`, plain or navigated, gives the page with its `_headers`; existing files keep `immutable`.
  - Left as it is: a navigation typed straight to a missing `/assets/…` URL still gets the platform's SPA fallback (HTML, immutable). Only `run_worker_first` on the asset paths would change that, and it would bill every asset request.
  - 2 mutant rows, both killed: `/love/` misses served as the page, and the 404 made cacheable.
- **Web rollback rehearsed (DEP2, decision 8).** Back one version and forward again, 59 s in all (backend-data.md §3.7.3, "Web Worker rollback rehearsal"). The API Worker's rollback is still not rehearsed.
- **Repository Actions settings (CI8).** `allowed_actions: selected` (GitHub-owned actions plus `oven-sh/setup-bun@*`) and `sha_pinning_required: true`, set through `gh api`. The next CI run (0a8feadf) was green under them. Watch the next weekly Dependabot run, which GitHub runs with its own actions.
- **lexio.** `scripts/deploy.ts` runs every wrangler write (migrations, upload, promote, rollback) with the deploy token, and refuses to run without it outside GitHub Actions, where the environment's secret already is a deploy token. Reads keep the shell's read-only token. Branch `deploy-token` (e4bd4e6), not pushed: the user merges it. `origin/README.md` has a "The token" paragraph.
- **Still open:** revoking or narrowing the old `agents` token (kept file-only as `CLOUDFLARE_AGENTS_TOKEN`; the user's call), and a rehearsal of the API Worker's rollback.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit: the log guard, `logErrorThrottledBy`, and a runbook a player's report can be looked up in

Phase 0 of the observability and monitoring audit (report `tmp/obs/obs-audit.md`, evidence in `tmp/obs/probe/`; 20 findings, 2 medium, 0 high). Decision id OBS-P0. The user took all 8 recommendations of the report's section 6:

1. **1a.** Keep Cloudflare's invocation logs, set `redact_query_string`, and document that they hold the client IP and geolocation for 3 days.
2. **2a.** Enable Workers Issues in config while it is a free beta, and add one Real-Time Issue email notification.
3. **3a.** A liveness probe from GitHub Actions every 6 hours, with no secret.
4. **4a.** The threshold report is run by hand, monthly and after each release.
5. **5a.** Reliability decision 17 stands: nothing is reported off-device, so the client-reporting phase is deferred.
6. **6a.** Match counts live in log lines only, with no Analytics Engine.
7. **7a.** Two light SLOs (99.5% non-5xx, p95 under 500 ms on the core routes) printed by the report.
8. **8a.** Narrow the log-level rule: error is a code defect or an unexpected exception, warn an expected degradation or a refusal.

Phase 0 changes no runtime behaviour; nothing to release.

- **`scripts/log-guard.ts` (LOG4), in `check:static`.** It reads `apps/api/src` apart from tests and `testing/`, and fails on:
  - a `console` call outside `http/log.ts`;
  - a denied field key in a log call's object literals, or in a function that returns `LogFields` (`errorFields`). A key containing `email`, `password`, `token`, `cookie` or `secret` is denied, and so are `ip`, a camelCase `…Ip`, `name`, `displayName`, `deckName`, `deckId`, `userId`, `accountId`, `session` and `sessionId`, without case. `code` is allowed, as the report advised, because lobby lines carry a WebSocket close code under it. `name` is denied against the report's advice: no line uses it, and on a log line it would most likely be a display or deck name;
  - an `evt` and `kind` that backend-data.md §3.7.4 does not name: the backticked words of that `evt`'s query row, and for `lobby` the kind table too. `evt` and `kind` must be string literals, apart from 5 forwarding calls (`bindingRefuses`, and `LobbyDO.log` and `.info`) whose callers are read instead.
  - Today: 68 files, 59 pairs, no console call and no denied key. The runbook lacked 4 kinds, now added: `auth` `logout_failed`, `oauth_unavailable` and `expired_session_delete_failed`, and `lobby` `seat_orphaned`.
  - It reads source text, so a key built in a variable, or a log function imported under another name, goes unseen. `LogFields` and the lobby's LB8 test cover part of that at run time.
  - A phase that adds a kind (Phase 2's `frame_failed`, `match_started`, `match_ended`) adds its runbook row in the same change, or the check fails.
- **`logErrorThrottledBy` in `apps/api/src/http/log.ts`.** It has `logEventThrottledBy`'s signature and per-`by` throttle, at error level, so Phase 2 can move the lobby's code-bug lines to error without touching log.ts. The level rule and `logInfo` are unchanged; Phase 1 owns them.
- **Runbook, backend-data.md §3.7.4 (ERR4, LOG1, LOG2, LOG4).**
  - The Workers Logs paragraph now names both event types: the app's lines (`$metadata.type = "cf-worker"`) and Cloudflare's invocation events (`cf-worker-event`), which share `$workers.requestId`.
  - Info lines arrive with no level, so an info query needs the type filter.
  - The lobby's `describeError` keeps `name: message`, up to 200 characters.
  - A new paragraph says what the logs hold about a player: the app's lines carry no IP, while the invocation event holds the IP, geolocation and the full URL for 3 days (decision 1a). §3.5's account-tag paragraph points there.
  - New: a Workers Issues paragraph (off until Phase 1 enables it), a `$workers.outcome = "exception"` row, and an "A player reports…" table that maps a report to a query.
  - "Saved queries" is now "Log queries", since none are saved in the dashboard.
- **DEP-P3 amended (ERR1).** The source map serves only uncaught exceptions, which here means the cron and the Durable Object. The same claim in the comment above `upload_source_maps` in `apps/api/wrangler.jsonc` is left for Phase 1, which owns that file.
- 7 OBS-P0 mutant rows, all killed. Three are on API source and are killed by running the guard, each for its own reason: an `email` field on `login_ok`, a raw `console.warn` for `login_failed`, and a renamed lobby kind with no runbook row. Three are on the guard and are killed by `scripts/logGuard.test.ts`: shorthand keys unread, the deny-list compared with case, and kinds taken from the whole of backend-data.md. The seventh, `logErrorThrottledBy` logging at warn, is killed by `log.test.ts`.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit Phase 1: an unhandled error names its frames, info lines get a level, Workers Issues and query redaction on

Phase 1 of the observability and monitoring audit (report `tmp/obs/obs-audit.md`, findings ERR1, LOG1, LOG2, LOG3, OPS3; decisions 1a, 2a, 8a). Decision id OBS-P1.

- **Frames on a failure nothing handled (ERR1 step 2).** `apps/api/src/http/errors.ts` has `errorFrames` and `unhandledErrorFields` (`errorFields` plus `frames`).
  - `frames` holds up to 5 of the stack's innermost `at` lines, each cut to 200 characters and joined with newlines.
  - They are read only from below the message. A DrizzleQueryError's message spans lines and carries its params, and a param can hold a line that looks like a frame. A stack that no longer contains the message gives no frames.
  - Only `http unhandled` (onError) and `d1 sweep_failed` carry it. `sweep_failed` used to log the error name alone and now logs the full error fields as well.
  - Warn lines (kv, health, after_response, auth) keep `errorFields` alone. A handled degradation's stack only shows a binding, and leaving them as they were keeps their tests.
  - The report proposed putting `frames` in `errorFields` itself. Under 8a that would have added frames to every warn line.
  - Logged frames come from the minified bundle and the source map does not resolve them. Function names survive minification (keep_names). If a line has to be mapped, rebuild the map from the release tag with `wrangler deploy --minify --dry-run --outdir`.
- **Workers Issues on (ERR1 step 1, decision 2a).** `observability.issues.enabled: true` in `apps/api/wrangler.jsonc`.
  - Verified in three places: the wrangler 4.158 schema (`config-schema.json`, `Observability.issues.enabled`); wrangler's validator, which lists `issues` among the known `observability` keys and checks `enabled` as a boolean; and Cloudflare's configuration and Issues docs ("Requires Wrangler 4.134.0 or later"; "the next deployment turns off Issues" if it is missing from config).
  - The upload sends the `observability` object whole. `wrangler deploy --dry-run` printed no config warning.
- **Source-map comment fixed (ERR1).** The comment above `upload_source_maps` now says the map serves uncaught exceptions only, which here means the cron and the Durable Object. This matches the DEP-P3 amendment in OBS-P0.
- **Query strings redacted (LOG1, decision 1a).** `observability.redact_query_string: true` in `apps/api/wrangler.jsonc`.
  - It appears in the same schema and validator. The live script settings already had a `redact_query_string: false` key.
  - The log.ts and logTag.ts headers now say the no-IP rule covers the app's lines, and that Cloudflare's invocation event holds the client IP and geolocation.
  - Not verified until a release: whether it also strips `$workers.event.search`.
- **Info lines get a level (LOG2).** `logInfo` writes with `console.info` and keeps its signature.
  - In production a `console.log` line arrived with no level at all, while `console.warn` lines carried `source.level` and `$metadata.level`.
  - The report's other option, a `level: "info"` field in the JSON, was not taken. It would add a field to every info line, and how Workers Logs treats a JSON `level` is just as unverified.
  - The console spies on info lines follow: `log.test.ts`, `auth/routes.d1.test.ts`, `auth/sweep.d1.test.ts`, `catalog/routes.d1.test.ts`, and one line each in `lobby/lobbyDO.test.ts` and `lobby/lobbyAbuse.test.ts`. No lobby source was touched; Phase 2 owns the DO's levels.
- **Level rule narrowed (LOG3 log.ts part, decision 8a).**
  - log.ts's header now reads: info for what went as intended; warn for an expected degradation (a spent quota, a limiter or binding failure, a D1 or origin failure the code answers for) or a refusal; error for a code defect or an unexpected exception.
  - "Saved queries" is now "log queries".
  - `auth logout_failed` and `auth oauth_unavailable`, both D1 failures the route answers for, moved from error to warn. The API's error level is now `http unhandled`, `d1 sweep_failed` and the two pepper lines. Phase 2 moves the DO's code-bug lines to error with `logErrorThrottledBy`.
- **OAuth `cause` (ERR5 nit).** `oauth_exchange` and `oauth_profile` with `status: null` carry `cause: "timeout"` when the 10 s deadline aborted the fetch, or `"network"` for any other failure.
- **Web Worker logs (OPS3 config line).** The root `wrangler.jsonc` sets `observability.enabled: true`. Its script runs only on non-navigation asset misses, about 10 events a month. The `--release` report that reads them is Phase 3's.
- **Runbook, backend-data.md §3.7.4.** Changed:
  - the level sentence (8a, `console.info`);
  - `frames` on the `http` and `d1` rows;
  - the redacted query string in the "What the logs hold" paragraph;
  - the Workers Issues paragraph: on, free during the beta, the Real-Time Issue notification set by hand;
  - a new paragraph on the web Worker's logs;
  - the auth row's level moves and `cause`;
  - "Workers Issues first" in the player-report table.
  No evt or kind was added or renamed.
- **Mutants.** 7 OBS-P1 rows from the phase, all killed:
  - `OBS-P1-frames-from-whole-stack`: the frames filter reads the whole stack, so a frame-shaped param leaks.
  - `OBS-P1-frames-unbounded`
  - `OBS-P1-unhandled-without-frames`: onError back on `errorFields`.
  - `OBS-P1-sweep-failed-name-only`
  - `OBS-P1-info-via-console-log`
  - `OBS-P1-logout-failed-at-error`: via `logErrorThrottled`, the pepper lines' function.
  - `OBS-P1-oauth-cause-always-network`

  Two existing rows were re-transcribed onto the lines this change moved, with the same mutation, and both are still killed: `REL-AP3-logout-keeps-cookie` and `API-OPS2-info-is-warn`. API-P5's 17 rows are all still killed.
- **Guarded at merge.** `scripts/worker-bundle.ts --check` now also fails when `apps/api/wrangler.jsonc` drops `observability.enabled`, `redact_query_string` or `issues.enabled` (`observabilityProblems`, which reads Issues' own `enabled` apart from the logs' one). 3 more OBS-P1 rows, all killed: `OBS-P1-redaction-dropped` and `OBS-P1-issues-dropped` by running the check, `OBS-P1-issues-enabled-stands-in` by workerBundle.test.ts.
- **Release, and after it.** The API and the web both need a release. After it:
  - run `bun tmp/obs/probe/logs/levels.ts 24`; the next `d1 sweep` line should show `meta.level=info`. If it has no level, fall back to a `level: "info"` field;
  - run `bun tmp/obs/probe/logs/telemetry.ts invocations 1`; `queryKeys` should be empty;
  - open `colorless-api` → Observability → Issues and check whether a `console.error` line shows there;
  - the user creates one "Real-Time Issue" notification to the account email (decision 2a). The read token cannot create it.
  - Recheck Issues pricing with the 2026-12-01 Workers Logs change, and remove it with one config line if it stops being free.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit Phase 2: `frame_failed` under the lobby code, the DO's code-bug lines at error, and `match_started` / `match_ended`

Phase 2 of the observability and monitoring audit (report `tmp/obs/obs-audit.md`), findings ERR3, LOG3 (the Durable Object part) and MET3, under decisions 6a (match counts in log lines only, no Analytics Engine) and 8a (error is a code defect or an unexpected exception, warn an expected degradation or a refusal). Decision id OBS-P2. It changes the API Worker's Durable Object; it ships with Phase 1's API release.

- **`frame_failed` (ERR3).** `webSocketMessage` now runs the overflow answer, the snapshot read and the dispatch inside one try. A throw logs `lobby` `frame_failed { lobby, frame, error }` and is then rethrown, so the invocation still ends as an exception and Workers Issues sees it as before.
  - `frame` is the frame's kind (`hello`, `intent`, `action`, `reject`, `bye`, `rematch`), and `error` is `describeError`, like every lobby line. The line is error level, throttled per frame kind with `logErrorThrottledBy`, so a client's hello retry does not hide the move that failed first.
  - The dispatch moved into `dispatch()`, with each arm `return await`. The old switch returned its handler's promise unawaited, and a try around it would have caught nothing (mutant row `OBS-P2-frame-dispatch-unawaited`).
  - The line cannot throw over the error it reports. `failureFields` reads the code from the in-memory snapshot first, and when a storage read for it fails too, the code is null. The whole line is also inside its own try. `alarm_failed` uses `failureFields` as well, so a failed alarm pass can no longer throw from its own log line past the `catch` that re-arms it.
  - The SQLITE_TOOBIG snapshot test now asserts the line. A new test makes every storage read fail on a woken instance: the frame's own error is rethrown (`read 1 failed`, not the code read's `read 2`), `lobby` is null, and a second intent is throttled while a hello still logs.
  - No reject is sent to the socket (the report's optional step). A frame can fail after one of its writes landed (an action's match write, then its activity stamp), and a `reject-action` would then spring back a move that was stored. The client resyncs on its next hello once storage recovers.
- **Code-bug lines at error (LOG3, rule 8a).** `action_threw` (still throttled per action `type`, now through `logErrorThrottledBy`), `alarm_failed` (not throttled, as before), and `handoff_failed` when starting the match threw (`match_refused` from the `beginMatch` catch; `failedStart` takes a `threw` flag) now log at error. They call log.ts directly with literal `evt` and `kind`, so the log guard checks them against the runbook with no new forwarders. Expected refusals and degradations stay warn: `handoff_failed` for `d1_error`, `deck_unresolved` and an engine-refused deck, `ws_refused`, `rematch_unchecked`, `hello_refused` and the rest. log.ts and its level comment are unchanged (Phase 1 owns them).
- **`match_started` and `match_ended` (MET3, decision 6a).** Both are info level and carry no name, seat, deck or account.
  - `match_started { lobby }` is logged once the handoff has written the new match. Several under one lobby code are rematches.
  - `match_ended { lobby, cause, turns, durationMs }` is logged when a write takes a game into `gameOver` (it checks the game was not over before). `cause` is the engine's reason for a win (`prizesTaken`, `noPokemon`, `deckOut`, `cardEffect`, `conceded` for the Concede button), `tie`, or the server's forfeit `left` or `abandoned`, which also log `forfeit`. `turns` is `state.turn`, and `durationMs` is the time since `startedAt`.
  - A match that never finishes has no `match_ended`: one retired as unreadable (`match_retired`), wiped with its lobby, or ended by the host's kick. That count is started minus ended.
  - That is 2 events per match (3 with a forfeit). The e2e run's API log under workerd showed `match_started`, `match_ended {cause: "conceded", turns: 0}` and the rematch's `match_started`.
- **Runbook (backend-data.md §3.7.4).** The lobby table's level sentence names the info and error kinds. There are new rows for `match_started`, `match_ended` (with the count query `evt = "lobby" AND kind IN (match_started, match_ended)` grouped by `cause`) and `frame_failed` *t*. `action_threw`, `alarm_failed` and `handoff_failed` say they log at error. The "A match froze or will not start" row now starts from `frame_failed`. The `$workers.outcome = "exception"` row still says an exception names no lobby code. That stays true of the exception event itself, and the row is left for Phase 1's edits of that table.
- **Mutant rows.** 3 existing rows had their `find` re-anchored onto the moved lines, with the same mutations: `MI3-throw-refused-silently`, `API-LOB1-start-throw-escapes`, and `API-LOB7-lobby-per-kind` (which now shares one `by` value across action types). All 3 are still killed, and so are 16 other lobbyDO.ts rows (run with `--only`).
- 11 OBS-P2 mutant rows, all killed by the lobby suites:
  - `frame_failed` itself, 4 rows:
    - dispatch unawaited;
    - error swallowed;
    - code read through the unguarded `fields`;
    - one throttle for every frame kind.
  - the level moves back to warn, 3 rows: `action_threw`, `alarm_failed`, and the handoff throw without its flag;
  - a D1 handoff failure raised to error;
  - no `match_started`;
  - the forfeit cause lost to the engine's `conceded`;
  - no `match_ended` from the server's concede.

  A row that drops the "not already over" check was not written: the engine refuses every action once the game is over, so it would be equivalent.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit Phase 4: a 6-hourly production watch from GitHub Actions, retried, that never fails on a 4xx

Phase 4 of the observability audit (OPS1, with OPS5 merged into it; decisions 3a and 4a). Decision id OBS-P4. No runtime change; nothing to release.

- **`scripts/prod-watch.ts`.** Read-only GETs with no secret, all at once, each with a 10 s timeout:
  - API `/health`: 200 and a `/health` body with a version tag (any tag: `healthTagProblem(body, "*")`);
  - API `/health/db`: 200, `ok: true` and a migration count. A count other than drizzle/'s file count is printed as a note, not a failure: between a push and its release the two differ, and that is not an outage. `release:health` keeps the strict comparison;
  - API `/sets` 200 JSON and `/auth/me` 401 JSON: releaseChecks.ts's `API_PROBES` and `probeProblem`;
  - web `/`: 200, a `<meta name="build">`, an `/assets/` script, and every `/assets/` and `/love/` file the page references answering HEAD 200 with a non-HTML type for a script or a style (`buildMetaOf`, `pageReferences`, `assetProblem`). The build is not compared with HEAD, for the same reason as the migration count.
  - The origins default to `.env.production`'s; `--api` and `--site` override them.
- **Retry and fail rules (OPS5).** A check that is not ok is tried again after 15 s and after 45 s, and its last try decides. The run fails, printing `PROD DOWN — …` and exiting 1, only when a check's last try met a 5xx, no answer, a timeout or a wrong body. A 4xx is never an alert, since rate limiting answers 429: the check is held, printed as a `PROD HELD` warning annotation, and the run passes with a `PROD HELD — …` line. The one exception is a file the page references answering 404: the deployed page names a file its own deployment lacks (the blank page), which is a broken deploy, not a refusal. Otherwise the line is `PROD OK — …`, with each check's time.
- **`.github/workflows/prod-watch.yml`, "Production watch".** `schedule` at minute 41 every 6 hours (off the hour, where GitHub delays and drops scheduled runs) plus `workflow_dispatch`; `contents: read`; `persist-credentials: false`; checkout and setup-bun pinned to the same SHAs as ci.yml; one job, `Probe production`, `timeout-minutes: 5`. A failure is mailed as "Run failed: Production watch", never CI's subject, and its annotation is titled `PROD DOWN`.
  - The job does not use `.github/actions/setup`. The probe imports only Node built-ins, releaseChecks.ts (which imports nothing) and `scripts/lib/repo.ts`, so it needs Bun and no Node, install or cache. Measured: run from a `git archive` tree with no node_modules, it prints `PROD OK`.
  - So `scripts/import-graph.ts` gains one `ALLOWED_CROSSINGS` entry, `scripts/prod-watch.ts -> ../apps/api/src/releaseChecks`: a workspace package does not resolve without an install. A future import of a package would break the workflow (a module error, mailed under the same subject), so keep the probe's imports to these.
- **Cost.** A healthy run is about 20 s, billed 1 minute: 4 × 30.4 ≈ 122 minutes a month of the account's shared 2,000 (October used 1,850). A failing run waits 60 s for its retries, so about 2 billed minutes. Production load: 11 requests a run (4 on the API, the page and its 6 files), 44 a day, more only on retries.
- **Decision 4a.** The threshold report stays by hand; no Cloudflare token or other secret is added to Actions.
- **Verified.** Against production: `PROD OK — /health 0a8feadf 227 ms · /health/db 13 migrations 153 ms · /sets 200 152 ms · /auth/me 401 100 ms · web 0a8feadf + 6 files 196 ms`. Against an unresolvable API host and a local server answering 503: `PROD DOWN`, exit 1, after 60 s of retries. A local server answering 200 with no build meta: `PROD DOWN — web /: no <meta name="build">`. One answering 429 everywhere: `PROD HELD`, exit 0. actionlint 1.7.12 (CI's pinned version and sha256) with shellcheck 0.11.0: 0 errors in 3 files. The `workflow_dispatch` run is the user's, after the push.
- 10 OBS-P4 mutant rows on `scripts/prod-watch.ts`, all killed by `scripts/prodWatch.test.ts`: only 429 held, 5xx held, a held try not retried, two tries instead of three, a migration-count mismatch failing, a missing referenced file held, held read before down in the page check, a held check counted as down, the page's files never checked, and a TimeoutError not named as a timeout.
- **For the user after the push.** Scheduled workflows run from the default branch only, so the watch starts once this is on main. Run it once with "Run workflow" (Actions, Production watch) and check that it is green. GitHub mails a scheduled run's failure to the user who last changed its `cron` line; check that Settings, Notifications, GitHub Actions has email on ("only failures" is enough). GitHub's 60-day inactivity disable of scheduled workflows is a public-repository rule; it would apply if the repo were made public.
- **Runbook and conventions.** backend-data.md §3.7.4's report table has a `Run failed: Production watch` row, and conventions.md's CI paragraph names the workflow and its import rule. `scripts/import-graph.ts` allows the one crossing.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit Phases 1, 2 and 4 merged

Branches `obs-p1`, `obs-p2` and `obs-p4` merged into `obs`. Conflicts: `scripts/mutation/mutants.ts` (append-only, both sides kept), and the console spies of `lobby/lobbyDO.test.ts` and `lobby/lobbyAbuse.test.ts`, where Phase 1 moved the info spy to `console.info` and Phase 2 added an error spy; the merge keeps both. On the merged tree `bun run check` is green (752 files, 17,416 tests, 76/76 Lua), and the OBS-P0, P1, P2 and P4 rows plus the 5 rows the phases re-anchored are all killed.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit Phase 3: `prod:report`: production usage, latency, 5xx, D1, thresholds and SLOs in one read-only command

Phase 3 of the observability audit (MET2 with METX1, MET5, MET6, OPS2, OPS3, OPS4; decisions 4a and 7a). Decision id OBS-P3. It is a dev script, so there is no runtime change and nothing to release.

- **`bun run --cwd apps/api prod:report [--days N] [--release]`** (`apps/api/scripts/prod-report.ts`). Read-only. It uses GraphQL Analytics, the Workers Logs query API (`view: "calculations"`, which saves no query: the account still has 0 saved queries after the runs) and the deployments and versions GETs, all with the ambient `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`. The account is the single one the token lists, or `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`.
  - `--days` takes 1 to 30, default 7. A GraphQL range that starts past the dataset's retention answers no rows at all, with no error, so the cap is 30.
  - Workers Logs sections read the last `min(days, 7)` days.
  - The SLOs always use 7 days. The cron check always reads the last week.
  - It takes about 10 s and makes about 40 reads (about 48 with `--release`), at most 4 Workers Logs reads at a time per section.
- **Sections:**
  - usage against the Free quotas, account-wide, per UTC day: peak day, today and the share of the limit. This covers Workers requests, D1 rows read and written, KV reads, writes, deletes and lists, Durable Object rows read and written, and Workers Logs events (one count per UTC day against 200k);
  - API requests by HTTP status. WebSocket sessions and cron runs (status below 200) are counted apart and left out of every latency figure;
  - invocation outcomes, CPU p50/p99/max against the 10 ms reference, and wall p50/p95/p99;
  - LobbyDO invocations, with its exceededCpu, exceededMemory and fatalInternal counts;
  - 5xx by day, status and version;
  - latency by route over Workers Logs: requests and wall and CPU p50/p95/p99. Routes are the Worker's own Hono table, imported from `src/index.ts`, so a printed pattern is one the code declares and no route list is kept by hand. A trigger is matched to the pattern with the most literal segments. Each route's query is its regex less the literal routes that regex also matches (`GET /cards/:id` excludes `GET /cards/index`). A trigger no route matches is only counted;
  - the top 5 D1 queries on `colorless` by rows read and by total duration, as shapes with string, blob, number and non-identifier double-quoted literals replaced by `?`, cut to 90 characters;
  - counts of the watched log lines (`http unhandled`, `http invalid_request`, `lobby match_retired`, `handoff_failed`, `action_threw`) and `quota: true` lines, plus the daily cron;
  - the two SLO lines and the match completion share;
  - with `--release`, the release table;
  - the thresholds.
- **Thresholds.** Exit 1 on any crit, 2 when it cannot run, else 0. All quotas warn at 50% and go crit at 80% of the limit:

  | Signal | Warn | Crit |
  |---|---|---|
  | Workers requests / day (account) | 50k | 80k |
  | D1 rows read / day | 2.5M | 4M |
  | D1 rows written / day | 50k | 80k |
  | KV reads / day | 50k | 80k |
  | KV writes, deletes, lists / day (each) | 500 | 800 |
  | DO rows read / day | 2.5M | |
  | DO rows written / day | 50k | |
  | Workers Logs events / day | 100k (50% of 200k) | |
  | Daily cron | a failed run in the last week | no successful run yesterday |
  | `match_retired`, `handoff_failed`, `quota: true` lines | any | |
  | Worker `exceeded*` outcomes, DO exceeded/fatal counts | any | |
  | SLO missed | yes | |
  | A source unreadable, a core path no longer routed, 1,000 triggers (grouping capped) | yes | |
  | `--release` findings | yes | |

  OPS2's table plus KV reads, deletes and lists and DO rows read, which share the same Free limits. The Workers Logs warn counts events. The 2026-12-01 Free cap is 0.5 GB a day, and counts cannot measure bytes: at the 5 KB median event, 0.5 GB is about 100k events, which is the same warn. When nothing trips, the section prints `all clear: N signals ok`.
- **SLOs (decision 7a).**
  - Availability: over 7 days of GraphQL, 99.5% of API answers (status 200 and above) are not 5xx. The line prints the budget, `floor(requests × 0.5%)`.
  - Latency: over 7 days of Workers Logs, the p95 wall time of `/decks`, `/decks/:id`, `/auth/me`, `/sets` and `/cards/index` (any method) is under 500 ms.
  - A miss is a warn.
  - Match completion: from `evt = "lobby"` `match_started` and `match_ended` lines grouped by `cause`, with Phase 2's vocabulary (obs-p2 6a8de9a9). The causes are `prizesTaken`, `noPokemon`, `deckOut`, `cardEffect`, `conceded`, `tie`, `left` and `abandoned`.
    - Every ending but `abandoned` counts as completed: a player who never came back is how a desync or a client crash shows from the lobby.
    - Any other cause is printed as `other`, never by name.
    - A start with no end is printed as "not ended" (still playing, retired, wiped or kicked).
    - It has no target, and prints `no match lines yet` until Phase 2 is released.
- **`--release` (OPS3).**
  - The newest API deployment (the version taking the most traffic) is compared with the version before it. The new version is read since its deploy, and the old one over the same span before the deploy, capped at 7 days. Both come from Workers Logs, by `$workers.scriptVersion.id`.
  - Per version it prints requests, 5xx with their rate, wall p95 and the watched lines. The commit tag comes from the version's details; only a hex tag is taken, because those details also carry the uploader's email.
  - Any new-version 5xx or watched line is a warn. So is a p95 over 2× the old one when both versions served at least 20 requests; under 20, only presence is judged. A finding never blocks.
- **Privacy.**
  - Only counts, dates, route patterns, statuses, Cloudflare's outcome names, version ids, the hex tag and literal-free SQL shapes are printed. Raw triggers and log fields are never printed.
  - Before printing, `leaksIn` checks the whole text for an email, an IPv4 or an IPv6 address (compressed forms too). On a match it prints nothing and exits 2.
  - The saved real run was also grepped for those patterns: no match.
- **Pure parts in `apps/api/src/prodReport.ts`**, with `prodReport.test.ts` (33 tests). They cover threshold levels, the exit code, quota aggregation from GraphQL rows, UTC day slices, route patterns and trigger matching, the route filters, the core-route regex, availability and latency math, match lines, the cron signal, release signals, the deployments pair, the tag, Workers Logs result parsing, the SQL shape, tables and the leak guard.
  - The pure module sits in the api's `src`, beside `releaseChecks.ts`, because vitest does not collect `apps/api/scripts`. The Worker does not import it.
  - No registration was needed: `check:static` passes as is (typecheck coverage, lint coverage, the import graph, the worker bundle).
- **Wiring.**
  - `apps/api/package.json` gets a `prod:report` script.
  - `release:finish` prints one line after the tag: after about an hour of use, run `bun run --cwd apps/api prod:report --release`.
  - The monthly routine in conventions.md (Dependencies) gets step 6, `prod:report --days 30`; the old step 6 is now 7.
- **Real runs, 2026-11-10** (`tmp/obs/drafts/p3-report-run.txt` is the `--release` run).
  - 7 days, every quota ok. Peaks: D1 rows read 46,874 (0.9%), Workers requests 157, KV writes 5, Logs events 97 a day. 87 API answers and 7 WebSocket/cron, CPU p99 54 ms, 0 5xx, cron ran on 11-09.
  - Availability SLO 100% (met). Latency SLO p95 1,102 ms over 27 core-route requests: **missed**. With 27 requests, the 2 card-index KV misses (GET 1,545 ms, HEAD 1,102 ms) are the p95.
  - Release: 99fbfdba (0a8feadf, 3.4 h) has 28 requests, 0 5xx and p95 117 ms; 907516db has 8, 0 and 251 ms. Exit 0 with one warn (latency SLO).
  - 30 days: D1 read peak 2,435,769 on 10-23 (48.7%), D1 written 30,607 on 10-19, and KV writes 550 on 10-23, which is a warn. These are the audit's pre-CAT1 peaks. The one 5xx burst is 10-30, 502 ×5 on b118e511, which is METX1. Exit 0.
- **Not built:** MET2's per-version CPU and wall table for the last 3 releases (`--release` compares the newest two); OPS4's optional daily line in `tmp/slo.log`; OPS3's `hours=24` argument, since the window is "since the deploy"; and OPS3's web Worker `/assets/` 404 count, which needs Phase 1's web `observability` line.
- **For the user.** At about 30 core-route requests a week, the latency SLO is decided by one or two cold `/cards/index` builds. It will read MISSED most weeks while the target is right about steady state. Options: (a) keep it, accepting that the miss means "the index was cold"; (b) take `/cards/index` out of the core routes; (c) judge p95 only from 100 requests up. Recommendation: (a) for now, since it is a warn and never fails the run, and revisit with the first month of reports.
- 13 OBS-P3 mutant rows on `apps/api/src/prodReport.ts`, all killed by `prodReport.test.ts` (`bun run mutants --decision OBS-P3`: 13 killed, 0 gaps, 11.1 s):

  - a quota exactly at its crit rated warn;
  - any warn exiting 1;
  - an unread quota rated ok;
  - Logs days cut 24 h from the start instead of at midnight;
  - the first matching route winning;
  - the shadowed-literal check reversed;
  - 500 not counted as an error;
  - yesterday's failed cron only a warn;
  - the p95 ratio on an old version with few requests;
  - SQL string literals kept;
  - compressed IPv6 not caught;
  - an abandoned seat counted as completed.

### 2026-11-10 — Observability audit released

`main` b00fa6d3 released as `release-20261110-1331`: API da6bfe40 (from 99fbfdba), web 8e86d8ea. Checked in production right after:

- The live script settings show `redact_query_string: true` and `issues.enabled: true` on `colorless-api`, and observability on for `colorless`.
- On da6bfe40, 0 of 10 invocation events kept a query string, two `?obsprobe=…` requests included; the version before kept them.
- `bun scripts/prod-watch.ts` printed `PROD OK` on b00fa6d3.
- `prod:report --release` exited 0. Its one warn is the latency SLO: p95 1,111 ms over 39 core-route requests, set by cold `/cards/index` builds. That stays a warn, to be revisited after a month of reports.
- **Still open:**
  - whether a `console.info` line now carries a level (the next `d1 sweep` line, or a sign-in; `bun tmp/obs/probe/logs/levels.ts 24`);
  - the user's Real-Time Issue notification;
  - the first `workflow_dispatch` run of Production watch and GitHub's Actions failure email.

### 2026-11-11 — No email alert for a new Workers issue (amends decision 2a)
- Decision 2a planned one "Real-Time Issue" email notification. The account has none to create: Alerts → Platform Notifications → Availability & performance lists no Workers type, and a search for "worker" finds nothing. Issues → Automations sends only to a coding agent, a webhook, a chat or an incident tool, not to email.
- So nothing emails a new issue. The Issues page is read by hand after each release and when a player reports a problem (runbook §3.7.4). An outage still emails, through the Production watch's failed run.
- Revisit if Cloudflare adds a Workers issue alert type, or with the 2026-12-01 Workers Logs pricing change.

### 2026-11-11 — Documentation audit decisions and Phase 0: wrong facts fixed, and production writes only through the deploy token (DOC-P0)
- **The audit.** A read-only audit of the docs against the user's 15-point checklist (main 7a3a2f2f2; the report, `tmp/docs/docs-audit.md`, is untracked). 40 findings: 0 high, 15 medium, 14 low, 11 info, in five areas: entry and onboarding, architecture and limitations, reference, operations, staleness. The mechanical health is good: every `bun run` name in the live docs exists and the entry docs have no dead paths. The problems are semantic:
  - values copied into prose that the code has since changed (README's KV TTL, status.md's deployed release);
  - copy-paste production writes that the read-only ambient token refuses;
  - history presented as current (conventions.md's lessons, decisions.md's numbered rows, React-era workstreams);
  - comments that cite ids and history against the minimal-comments rule.
- **The user's decisions (all recommendations accepted).**
  1. (a) No CHANGELOG: release tags and `git log` between them, as build audit decision 7 chose. No tag-annotation notes.
  2. (a) A pointer-only root CLAUDE.md.
  3. (a) conventions.md's finished-program lessons move verbatim to `docs/archive/conventions-lessons.md`, with a short tool-gotchas list kept.
  4. (a) The numbered decision rows D101-D647 move to `docs/archive/decisions-numbered.md`, and the index reads both files.
  5. (a) The minimal-comments rule stays as written: ids and history phrases are swept (reasons kept), and a grep guard joins `check:static`.
  6. (a) deck-builder.md and polish.md move to `docs/archive/workstreams/`.
  7. (a) Open items a session could act on are listed in status.md, and the auto-memory points there.
  8. The Lua/love.js frontend, in the user's words: "because it's easier to replicate Balatro's visual and feel". Phase 5 writes it up with the accepted costs.
  9. (a) README describes the Balatro source tree without naming its private repository.
  10. (a) The only new docs guard is the bare production-write check.
- **The plan.** Phase 0 runs alone and merges first; Phases 1-6 run as parallel worktrees on its tip, each owning disjoint files.
  - Phase 0: wrong facts on the paths people follow, and the bare-write guard (this entry).
  - Phase 1: entry: README getting started, status.md open items as pointers, one doc map, CLAUDE.md, the Lua README file map.
  - Phase 2: backend-data.md: one "Releasing" checklist replacing the D203 runbook, the lobby and match lifecycle, the §5.3 and §6 gaps.
  - Phase 3: conventions.md and its archive.
  - Phase 4: workstreams and reference: simulator.md as built, the React-era docs archived, the backlogs marked finished.
  - Phase 5: the decision log: the archive split, forward pointers on superseded entries, the "how to add" rule, the frontend's why.
  - Phase 6: the comment sweep and its guard.
- **Phase 0, what changed.**
  - **Production writes (OPS1).** Every write in README and the workstream docs runs through `bun scripts/with-deploy-token.ts` from `apps/api/` (or `bun apps/api/scripts/with-deploy-token.ts` from the root, for the web Worker's rollback). That covers the four secret puts, the rollbacks, the Time Travel restores, the catalog epoch KV puts, the export rebuild loop and every remote ingest; no repo-root form is given for an API secret. backend-data.md §3.7.2's wrapped-writes list adds the remote KV put. README's Deployment opens with a "Cloudflare access" paragraph. The six hints the scripts print on a failure give the wrapped form: release-gate.ts (secrets, `health`, `web-health`), d1.ts (the restore printed with every bookmark), ingest.ts (the epoch, wrapped only for `--remote`) and prod-report.ts.
  - **The guard (decision 10).** `scripts/docs-writes.ts` in `check:static`, with `scripts/docsWrites.test.ts` on fixture strings: conventions.md forbids a test that reads doc text, so it is a script like `log-guard.ts`. It applies the audit probe's write pattern to README.md, `docs/workstreams/*.md` and `apps/api/scripts/*.ts`. A line passes when it names the wrapper, the token loader, the drill database or `--dry-run`. In Markdown, an inline code span wrapped over two lines is read as one line; that is how it found deck-builder.md's wrapped ingest, which the probe missed. A bare `wrangler secret put` planted at the end of README made it fail with the line, and it passed again once the plant was removed.
  - **README (OLD2).** KV 1 day (of `/cards`, pages 1-3), retired by the catalog epoch; no `/folders`; the web Worker's 404 script; `src/host`, `src/seam` and `notFoundWorker.ts` in the tree; `bun run e2e` and a pointer to the other scripts; Lua 5.1 only for `check:lua`. The `web-health` explanation (backend-data.md §3.7.2) and `assetProblem`'s message name the host page rather than the SPA fallback.
  - **status.md (ENT3 a, c, d).** The deployed line names the command that tells the truth (the newest `release-*` tag, the commits after it, `/health` and `<meta name="build">`) instead of a release value. The `print_key` step says the gate refuses a difference. The `src/` row and docs/README.md's code-paths note list `host` and `seam`.
  - **Online limits (DES3).** frontend/lua/README.md's Online section says the HUD reads the frame's allowances, coin flips come from the log's cues and prompts carry their titles; only stack bottoms are still inferred client-side. online.md's old "not on the wire" bullet is marked superseded. The cookie "known limit" in the 2026-10-22 entry was already corrected by 2026-10-27; the forward pointers are Phase 5's.
  - **Catalog scope (DESX1).** The locked assumption "Ingest filters to SV-era" is amended in place. backend-data.md §3.4.1 gives the full catalog: `--serie sv`, `--serie me` and `--sets swsh10.5`.
  - **Small fixes.**
    - ptcg-rules.md: seven internal § references corrected (DES9).
    - Two comments fixed (REF6): `listDecks` returns the card list of a complete deck; the lobby routes' header names `src/online/session.ts` and says an account binds the seat it first claims.
    - OLD9: conventions.md defers the `check:static` step list to package.json; the runbook's lobby address is `/online/<CODE>`; §3.7.2 lists the `bun install --frozen-lockfile` step; the Balatro source defaults to `../balatro-source`.
    - The D369 note's path is `docs/archive/progress.md` (DES4). release-gate.ts's header lists CI's jobs as static, test, mutants (OPS2).
- **Mutants.** 5 DOC-P0 rows, all killed (`bun run mutants --decision DOC-P0`):
  - the secrets hint and the restore hint printed bare again, killed by the guard itself;
  - the guard wanting `--remote` right after `ingest.ts`, reading Markdown without joining wrapped spans, and ignoring fences, killed by `docsWrites.test.ts`.
